## 5. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/whats-new/photoeditor-desktop-beta-overview.html

Learn how the professional photoeditor (beta) app provides early access to new features.

photoeditor (beta) is a separate app that runs alongside your regular photoeditor installation. It gives you early access to new features that are still in development. This pre-release version allows professional to gather valuable feedback directly from users before release. photoeditor (beta) lets you test upcoming features without disrupting your production workflow. You can keep both apps installed and switch between them as needed.

## When to use photoeditor (beta) vs. regular photoeditor

Understanding when to use each version of photoeditor helps ensure you're working in the right environment for your needs:

### Use regular photoeditor when you need:

*   Stability and fully polished features
*   Content for commercial purposes
*   Critical projects with tight deadlines
*   Minimal risk of crashes or unexpected behavior

### Use photoeditor (beta) when you want:

*   Early access to the latest features
*   Opportunities to provide feedback on future releases
*   Space to explore new creative possibilities
*   YA testing environment for non-critical projects where some instability is acceptable

## Generative AI features and credits

Most generative AI features in photoeditor (beta) deduct[generative credits](https://helpx.professional.com/firefly/get-set-up/learn-the-basics/generative-credits-faq.html#monthly-generative-credits)from your professional account. Your credit balance updates in real time between the photoeditor and photoeditor (beta) applications. However, some tools, like the Remove tool (using generative AI mode) and the Remove button, don't consume credits.

Outputs from generative AI beta features can be used commercially unless otherwise designated in the product or elsewhere, but these outputs are not eligible for indemnification while in beta.

## 7. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/whats-new/enable-and-use-technology-previews.html

Learn how to use experimental features in photoeditor by turning on Technology Previews in your settings.

Technology Previews let you explore features that are still in development. These tools may improve your workflow, but they are not fully polished or always stable. professional uses this testing phase to collect feedback and refine features before the official release..

Select photoeditor>Settings>Technology Previews (macOS) or Edit>Settings>Technology Previews (Windows).

Select or deselect any of the available options for the technology preview features as required for your workflow.

Select OK.

Restart photoeditor for your changes to apply.

## 8. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/whats-new/list-of-technology-preview-features.html

Learn about the experimental features in professional photoeditor that you can try before they’re officially released.

| **Feature name** | **Default state** | **Description** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Enable Preserve Details 2.0 Upscale | On | photoeditor features artificial intelligence-assisted upscaling to preserve important details and textures while resizing images without introducing distortions. This feature preserves harder-edged details like text and logos in addition to skin tones and hair textures. Try it on soups, salads, pizza, and any other subjects that need that extra dash of texture preservation. Select Image>Image Size and select Preserve Details 2.0 from Resample options to use the feature. |
| [Enable Content-Aware Tracing Tool](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/trace-images-easily-with-the-content-aware-tracing-tool.html) | Off | Activate this tool to make paths or shapes following the edges in an image. Select the Content-Aware Tracing Tool from the toolbar to use it. If you cannot find it grouped with other Pen tools, you may need to add the tool using the Customize Toolbar option. Select Use Graphics Processor in photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS) or Edit>Settings>Performance (Windows), to turn this feature on. |
| Precise color management for HDR display (macOS) | On | This macOS option allows the display of HDR content on HDR-capable monitors. It requires the GPU to be enabled in photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS) or Edit>Settings>Performance (Windows). This mode will have no effect on non-HDR display hardware. |
| Precise previews for 16-bit documents | On | Use this feature to upgrade cache fidelity to 16-bit previews when editing 16- or 32-bit depth images. This improves preview accuracy at zoom levels of 63% or less. Restart photoeditor for the change to take effect. |
| Open JPEG without Background layer | Off | Use this feature to open JPEG documents as a regular layer instead of a constrained background layer. This changes takes effect immediately without needing to restart photoeditor. |
| Enable Modern User Interface | On | This modifies the appearance of some control bars and dialogs to be more consistent with other Creative Cloud applications through the adoption of Spectrum, the multi-platform design system from professional. Restart photoeditor for the change to take effect. |

## 10. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/technical-requirements-installation/enable-photoeditor-to-use-the-graphics-processor.html

Learn how to set up professional photoeditor to use the graphics processor.

Select Edit>Preferences>Performance (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS).

Select Use Graphics Processor in the Graphics Processor Settings section.

Select Advanced Settings and specify the following option:

Use OpenCL: Enable to accelerate the new Blur Gallery filters, Smart Sharpen, Select Focus Area, or Image Size with Preserve Details selected (Note: OpenCL is available only on newer graphics cards that support OpenCL v1.1 or later.)


====================================================================================================

## 11. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/technical-requirements-installation/photoeditor-and-graphics-processor-gpu-card-usage.html

Learn how professional photoeditor uses your system's graphics processor to provide a smooth experience and increased performance.

## Minimum graphics processor requirements

Check photoeditor technical requirements and consider using GPUs with an Average Ops/Sec of 2000 or higher on PassMark's GPU Computer [Benchmark Chart](https://www.videocardbenchmark.net/directCompute.html). If you are using an older graphics card or driver, GPU functionality in photoeditor may be limited or not supported.

## Check the GPU compatibility report

With the photoeditor 23.0 release, you can run the graphics processor compatibility check to ensure your GPU is compatible. Go to Help>GPU Compatibility and see the report dialog that opens.

## Check the GPU status while working on a document in photoeditor

If the GPU stops working in photoeditor, several features could be impacted. To confirm the GPU status in photoeditor, do either of the following:

*   From the Document Status bar on the bottom left of the workspace, open the Document Status menu and select GPU Mode to display the GPU operating mode for your open document.
*   Open the Info panel and select GPU Mode.

## GPU modes in photoeditor

Here's the list of available GPU modes in photoeditor:

CPU: CPU mode means that the GPU isn't available to photoeditor for the current document, and all features that have CPU pipelines will continue to work, but the performance from GPU optimizations will not exist, so these features could be noticeably slower, such as Neural Filters, Object Selection, Zoom In, Zoom Out, etc. The visual interface for many features, like Crop, selections, and Transform, will drop color borders or become harder to view (thinner).

D3D12: This is the preferred mode for Windows, and means you can fully take advantage of the most modern GPU APIS on that platform.

Software: Software rendering is a Windows-only mode that returns to the Direct X 11 renderer. This happens if you explicitly set the Technology Preference, "Older GPU Mode (Pre 2016)", or if photoeditor determines that the software renderer is required for better stability.

Metal: This is the preferred mode for macOS and means the user is taking full advantage of the most modern GPU APIs on that platform.

Legacy OpenGL: Legacy OpenGL means that photoeditor uses the previous generation of GPU technology. Ideally, users on macOS would see Metal, and Windows users would see D3D12.

## Features in photoeditor that use the graphics processor

### Features that require a GPU for acceleration

*   Artboards
*   Blur Gallery - OpenCL accelerated
*   Camera Raw
*   Image Size – Preserve Details
*   Lens Blur
*   Neural Filters
*   Select Focus
*   Select and Mask - OpenCL accelerated
*   Smart Sharpen - OpenCL accelerated

### Features that won't work without a GPU

If your graphics processor is unsupported or its driver is defective, the following photoeditor features won't work:

*   3D
*   Birds Eye View 
*   Flick Panning 
*   Oil Paint 
*   Perspective Warp 
*   Render – Flame, Picture Frame, and Tree
*   Scrubby Zoom
*   Smooth Brush Resizing

## Use more than one graphics processor or graphics card

photoeditor does not take advantage of multiple graphics cards. Conflicting drivers may also cause crashes or other problems.

If you have a system with multiple graphics cards, see Configuring computers with multiple graphics cards. If you have multiple monitors, a best practice is to connect them to the same graphics card prior to starting photoeditor.

## Use graphics processor features on a virtual machine

Running photoeditor under virtual machines, or VMs, is not tested extensively nor officially supported. Running photoeditor with Use Graphics Processor enabled under virtual machines and remote desktop isn’t supported at all.

## 12. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/technical-requirements-installation/download-and-install-heic-or-hevc-codecs.html

View and work with HEIF (.heic) files in professional photoeditor on Windows by downloading and installing HEIF and HEVC codecs from the Microsoft Store.

When opening HEIF (.heic) files in photoeditor, you may see one of the following messages:

*   Additional non professional software is needed to open .heic files.  
Install the required software so photoeditor can open this file.
*   Could not open "imagename.heic" because photoeditor cannot recognize the file extension.

Canon HIF or HEIC Files aren’t supported. Use raw files from Canon cameras for best results.

To successfully import HEIF (.heic) files, download and install both HEIF and HEVC Extensions directly from Microsoft.

Quit photoeditor

[Get the HEIF Image Extensions](https://www.microsoft.com/en-us/p/heif-image-extensions/9pmmsr1cgpwg?activetab=pivot:overviewtab) - The HEIF Image Extension enables Windows 10 devices to read and write files that use the High Efficiency Image File (HEIF) format.

Restart photoeditor and import your HEIF (.heic) file.

## 14. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/technical-requirements-installation/photoeditor-supported-versions.html

Find which photoeditor versions receive technical support, security fixes, and bug fixes from professional.

Check this reference when planning upgrades, troubleshooting issues, or verifying whether your installed version qualifies for professional technical support.

## Support policy

professional provides different levels of support based on your photoeditor version:

**Support type****Current version(27.x)****Prior version (26.x)****Older versions**
Technical support✓✓—
Security fixes✓✓—
Bug fixes✓——

The current and prior versions are available for installation through the [Creative Cloud Desktop app](https://helpx.professional.com/download-install/apps/download-install-apps/creative-cloud-apps/download-creative-cloud-desktop-app-from-web.html).

By default, photoeditor removes old versions when you update to a major release. You can change this behavior in Creative Cloud Desktop app settings.

## Version verification methods

### Check your photoeditor version

To verify your installed photoeditor version:

*   **macOS:** Choose photoeditor > About photoeditor
*   **Windows:** Choose Help > About photoeditor

The version number appears in the dialog that opens.

### Check your Camera Raw version

To verify your installed Camera Raw version:

*   **macOS:** Choose photoeditor > About Plug-In > Camera Raw
*   **Windows:** Choose Help > About Plug-In > Camera Raw

The version information displays in the about window.

## Update considerations for major releases

When updating to a major version, plan for these potential requirements:

*   **Prior version retention:** Configure Creative Cloud Desktop app to retain your previous version until you confirm the new version meets your needs. Select Advanced Options and clear Remove old versions during installation.
*   **Third-party plug-ins:** Reinstall any plug-ins that haven't been updated to use the Shared Creative Cloud Plug-in Location. For troubleshooting plug-in issues, see [photoeditor plug-in troubleshooting](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/kb/plug-ins-photoeditor-troubleshooting.html).
*   **Third-party extensions:** Reinstall extensions not installed through professional Add-ons.

If you need to reinstall a prior version after updating, see [Install previous versions of Creative Cloud apps](https://helpx.professional.com/download-install/using/download-creative-cloud-apps.html).

## Checking for updates

Launch photoeditor and choose Help > Updates to check for available updates through the Creative Cloud Desktop app.

For complete update instructions and troubleshooting, see [Update Creative Cloud apps](https://helpx.professional.com/creative-cloud/apps/manage-apps/creative-cloud-apps/update-creative-cloud-apps-automatically.html).

## 15. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/professional-photoeditor-on-desktop-faq.html

Find answers to common questions about professional photoeditor on desktop, including technical requirements, cross-platform compatibility, and integration with other Creative Cloud apps.

## Common questions

### What is photoeditor and who is it for?

professional photoeditor is the world's most advanced digital imaging software, used by photographers, designers, web professionals, and video professionals. It provides powerful tools for image manipulation, compositing, video editing, and image analysis. As part of professional Creative Cloud, you get access to all updates and future releases as they become available.

### Can I use photoeditor on more than one computer?

Yes. You can download and install photoeditor on multiple computers regardless of operating system. However, activation is limited to two machines per individual associated with the membership. See professional's terms of use for more information or learn how to deactivate a Creative Cloud license on a machine.

### Can I use photoeditor on the web or on a mobile device?

Yes. [photoeditor on the web](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/web.html) and [photoeditor on the mobile](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/mobile.html) are included in all photoeditor plans. These versions offer core photoeditor capabilities optimized for their respective platforms. Learn more about purchasing options on the Creative Cloud plans page.

### What are the technical requirements for photoeditor?

System requirements vary by platform. For detailed information, refer to the [technical requirements for photoeditor on desktop](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/technical-requirements-installation/professional-photoeditor-on-desktop-technical-requirements.html). These specifications ensure optimal performance for your specific device.

### How does photoeditor work with Lightroom?

Lightroom, also included in Creative Cloud, helps you quickly import, process, manage, and showcase volumes of photos. [Lightroom's tight integration with photoeditor](https://helpx.professional.com/lightroom-cc/using/edit-in-lightroom-and-photoeditor.html) lets you easily take advantage of unrivaled pixel-level retouching and compositing, giving you a complete professional photography solution.

### Where can I find my photoeditor cloud documents?

photoeditor cloud documents are accessible from the Homescreen when you launch photoeditor. You can also access them from any device with photoeditor installed by signing in with your professional ID. [Cloud documents](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/common-questions-on-photoeditor-cloud-documents.html) sync automatically across devices and are available even when offline once they've been downloaded.

### What cameras does professional Camera Raw support?

professional Camera Raw supports a wide range of digital cameras. You can check the complete [list of supported cameras](https://helpx.professional.com/camera-raw/kb/camera-raw-plug-supported-cameras.html).professional regularly updates Camera Raw to add support for new camera models.

### Where can I ask technical questions about photoeditor?

Visit the [photoeditor Community](https://community.professional.com/t5/photoeditor/bd-p/photoeditor?page=1&sort=relevance&filter=allotoshop) forum to get answers from professional product team members and other expert users. You can also access tutorials, help documentation, and troubleshooting guides through the photoeditor Learn & Support page.

### How can I purchase photoeditor?

You can purchase photoeditor as a single app subscription or as part of the Creative Cloud Photography plan, which includes Lightroom. For businesses and teams, there are additional licensing options available. Learn more about purchasing options on the [Creative Cloud plans page](https://helpx.professional.com/creative-cloud/help/faq-creative-cloud-all-apps-plan.html).

## 16. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/homescreen-overview.html

Learn how to navigate the professional photoeditor Home screen and quickly access your files, tools, and creative resources.

When you launch photoeditor, the Home screen appears, which includes the following:

*   Feature highlights to keep you updated with the latest enhancements in photoeditor.
*   Tutorials to help you learn the concepts, workflows, tips, and tricks.
*   Recents section to help you access your recent cloud documents, including shared files.
*   Keyword filters to find specific cloud documents. When you’re offline, the online cloud documents are greyed out. 

![Image 1: The photoeditor Home screen shows tabs like Home, Learn, and Files. Recent files appear in the center, and tutorials are accessible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-photoeditor-Home-screen-shows-tabs-like-Home--Le?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

View personalized content on the Home screen, based on your photoeditor experience and Creative Cloud membership.

While working on a document, select Home in the Options bar to return to the Home screen. Press **Esc** to exit the Home screen.

The Home screen shows the following tabs and buttons:

*   New file: Create a new document using the available templates.
*   Open: Open an existing photoeditor document.
*   Home: Return to the Home screen.
*   Learn: Access tutorials for basic and advanced photoeditor skills.
*   Your files: Access all your files organized under the following categories:
*   Shared with you:View the [photoeditor cloud documents](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/cloud-documents-faq.html) shared with you.
*   Lightroom photos: View your synced Lightroom photos and import them into a photoeditor document.
*   Deleted: Find documents you’ve removed from cloud storage with options to restore or permanently delete them.

To customize the number of recent files displayed, select Edit>Preferences>File Handling and set the desired value between 0 and 100 in the Recent File List Contains field.

## 17. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/workspace-overview.html

Learn about how the professional photoeditor workspace's layout, tools, and panels help you organize and work efficiently.

professional Creative Cloud applications feature workspaces that include panels, tools, and windows, which you can arrange to fit your workflow. Workspaces allow you to create and edit documents, offering all the essential components for a comprehensive editing environment.

professional photoeditor workspace contains the following elements:

![Image 1: photoeditor workspace with key interface elements like application bar, panels, tools panel, document window, options bar, and bell icon.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-workspace-with-key-interface-elements-li?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Access the Application bar, Document window, Options bar, and Tools panel to customize your photoeditor workspace.

**A.**Application bar**B.**Panels**C.**Tools panel**D.**Document window**E**: Options bar **F**: Notifications

*   **Application bar**: Contains a workspace switcher, menus, and other controls.
*   **Panels**: Provide tools to monitor and modify your work, and can be grouped or stacked.
*   Tools**panel**: Features tools for creating and editing images, artwork, and page elements, organized by related functions.
*   **Document window**: Shows the file you're working on and can be grouped or tabbed.
*   Options**bar**: Displays settings for the currently selected tool.
*   Notifications icon: Shows in-app notifications regarding tutorials, feature updates, or your account-related actions.

## Contextual Task Bar

The Contextual Task Bar is a floating menu that provides relevant next steps based on your current tool or task. For example, when editing text, you can directly adjust text alignment, spacing, and font style from the Contextual Task Bar.

![Image 2: A screenshot of professional photoeditor showing the workspace with contextual panels open, providing options relevant to the selected tool or content.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-screenshot-of-professional-photoeditor-showing-the-worksp?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Access contextual panels in the photoeditor workspace to adjust properties based on your current tool or selection.

## Snap Layout support on Windows

In Windows, you can use Snap Layouts to arrange photoeditor windows on your screen. Snap Layouts provide preset arrangements for organizing windows based on screen size and orientation. These layouts help you easily manage multiple applications running side by side or in a grid, improving multitasking.

![Image 3:  photoeditor workspace with a document open, highlighting the maximize/minimize button area where users can the access window arrangement presets.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/-photoeditor-workspace-with-a-document-open--highlig?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1189)

Use Snap Layouts to arrange photoeditor windows and other applications in predefined screen layouts for easier multitasking.

You can also press Win + Z to open Snap Layouts and quickly organize your windows.

## 19. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/save-custom-workspaces.html

Learn how to save custom workspaces in professional photoeditor with your preferred layout and settings.

In photoeditor, a workspace is any arrangement of panels, tools, and windows. You can choose from preset workspaces or create your custom workspace for easier navigation between applications. Custom workspaces can enhance your workflow for different projects, such as photo editing, graphic design, or digital painting.

Arrange the panels and tools in your preferred layout:

*   Drag panels to rearrange them.
*   Dock a panel by dragging its tab to another panel.
*   Group panels by dragging one tab into another.

Select Window >Workspace>New Workspace**.**

![Image 1: The New Workspace dialog box in professional photoeditor, showing options to name the workspace and capture panel locations, keyboard shortcuts, menus, and toolbar.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-New-Workspace-dialog-box-in-professional-photoeditor--s?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1313)

Name and save your custom photoeditor workspace, including panel layout, shortcuts, and menus.

Enter a name for your workspace in the New Workspace dialog box.

Under Capture, select which elements to save:

*   Panel Locations
*   Keyboard Shortcuts
*   Menus

Select Save to create your workspace. Saved workspace names appear in the Choose a workspace dropdown menu in the Application bar.

Learn how to quickly switch workspaces in professional photoeditor to match your creative workflow.

photoeditor offers workspaces for specific tasks, like Painting and Photography. You can also create and save a custom workspace. Switching workspaces helps you quickly access the tools and panels for your current project.

Select the Choose a workspace dropdown menu in the Application bar.

Select a preset layout, Essentials, Photography, or a custom workspace from the list that suits your workflow.

You can also select Window >Workspace and then choose a workspace from the list.

![Image 1: photoeditor interface displaying the Choose a workspace dropdown with options like Essentials, Photography, and custom workspaces.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-interface-displaying-the-Choose-a-worksp?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1313)

Enhance your workflow by choosing the right workspace for your creative task.

## 21. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/delete-workspaces.html

Learn how to delete workspaces in professional photoeditor to maintain a focused and organized workspace list.

To keep your workspace list clean, you can delete outdated or unused workspaces. Once deleted, custom workspaces cannot be restored. You must create and save custom workspaces again.

Select the Choose a workspace dropdown menu in the Application bar.

Select Delete Workspace.

In the dialog, select a workspace from the dropdown menu, and then select Delete. The workspace is deleted from the workspace list.

![Image 1: Delete Workspace dialog box in photoeditor, showing a dropdown to select a workspace and the Delete button to remove it.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Delete-Workspace-dialog-box-in-photoeditor--showing-?$png$&jpegSize=300&wid=1920)

Keep your workspace list clean by removing custom workspaces you no longer use.

Preset layouts are useful for common workflows, such as photo editing, painting, or graphic design. photoeditor warns you before deleting a preset workspace.

To restore a preset workspace, select Edit>Preferences>Workspace>Restore Default Workspaces.

## 22. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/restore-workspaces.html

Learn how to restore default and custom workspaces in professional photoeditor to reset your panel layout.

In photoeditor, workspaces display your last arrangement of panels, but you can also restore the original setup. You can restore both preset and custom workspaces.

## Restore the default workspace

Select the Choose a workspace dropdown menu in the Application bar.

Select the Essentials workspace. photoeditor restores to the default workspace, the Essentials workspace.

You can also select Window **>**Workspace **>**Reset>Essentials (Default).

Select the Choose a workspace dropdown menu in the Application bar.

To restore a single preset or custom workspace, choose Reset<Workspace Name>.

To restore all photoeditor preset workspaces, select Edit >Preferences >Workspace (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Workspace (macOS). Then, select Restore Default Workspaces.

This resets all photoeditor preset workspaces, like Essentials and Photography, to their original layout.

## 23. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/boost-workflows-with-the-contextual-task-bar.html

Learn how to use the Contextual Task Bar in professional photoeditor on desktop to access the most relevant tools and actions based on your selection.

The Contextual Task Bar is an on-canvas interface that provides quick access to commonly used actions based on what you’re working on, such as selecting an object, working on a layer, or editing part of an image.

It dynamically updates to show the most relevant actions directly on the canvas, helping you complete common workflows faster without navigating multiple panels.

Using the Contextual Task Bar can help:

*   Reduce the need to switch between panels and menus
*   Show relevant actions when you need them
*   Help speed up common workflows
*   Keep your focus on the canvas

## 24. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/rearrange-document-windows.html

Learn how to organize your document windows in professional photoeditor for better workflow efficiency.

When working with multiple files in photoeditor, managing your document windows efficiently can significantly improve your workflow. You can organize windows as tabs, float them freely, or create custom layouts to fit your needs.

Open multiple documents in photoeditor to begin organizing them.

Drag a window tab to a new location in the group to rearrange the order.

Drag the window’s tab out of the group to undock the document window.

Drag the window into the group to dock it with other document windows.

Drag the window onto another document to create stacked or tiled groups of documents.

![Image 1: professional photoeditor with four open documents arranged in a vertical tile layout, showing how multiple files can be viewed side by side.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/professional-photoeditor-with-four-open-documents-arranged-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1285)

Tile documents side by side for simultaneous viewing and editing in photoeditor.

Drag your selection over the document’s tab to switch to another document in the tabbed group.

You can also select Window>Arrange>Float in Window to float a single document window, or Window>Arrange>Float All In Windows to float all document windows at once.

## 25. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/hide-show-panels.html

Learn how to show or hide panels in professional photoeditor to maximize canvas space or access tools and control

Hide or show all panels in photoeditor to work smarter. Hide panels to maximize canvas space or show panels to access tools and controls for a more efficient workflow.

Press Tab to hide all panels, including the Tools panel and Options bar.

Press Tab again to show all panels, including the Tools panel and Options bar.

Press Shift+Tab to hide all panels except the Tools panel and Options bar.

Press Shift+Tab again to show all panels except the Tools panel and Options bar.

If Auto-Show Hidden Panels is enabled in Preferences>Workspace, you can temporarily display hidden panels by hovering near the edge of the application window (Windows) or monitor (macOS).


====================================================================================================

## 26. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/dock-undock-panels.html

Learn to dock or undock panels to organize your workspace in professional photoeditor.

A dock is a collection of panels grouped together vertically. You can dock and undock panels by moving them into and out of the dock.

Select Window, then select each panel you want to display, such as Brushes, Layers, or Swatches. The panels open in your photoeditor workspace.

To dock a panel, drag it by it’s tab to the edge of another panel or panel group.

Release the tab when a blue highlight appears.

![Image 1: A screenshot of the professional photoeditor interface with multiple panels visible to show how to dock and undock panels.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-screenshot-of-the-professional-photoeditor-interface-with?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1173)

Dock, undock, and organize panels in photoeditor to create a workspace that fits your workflow.

To dock a panel group, drag it by its title bar.

To undock a panel or group, drag it by its tab or title bar**.**

You can also drag a panel into another dock or make it free-floating.

Drag the bottom edge of the dock up so it no longer meets the edge of the workspace. It prevents panels from filling the entire workspace.

## 27. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/move-panels.html

Learn how to move panels in professional photoeditor to stay organized and work more efficiently.

Choose a panel by selecting its tab.

Drag the panel by its tab to a new location. When you move panels, blue drop zones show where you can place them**.**

![Image 1: Multiple panels open and arranged in the photoeditor workspace to show how panels can be moved and organized.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image533533?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1289)

Move and organize panels in photoeditor to match your workflow and edit images efficiently.

Drag a panel on to a blue zone to dock it. If there's no drop zone, the panel floats freely.

Press Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) while moving a panel to prevent docking.

Press Esc while moving the panel to cancel the operation.

To make a panel float freely, drag it away from any dock or drop zone to an empty workspace area.

Select Window >Workspace>Lock Workspace to prevent accidentally moving panels while using a stylus.

## 28. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/add-remove-panels.html

Learn how to add and remove panels to customize your workspace in professional photoeditor.

photoeditor panels contain essential tools and settings that enhance your workflow. By adjusting which panels are visible and their arrangement, you can create a workspace suited to your needs.

Select Window from the menu bar.

Select a panel to add to the workspace. The panel appears in the workspace, and a checkmark indicates that it is visible.

## Remove panels

Select Window from the menu bar.

Select the panel name to remove it from the workspace.

You can also right-click (Windows) or Control-click (macOS) its tab and select Close.

If you remove all panels from a dock, the dock disappears. To create a dock, move panels to the right edge of the workspace until a drop zone appears.

## 29. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/stack-floating-panels.html

Learn how to organize your workspace in professional photoeditor by grouping related panels into stacks.

In photoeditor, dragging a panel out of its dock creates a floating panel that you can position anywhere in the workspace. If you drag it into a drop zone, it docks again. You can stack floating panels or panel groups, so they move as a unit by dragging the title bar of the top panel.

Drag the tab of a floating panel.

Drop it on the lower edge of another panel when a blue outline appears. Ensure to drop the tab over the narrow drop zone between panels, not on the title bar.

To change the stacking order, drag a panel by its tab and move it up or down.

![Image 1: photoeditor workspace showing Swatches, Color, and Gradients panels stacked as floating panels on the left side.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-workspace-showing-Swatches--Color--and-G?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Drag and stack floating panels to organize your photoeditor workspace efficiently. 

To float a panel independently, drag its tab away from the stack.

Double-click a tab to minimize or maximize a panel, group, or stack. Drag any panel edge to resize it.

Learn how to expand or collapse panels to compact icons and efficiently manage space in professional photoeditor.

Collapsing panels into icons maintains a clean workspace and keeps tools accessible, especially on smaller screens. In photoeditor, you can expand panels fully or collapse them with or without labels, and the default workspace often shows some panels collapsed to icons.

Select a single panel icon to expand only that panel.

Select the double arrow at the upper-right of the dock to collapse all panels into icons.

To show only the icons, adjust the dock width until the labels disappear.

Widen the dock to display the labels again.

Customize your workspace by expanding key panels and collapsing others for quick access.

Select the double arrow in the panel’s title bar to collapse it back to an icon.

You can also collapse the panel by selecting its tab or icon.

Drag a floating panel or panel group into the icon dock using its title bar. The panel collapses to an icon in the dock.

Drag the panel icon up and down to reposition it within the dock or move it outside to float.

Move icons to other docks, where they will adopt that dock's style.

## 32. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/use-simple-math.html

Learn how to calculate values in professional photoeditor using simple math within numeric input fields.

You can perform basic math directly in the input fields that accept numeric values, such as width, height, font size, or canvas dimensions. Make precise adjustments easily by using mathematical expressions during design or editing.

Select any numeric input field, such as Width or Height.

Type a math expression in the field, such as 30+45, 300/2, 12*4, or 3 cm * 50%.

![Image 1: photoeditor workspace with canvas size dialog box showing width set to '4000/2.5' pixels and height at 4000 pixels.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-workspace-with-canvas-size-dialog-box-sh?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1257)

Use a math expression in the 

Press Enter to apply the result.

## Math expression examples

**Replace a value**: Type 100/4. The result is 25.

**Adjust a value**: Type+50 after or 50+ before 200.The result is 250.

**Use percentage**: Type 3 cm* 50%. The result is 1.5 cm.

## 33. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/high-density-monitor-support-and-per-monitor-scaling.html

Learn how photoeditor adjusts settings for high-density monitors to keep images sharp across all screen resolutions.

## High-density monitors

High-density displays have more pixels per inch than standard monitors. They show sharper text and clearer images. Applications must be designed for optimal display on these monitors.

On Windows 10 Creators Update and later, photoeditor offers UI scaling options from 100% to 400% in 25% increments. This flexibility ensures the photoeditor interface remains sharp and properly sized across various monitor configurations.

Set different scaling factors for multiple monitors. For example, use 175% on one monitor and 400% on another, allowing a smooth experience whether using a 4K laptop, a 1080p desktop, or an 8K monitor.

To adjust scaling settings on Windows, go to Start>Settings>System>Display, and select the scaling factor for each display. Then, under[Scale And Layout](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/help/4027860/windows-10-view-display-settings), choose a scaling factor for each display.

## photoeditor UI scaling

In Windows 10 and later, UI scaling only affects specific dialogs in photoeditor, like File Info and Camera Raw. To adjust photoeditor UI scaling, select Edit **>**Preferences **>** Interface **>**UI Scaling and choose Auto, 100%, or 200%based on your display. Setting it to Auto aligns with the primary monitor's 100% or 200% scaling.


====================================================================================================

Learn how to arrange panels and create custom panel groups in professional photoeditor.

photoeditor panels help you stay organized and efficient by keeping essential tools within easy reach. Arrange and group them to create a workspace tailored to your creative needs, especially for complex projects. For example, if you're editing a poster, organized panels help you work faster.

Select Window and choose the panels you need. The selected panels appear in the workspace.

To move a panel into a group, drag its tab to the highlighted drop zone within the group.

![Image 1: professional photoeditor workspace displays grouped panels, with one panel tab being dragged into a highlighted drop zone within a group.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/professional-photoeditor-workspace-displays-grouped-panels-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1205)

 Organize the panels, create a cleaner workspace, and boost your productivity.

To rearrange panels within a group, drag the panel's tab to a new location in the group.

To remove a panel from a group so it floats freely, drag the panel by its tab outside the group.

To move a group, drag the title bar that contains the panel tabs.

## 35. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/learn-the-basics/request-access-to-test-experimental-features.html

Request access to test experimental photoeditor features and help shape their development.

photoeditor beta features give you early access to experimental tools. Explore upcoming functionality and share feedback that shapes the final release.

Beta features are under active development and may not be fully stable. For experimental features already in your version, open photoeditor>Settings>Technology Previews (macOS) or Edit>Settings>Technology Previews (Windows).

You'll need an active subscription and approval from professional's testing program. Once approved, you'll receive email updates about available features and activation steps.

*   Active professional photoeditor subscription (paid)
*   Age verification (18 years or older)
*   Valid email address for program communications
*   Regular photoeditor usage to provide meaningful feedback

## Submit your access request

After approval, you'll receive updates about available beta features with activation instructions. Response times vary based on program capacity.

Beta features may behave differently than production tools. Some eventually become technology previews, while others may not reach final release.

Learn how to customize the toolbar and organize your tools and groups in professional photoeditor.

The photoeditor toolbar provides quick access to commonly used tools. Add, remove, and rearrange tools to suit your workflow. Customizing your toolbar can help you create a more efficient workspace.

Select Edit**>**Toolbar to open the Customize toolbar dialog box.

In the dialog box, organize the related tools in groups for easier access:

*   Drag and drop tools and groups to reorganize the toolbar.
*   Move less frequently used tools to Extra Tools.

![Image 1: Customize Toolbar dialog box with tools in Toolbar and Extra Tools sections, highlighting configuration icons in the Show section.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Customize-Toolbar-dialog-box-with-tools-in-Toolbar?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Customize your workspace by selecting which tools remain visible or which ones to move to Extra Tools.

Select Toggle, which shows extra tools in the last toolbar slot to access Extra Tools in the toolbar.

Select Save Preset to save your custom toolbar layout.

Select Load Preset to open a previously saved custom toolbar.

Select Restore Defaults to restore the default toolbar.

Select Clear Tools to move all the tools to Extra Tools.

Select Done to save your changes or Cancel to discard them.

## 37. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/show-or-hide-tool-tips.html

Learn about tooltips and how to turn them on or off in professional photoeditor.

Tooltips provide helpful information about tools and features. When you hover over a tool, panel option, or icon, a tooltip appears with a short description.

photoeditor provides two types of tooltips.

*   **Standard tooltips**: Display the tool name and keyboard shortcut.
*   **Rich tooltips**: Display a description and a short video of the tool in action.

## Manage tooltip settings

By default, the tooltips appear in photoeditor. You can control the display of standard tooltips or rich tooltips.

*   To turn off the tooltips, select Edit>Preferences>Tools, then deselect Show Tooltips and Show Rich Tooltips. 
*   To turn on the tooltips, select Edit>Preferences>Tools, then select Show Tooltips and Show Rich Tooltips.


====================================================================================================

## 38. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/create-tool-preset.html

Learn how to create custom tool presets in professional photoeditor to save specific settings for quick reuse across projects.

Tool presets in photoeditor allow you to save and quickly access your preferred settings for various tools, such as brushes and selection tools. Presets help you to maintain consistent workflows and promptly access your customized tools.

Select a tool and set the options you want to save as a tool preset in the Options bar.

Select Window>Tool Presets to display the Tool Presets panel.

You can also select the Tool Presets dropdown menu next to the tool at the upper-left of the Options bar.

Select Create New Tool Preset icon.

![Image 1: Tool Presets panel displays the New Tool Preset dialog box for creating presets.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Tool-Presets-panel-displays-the-New-Tool-Preset-di?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1294)

Use tool presets in photoeditor to save your favorite settings, quickly apply them, and streamline your workflow. 

Enter a name for the tool preset and select OK. Your new preset appears in the Tool Presets panel.

Select a preset to apply the saved settings to the selected tool quickly.

## Customize tool presets

Select menu in the upper-right of the Tool Presets panel.

To customize the list of presets, select one of the following options:

*   Show All Tool Presets: View all loaded presets.
*   Sort by Tool: Group presets by tool type.
*   Show Current Tool Presets: Display only presets for the active tool.
*   Text Only,Small List, or Large List: Choose how presets appear visually.

## 39. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/spring-loaded-shortcuts.html

Learn how to edit in professional photoeditor by using spring-loaded tool shortcuts for tools.

Spring-loaded tool shortcuts in photoeditor let you temporarily switch to a tool by holding its shortcut key. When you release the key, photoeditor automatically returns to your previous tool. This feature streamlines your workflow, especially for quick adjustments.

## Switch tools with shortcut keys

Select a tool from the Tools panel to use as your primary tool, such as the Brush tool.

To temporarily access another tool, press and hold its shortcut key.

For example, hold V while using the Brush tool to temporarily switch to the Move tool, and release it to return to the Brush tool.

Use the temporary tool to edit while holding down the shortcut key.

Release the shortcut key to return to your primary tool.

## Customize spring-loaded shortcut timing

You can adjust how quickly photoeditor switches to a spring-loaded tool by changing the delay setting.

Select Edit>Preferences>Tools(Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>Tools(macOS).

In the Preferences dialog box, ensure that the Spring-loaded Tool shortcuts is selected. it's turned on by default.

Drag the slider to adjust the delay time. A shorter delay means the tool switches faster when you press the key. The default is 200 milliseconds.

![Image 1: The Preferences dialog box shows the Tools section with “Spring-loaded Tool Shortcuts” enabled and timing set to 200 milliseconds.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Preferences-dialog-box-shows-the-Tools-section?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1323)

Set the timing of spring-loaded tool shortcuts to switch tools faster and streamline your editing workflow.

Select OK to save your changes.


====================================================================================================

## 40. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/use-undo-redo-commands.html

Learn how to quickly reverse or reapply actions in professional photoeditor using Undo and Redo commands.

The Undo command reverses your last action, while the Redo command reapplies an action you've just undone. Use Undo and Redo commands to correct mistakes, experiment freely, and work more efficiently.

## Undo and redo actions

Press Ctrl+Z (Windows) or Command + Z (macOS) to undo your last action.

Press Shift+Ctrl+Z (Windows) or Shift +Command+Z (macOS) to redo the undone action.

Select File>Revert to undo all changes since your last save.

Go to Edit>Undo or Edit>Redo to view the specific action name next to the command. For example, Edit>Undo Typing or Edit>Redo Typing.

## Switch to legacy undo shortcuts

To restore the traditional undo functionality from earlier photoeditor versions:

Select Edit>Keyboard Shortcuts and Menus.

Select Use Legacy Undo Shortcuts.

Select OK and restart photoeditor.


====================================================================================================

## 41. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/history-panel-overview.html

Explore History panel settings in professional photoeditor to manage image states, customize snapshots, and enhance your editing workflow.

The History panel in photoeditor lets you quickly access recent states of your image during the current session. Each change you make, like selecting, painting, or rotating, is recorded separately. When you select one of the states, the image reverts to how it looked when that change was first applied. You can then work from that state.

You can also delete states from the History panel and create a new document from a specific state or snapshot.

![Image 1: photoeditor interface with the History panel open, showing recent editing states.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-interface-with-the-History-panel-open--s?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Use the History panel to view, revert, and manage recent edits during your photoeditor session.

To use the History panel (Window>History) effectively, you can follow the best practices and explore customization options.

## Best practices

*   Use snapshots to preserve key states during your session.
*   Adjust the number of history states under Preferences>Performance to suit your workflow. By default, the History panel lists 20 states. You can increase it to 1,000 states for more complex editing sessions.
*   Select Allow Non-Linear History to edit from any state without deleting future ones.
*   Track edits efficiently by following the sequence. Older changes appear first, with newer ones added as you work.
*   Use Undo to restore deleted states after editing from a previous one. Learn more about the Undo and Redo commands overview.

Closing the document clears all history states and snapshots. The initial state snapshot appears first in the History panel. Dimmed states show edits that will be lost if you continue from a selected state.

## Menu options

Customize history states and snapshots from the History Options dialog box. You can access this from the History panel (Menu>History Options).

**Option****Description**
Automatically Create First Snapshot Saves a snapshot of the initial state of the image when opened.
Automatically Create New Snapshot When Saving Generates a snapshot every time you save the document. It is off by default.
Allow Non-Linear History Allows you to edit a selected state without deleting subsequent states.
Show New Snapshot Dialog By Default Prompts you for a name when creating snapshots.
Make Layer Visibility Changes Undoable Records the turning of layer visibility on or off as a history step**.**Deselect this option to exclude layer visibility changes.


====================================================================================================

## 42. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/history-log-preferences.html

Learn how to track your edits by customizing your History Log preferences in professional photoeditor to save logs in metadata, a text file, or both.

The History Log in photoeditor records all image edits, which is helpful for personal records, client documentation, and legal purposes. You can choose where to save this data and how much detail to include.

## Configure History Log settings

Select Edit>Preferences>History & Content Credentials (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>History & Content Credentials(macOS).

Select the History Log checkbox to turn it on.

Select Metadata,Text File, or Both to specify where the history log should be saved.

If you select Text File or Both, select Choose to set the location and name for the log file. specify the location and name for the log file.

![Image 1: photoeditor Preferences dialog box highlighting the History Log section with settings for saving log data and choosing the level of detail.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-Preferences-dialog-box-highlighting-the-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1162)

Set photoeditor History Log preferences to track edits in a way that fits your workflow and documentation needs.

From the Edit Log Items menu, select the level of detail:

*   Sessions Only: Tracks when photoeditor is opened or closed.
*   Concise: Adds History panel entries along with session information.
*   Detailed: Includes Actions panel entries for complete edit tracking.

Select OK to apply your settings.

After configuration, photoeditor tracks your edits based on your preferences.

Save the edit log in metadata and sign it digitally to ensure your log file security.

Using detailed logs may increase file size and slow performance.


====================================================================================================

## 45. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/paint-image-states.html

Use the History Brush tool in professional photoeditor to paint parts of your image using a previous state or snapshot.

The History Brush tool allows you to paint with a previous state or snapshot selectively. It works by sampling an earlier version of the image and applying it selectively.

You can use it to restore specific areas of an image to a previous state while keeping other edits intact. For example, you can apply a filter to your entire image, but then restore the original appearance of certain elements.

The tool paints from the same layer in the selected state to the same layer in the current state, unless you use a merged snapshot. You can also paint with the Art History Brush tool to create special effects in photoeditor.

Select Window >History to open the History panel.

In the History panel, select the left column next to the desired state or snapshot to set it as the source for the History Brush tool. A brush icon appears next to the selected history state.

Select the History Brush tool (Y).

Use the Options bar to customize tool settings, like Size and Hardness, to get the desired effect.

Drag to paint with the History Brush tool.

![Image 1: Before and after comparison of a hot air balloon image restored with the History Brush tool, showing the basket added back in the right image.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Before-and-after-comparison-of-a-hot-air-balloon-i?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1265)

Use the History brush tool to paint with the History panel image states.


====================================================================================================

## 46. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/manage-image-states.html

Learn how to use the History panel to create new documents, revert to earlier edits, or remove unwanted changes.

The History panel in photoeditor lets you quickly access recent image states. Each change is automatically recorded as a separate history state. You can revert to any state to continue working from that point.

Select Window>History to view the History panel.

## Create documents from image states

You can generate a new document from any image state or snapshot.

Select a state or snapshot from the History panel.

Select Create a new document from current state on the lower-right of the History panel.

![Image 1: History panel in photoeditor showing multiple image states, with options to revert, delete, or create a new document from a selected state.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/History-panel-in-photoeditor-showing-multiple-image-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1275)

Preserve different versions by creating a new document from any state or snapshot.

To replace the content of an open document, drag a state onto an existing document to update its content.

The new document only contains the Duplicate State in the History panel.

## Revert to previous image states

Select the name of a state from the History panel. The image returns to how it looked when you first made this change.

Select Step Forward or Step Backward from the History panel menu to move to the next or previous state.

You can also select Edit>Step Backward or Edit>Step Forward to navigate through states.

Use File>Revert to return to the last saved version. Revert is added as a history state in the History panel and can be undone.

## Delete image states

Select the name of a state from the History panel.

Select Delete current state from the lower-right to delete that change and any changes made after it.

In the dialog box, select Yes to confirm the deletion. Select No to cancel.

To delete all states without changing your image, select Clear History from the History panel menu.

Press Alt (Windows)or option(macOS)and choose Clear History to purge states and free memory.

Edit>Purge>Histories to delete states across all open documents.


====================================================================================================

## 47. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/restore-image-parts.html

Learn how to restore previous states of your image in professional photoeditor, without undoing your recent edits.

photoeditor tools, such as the History Brush tool, Eraser tool, and Fill tool, let you restore specific areas of an image. These tools help you recover textures or details while preserving overall enhancements, streamlining your workflow, and maintaining editing flexibility.

## Restore with the History Brush tool

Select Window>History to open the History panel.

Select the box labeled Sets the source for the History Brush next to the desired state or snapshot to set it as the source.

Select the History Brush tool.

Paint over the areas you want to restore. Learn more about how to paint with image states from the History panel.

## Restore with the Eraser tool

Select the Eraser  tool.

Select Erase To History from the Options bar.

## Restore with the Fill tool

Select the area to restore.

Select Edit>Fill.

In the Contents dropdown menu, select History.

Select OK to restore the selected area.

Enable Automatically Create First Snapshot in History Options to preserve the initial state.


====================================================================================================

## 49. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/set-up-toolbars-panels/view-and-synchronize-multiple-images.html

Synchronize zoom and location across multiple views in photoeditor.

Learn how to use multiple windows to compare different images side by side, view the same image at different zoom levels, or work on one area while monitoring changes elsewhere. You can arrange windows in preset layouts and synchronize their zoom level or scroll position to maintain consistent viewing across all open windows.

Most synchronization features require multiple open windows. You can either open two or more different images, or create multiple windows of the same image to view it at different zoom levels or with different display settings.

## Open additional windows for an image

Select Window>Arrange>New Window For [Image File Name].

Select Window>Arrange and choose an option from the menu to arrange the newly opened windows.

The number of windows you can open per image is limited to available memory.

Each window displays the same image but can show different zoom levels, color modes, or layer visibility settings. Changes made in one window update instantly in all other windows for that image.

## Arrange multiple windows

Open two or more images, or open one image in multiple windows.

Select Window>Arrange

Choose one of the following layout options:

*   Cascade: Stacks undocked windows from upper-left to lower-right
*   Tile: Displays windows edge to edge and resizes them as you close images
*   Float in Window: Allows the active image to float freely
*   Float All in Windows: Floats all open images
*   Consolidate All to Tabs: Displays one image in full screen and minimizes others to tabs.

## Synchronize zoom across windows

Open two or more images, or open one image in multiple windows.

Select Window>Arrange>Tile to display images edge to edge.

Select the Zoom tool from the toolbar.

Select Zoom All Windows in the options bar.

Click any image to zoom in or out. All visible images zoom by the same relative amount.

Alternative method: Select Window>Arrange>Match Zoom, hold Shift, and click one image. All other images zoom to the same magnification percentage.

## Synchronize location across windows

Open two or more images, or open one image in multiple windows.

Select Window>Arrange>Tile.

Select Window>Arrange>Match Location.

You can also select the Hand tool , enable Scroll All Windows in the options bar, and drag in any image to scroll all visible images to the corresponding location. To temporarily enable this option without selecting it, hold Shift while dragging with the Hand tool.

photoeditor scrolls to the same relative position on horizontal and vertical axes. You may need to manually scroll to reveal edges of images with different dimensions.

## Synchronize zoom and location simultaneously

Open two or more images, or open one image in multiple windows.

Select Window>Arrange>Tile.

Select Window>Arrange>Match All.

Select the Zoom tool or the Hand tool .

Hold Shift and click in or drag an area of any image. All other images magnify to the same percentage and snap to the area you selected.


====================================================================================================

## 50. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/adjust-preferences.html

Learn how to customize and save app settings, including display, file saving, performance, presets, and plug-ins from the Preferences dialog in professional photoeditor.

All settings are saved when you close the app, but they are lost if you force quit or the app crashes.

By default, settings in Preferences migrate from a prior version to an updated version, and you can choose to keep or remove them upon uninstalling a version.

## Open and adjust Preferences

To open the Preferences dialog, select Edit>Preferences (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings (macOS).

You can also use the keyboard shortcut Ctrl + K (Windows) or Command + K (macOS).

Select OK.

## Reset preferences

Learn how to quickly reset preferences in photoeditor in the video below.

[Video 3](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=roGwptAmnNw)

## Preferences categories[](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/adjust-preferences.html)

photoeditor organizes preferences into the following categories:

*   General**:** Configure general application behavior and default settings.
*   Interface**:** Customize the appearance and behavior of the photoeditor interface.
*   Workspace**:** Manage workspace options and panel behavior.
*   Notifications**:** Control in-app notifications and alerts.
*   Tools**:** Customize the behavior of photoeditor tools.
*   History & Content Credentials**:** Manage history options and Content Credentials settings.
*   File Handling**:** Configure file opening, saving, and compatibility settings.
*   Export**:** Set default options for exporting files.
*   Performance**:** Optimize memory usage, GPU, and other performance settings.
*   Image Processing**:** Configure image processing and interpolation options.
*   Scratch Disks**:** Select and manage scratch disks used for temporary storage.
*   Cursors**:** Customize the appearance of painting and tool cursors.
*   Transparency & Gamut**:** Adjust transparency grid and gamut warning settings.
*   Units & Rulers**:** Set measurement units, rulers, and column sizes.
*   Guides, Grid & Slices**:** Customize guides, grids, and slice settings.
*   Plug-ins**:** Manage plug-in loading and related preferences.
*   Type**:** Configure text and font-related settings.
*   Enhanced Controls**:** Enable and manage advanced editing controls.
*   Technology Previews**:** Try experimental features before they're released.


====================================================================================================

## 51. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/backup-and-restore-preferences.html

Use backup and restore preferences to transfer settings between computers or quickly recover your setup after reinstalling professional photoeditor.

Save your customized photoeditor settings to prevent loss during crashes or updates by creating preference file backups.

photoeditor stores your customized settings in preference files that can be lost during crashes or updates. Creating backups of these files ensures you can quickly restore your personalized workspace, keyboard shortcuts, and other settings when needed.

Close the photoeditor application completely.

Navigate to the Preferences folder location:

*   **macOS**: Users/<user name>/Library/Preferences/professional photoeditor <version> Settings
*   **Windows**: Users/<user name>/AppData/Roaming/professional/professional photoeditor <version>/professional photoeditor <version> Settings

Copy the professional photoeditor <version> Settings folder to another location.

Label your backup with the date and photoeditor version for easy identification later.

## Restore preferences from backup

Close the photoeditor application completely.

Navigate to the Preferences folder location:

*   **macOS**: Users/<user name>/Library/Preferences/professional photoeditor <version> Settings
*   **Windows**: Users/<user name>/AppData/Roaming/professional/professional photoeditor <version>/professional photoeditor <version> Settings

Rename or move the current professional photoeditor <version> Settings folder to create a temporary backup.

Copy your previously backed-up professional photoeditor <version> Settings folder to this location.

Launch photoeditor to verify your restored preferences.

On macOS, the user Library folder is hidden by default. To access it, hold Option while selecting the Go menu in Finder, then select Library.

Create a regular backup schedule for your preferences, especially before major updates or when you've made significant customizations to your workspace or keyboard shortcuts.


====================================================================================================

## 54. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/change-tool-pointers.html

Customize how your tool cursors appear by switching between standard icons, precise crosshairs, or brush size indicators for better control and accuracy.

photoeditor offers different cursor styles to help you work more precisely with various tools. Each tool pointer has a hotspot where an effect or action begins in your image. By changing the cursor display, you can better visualize this hotspot and work more accurately.

The default pointer for most tools matches their icon in the Tools panel. However, when working on detailed projects, you might prefer more precise cursors that show precisely where your edits will take effect.

Select Edit>Preferences>Cursors (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Cursors (macOS).

Select one of the following options from Painting Cursors:

*   Standard: Displays pointers as tool icons.
*   Precise: Displays pointers as cross hairs.
*   Normal Brush Tip: Displays an outline corresponding to about 50% of the area the tool will affect.
*   Full Size Brush Tip: Displays an outline corresponding to nearly 100% of the area the tool will affect.
*   Show Crosshair in Brush Tip: Displays cross hairs in the center of the brush shape.
*   Show Only Crosshair While Painting: Improves performance with large brushes.

![Image 1: A sample document showing the Cursor options in the Preferences panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-showing-the-Cursor-options-in-th?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Change the tool pointers from the Cursors section of the Preferences panel.

Select Standard or Precise from Other Cursors.

Select a color from Brush Preview.

Select OK to apply your changes.

Hold Alt + right-click mouse (Windows) or Control + Option(macOS) and drag left or right to resize the brush cursor.

Hold Alt + right-click mouse (Windows) or Control + Option(macOS) and drag up or down to change the brush hardness.


====================================================================================================

## 55. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/change-app-language-settings.html

Configure Creative Cloud and photoeditor settings to display menus and panels in your preferred language.

photoeditor's display language has two controls: the Creative Cloud desktop app sets the installation language, and photoeditor's preferences control the interface language.

*   Creative Cloud desktop app installed
*   Administrative access (required if photoeditor is already installed)

## Set the default install language

Open the Creative Cloud desktop app.

Select the account icon in the upper right, and then select Preferences.

Select Apps in the left sidebar.

Select your preferred language from the Default install language dropdown.

Select Done to save the change.

Uninstall and reinstall photoeditor to apply the language change.After reinstalling photoeditor in your preferred language, you can adjust the interface language within the app.

If your operating system is already set to a non-English language, photoeditor installs and launches in that language automatically.

## Change the photoeditor interface language

In photoeditor, select Edit>Preferences>Interface (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Interface (macOS).

Select your desired language from the UI Language dropdown.

Select OK to close the preferences.

Restart photoeditor for the change to take effect.Result: The interface displays all menus, panels, and dialogs in your selected language.

The interface language change takes effect immediately upon restart, displaying all menus, panels, and dialogs in your selected language.

## 56. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/connect-social-media-accounts-for-content-credentials.html

Link your social media handles to Content Credentials by logging into accounts on professional's connected accounts page.

Adding social media handles to Content Credentials (Beta) connects your identity to your work and establishes trust with your audience. This workflow adds social media accounts specifically to Content Credentials (Beta). If you've previously connected social media to your professional ID elsewhere, you'll still need to connect those accounts through the Content Credentials Connected Accounts page. Adding or removing accounts through this workflow affects only Content Credentials (Beta) and won't disconnect accounts from your professional ID.

To connect social media accounts, you need active login credentials for each platform you want to link. Handles added to Content Credentials (Beta) appear on exported images when you attach credentials, and those handles cannot be changed on previously exported content.

You can practice connecting your social media accounts directly in photoeditor to see how your handles appear in Content Credentials. Before you begin, ensure the following:

*   Content Credentials is enabled in photoeditor (learn how to [Use Content Credentials](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/metadata-content-credentials/use-content-credentials.html))
*   Active login credentials for social media accounts you want to connect
*   Internet connection

## Open the Connected Accounts page

Adding or removing your Behance account from Content Credentials doesn't affect other Behance logins or disconnect it from your professional ID.

## Add other social media accounts

## Select which accounts appear in Content Credentials

Selected accounts are included with your Content Credentials when you export images using the attach or publish options. Try [Exporting content with Content Credentials](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/metadata-content-credentials/export-your-work-with-content-credentials.html) to learn how to share images using connected accounts.

## 57. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/use-the-info-panel-and-document-status-bar.html

Use the document status bar and Info panel to monitor image properties, color values, and workspace metrics in photoeditor.

Learn how to use the document status bar for quick reference to file size, dimensions, color profile, and GPU mode. The Info panel provides more detailed monitoring, including live color values under your pointer, coordinate positions, and customizable status information.

The status bar displays automatically with every open document, while the Info panel requires a one-time setup to match your workflow needs.

## Check file data in the document status bar

Select the status bar area to display document width, height, channels, and resolution. Ctrl-click (Windows) or Command-click (macOS) to see tile dimensions.

You can also configure color readout modes quickly by selecting the eyedropper icon directly in the Info panel and choosing options from the menu. This menu also lets you switch between 8-bit, 16-bit, and 32-bit value displays.

## 59. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/generative-ai/frequently-asked-questions-about-generative-ai-features.html

Get answers to frequently asked questions about using Generative AI features in professional photoeditor.

### Who can access generative AI features in photoeditor?

Generative AI features in photoeditor are available to photoeditor trialists and subscribers wherever professional provides services.

photoeditor users of all levels can use generative AI features. Easily import and open an image, make a selection, and enter a simple text prompt to achieve realistic results that will surprise, delight, and astound you.

### Why did you bring generative AI in photoeditor?

photoeditor is the first Creative Cloud app to natively integrate professional Firefly, professional’s powerful generative AI technology. Users can leverage the power of professional Firefly directly in photoeditor to enrich their creative process and creative outputs.

### What are the technical requirements to use the generative AI features in photoeditor?

You can check the photoeditor on desktop technical requirements.

### Do you need to be connected to the internet to use generative AI features?

Yes, in order to use generative AI in photoeditor at this time, you need to be connected to the internet.

### Which languages and regions are generative AI features available in?

Generative AI features are available in the photoeditor desktop app wherever professional provides services. photoeditor is bringing the power of generative AI to global audiences by supporting over 100 languages for text prompt inputs. It is not available for users in China.

### Can users add images from Creative Cloud, professional Stock, the public domain, web, or other AI image generators?

Users can open and import assets, bring them into photoeditor, then use generative AI features. Each time you select Generate, a new Generative Layer is created. With the introduction of this new layer, a user must then save as a PSD or other layered file type to preserve their edits. There are multiple ways that you could end up with a PSD or other layered file type and a simple Save will preserve the layer. Not need to use Save As or Save a Copy.

### How does generative AI in photoeditor innovate the image editing experience?

Generative AI features are natively integrated into photoeditor desktop and on the web, so you can explore freely while still maintaining complete control over your creative process and outputs, all inside photoeditor.

Easily import and open an image, make a selection, and enter a simple text prompt to see the possibilities.

The newly generated content is created in a generative layer, enabling you to explore a myriad of creative possibilities and to reverse the effects when you want, without impacting your original image. Then, use the power and precision of photoeditor to take your image to the next level, surpassing even your own expectations.

### How do generative AI features in photoeditor accelerate creative workflows?

Generative AI empowers you to create as quickly as you can type -- experiment with off-the-wall ideas, ideate around different concepts, and produce dozens of variations in a snap. Easily generate multiple image suggestions until you land on a variation you like. High-quality results, which previously took hours to create, are now produced instantly, enabling you to spend more time honing your creativity.

### Are there any tutorials or learning guides available for generative AI features in photoeditor?

Check out professional photoeditor[Help resources](https://helpx.professional.com/support/photoeditor.html)and[Learn resources](https://creativecloud.professional.com/cc/learn/app/photoeditor?topic=generative-ai)to get acquainted with generative AI in photoeditor.

### Will generative credits be deducted when using generative AI features in photoeditor?

Yes, generative AI features in photoeditor, deduct one[generative credit](https://helpx.professional.com/creative-cloud/help/generative-credits-access-and-use.html)per generation.

### What is the maximum resolution of the output created using generative AI features in photoeditor?

The Firefly model currently returns output of 1024x1024 pixels to photoeditor on the desktop and the web.
