## 66. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/import-files/import-images-from-a-digital-camera.html

Connect your camera or card reader to transfer photos using direct copying, professional Bridge, or Windows Image Acquisition.

photoeditor offers multiple ways to transfer images from your camera to your computer. Your choice depends on your operating system, camera model, and whether you need to organize and rename files during import.

Most digital cameras and card readers connect to your computer via USB and appear as external drives or devices.

## Choose your import method

Your camera or card reader determines which import methods are available:

*   **Direct copying**: Transfers files when your device appears as a drive in your file system. Provides the fastest option for simple transfers.
*   **professional Bridge**: Organizes, batch-renames, and applies metadata during download. Opens separately from photoeditor.
*   **Windows Image Acquisition (WIA)**: Imports directly into photoeditor using the built-in Windows interface. Works with WIA-compatible cameras only.
*   **Manufacturer software or macOS Image Capture**: Imports files using alternative tools provided by your camera manufacturer or operating system.

## Import using WIA

Connect your camera or card reader to your computer and verify that Windows recognizes the device.

In photoeditor, select File>Import>WIA Support.

Select a destination folder to save imported files.

Select Open Acquired Images in photoeditor to edit photos immediately after import. Deselect for large batches.

Select a Unique Subfolder to create a date-named folder for organizing your imported images.

Select Start to launch the import interface.

Choose your camera or card reader from the device list.

Select the images you want to import by clicking thumbnails. Hold Shift while clicking to select multiple consecutive images or select Select All to import everything.

Select Get Picture to transfer the selected images to your destination.

## Alternative import methods

### Use professional Bridge for organization

Launch professional Bridge.
Select File > Get Photos From Camera.
Set your options for renaming files, applying metadata, and organizing images into folders.
Complete the transfer.

### Copy directly from your device

When your camera or card reader appears as a drive in Windows Explorer or macOS Finder, drag and drop image files directly to your computer. This method works for any camera or card but doesn't offer renaming or metadata options.

### Use manufacturer software or OS tools

Your camera may include transfer software with device-specific features. Windows users can access WIA through the operating system's Scanner and Camera Wizard. macOS users can use the Image Capture application for device management and file transfer.

TWAIN scanner interfaces aren’t supported in current versions of photoeditor. For scanning documents or photos, use dedicated scanning workflows instead of camera import procedures.


====================================================================================================

## 67. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/import-files/use-raster-and-vector-files.html

Learn how raster and vector graphics differ so you can choose the right approach for your photoeditor projects.

photoeditor supports two types of images: raster and vector. Raster images use pixels, while vector images use scalable shapes.

## Raster images

Raster images use a grid of tiny squares called pixels. Each pixel stores a color.

When you edit raster images, you modify individual pixels.

### Resolution dependence

Raster images have a fixed number of pixels.

*   Enlarging adds new pixels and can cause blur or pixelation.
*   Reducing removes pixels and removes detail.

Plan your image size to avoid quality loss.

### File size considerations

Raster images store color data for every pixel, which increases file size.

Compression reduces file size:

*   JPEG (lossy): Removes some image data.
*   PNG (lossless): Preserves all image data.

Choose a format based on quality and file size needs.

### Common raster formats

photoeditor works with several raster file formats, each suited to different purposes:

*   JPEG: Supports a wide range of colors; ideal for web images.
*   PNG: Supports transparency with no quality loss.
*   TIFF: Used for print and archiving.
*   GIF: Supports simple graphics and animation.
*   PSD: photoeditor’s native format with layers.

## Vector images

Vector images use shapes, lines, and curves instead of pixels. Mathematical definitions keep them sharp at any size.

They are used for logos, icons, text, and illustrations.

### Resolution independence

Vector images do not rely on pixels.

You can scale them to any size without losing quality. This makes them ideal for designs used across multiple sizes and formats.

### File size advantages

Vector files are smaller than raster files for simple graphics.

However, complex vector artwork can increase file size. Converting photos to vector formats can create large files.

### Common vector formats

While professional Illustrator specializes in vector creation, photoeditor can work with these vector formats:

*   AI: professional Illustrator format.
*   EPS: Print-ready vector format.
*   SVG: Web-friendly vector format.
*   PDF: Supports both vector and raster content.

## Working with both types in photoeditor

photoeditor creates raster images by default. You edit pixels when you paint, retouch, or apply filters.

You can also add vector elements:

*   Use shape tools to create scalable vector layers.
*   Use type layers to keep text sharp.

These elements work together in a single document.

### Quality considerations for combined artwork

Combining vector and raster content affects output quality.

### Transparency and flattening

photoeditor flattens artwork with transparency when printing or exporting.

Flattening merges layers into a single image.

### Image resolution

Image resolution measures how many pixels fit into 1 inch. This value is called pixels per inch (PPI).

*   Low PPI → pixelated images.
*   High PPI → larger files with no visible benefit beyond limits.

Guidelines:

*   Screen: 72–96 PPI.
*   Desktop print: 150–300 PPI.
*   Professional print: 300 PPI.

### Printer resolution and screen frequency

*   PPI (pixels per inch): Image resolution.
*   DPI (dots per inch): Printer resolution.
*   LPI (lines per inch): Print screen detail.

Use appropriate resolution settings based on your output.


====================================================================================================

## 68. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/import-files/place-a-file-in-photoeditor.html

Use the Place command or drag-and-drop to add files to your document.

When you place a file, photoeditor adds it as a Smart Object, a special layer type that preserves the original file's quality. The placed file becomes a new layer that you can position, resize, and transform. This works with most photoeditor-supported formats, including JPEG, PNG, TIFF, PSD, and many others.

Smart Objects link to the original file data and reference the original resolution, letting you scale them without losing image quality.

## Add a file to your document

Open the photoeditor document where you want to add the file.

Select File>Place Embedded or File>Place Linked from the menu.

In the file browser, select the file you want to place and select Place. In the file browser, select the file you want to place and select Place. The file appears in a bounding box at the centre of your document.

Drag inside the bounding box or the corner handles to resize it. Hold Shift while dragging to maintain the original proportions.

Select Commit in the options bar or press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS) to finalize the placement.

The file appears as a Smart Object layer in the Layers panel. You'll see a small page icon  in the layer thumbnail.

## Alternative placement methods

You can place files using these methods:

*   **Drag-and-drop method**: Drag a file directly from Windows Explorer or macOS Finder onto your open document. The file appears where you drop it.
*   **professional Bridge integration method**: In professional Bridge, select a file and choose File>Place>In photoeditor to place it into the currently active photoeditor document.

Both methods create Smart Objects and display the same transformation options.

## Transform placed content

While the bounding box is active, you can apply these transformations:

*   **Scale:** Drag any corner handle. Hold Shift to constrain proportions.
*   **Rotate:** Position your cursor outside the bounding box until it becomes a curved arrow, then drag to rotate. You can also enter a specific rotation value in the options bar.
*   **Reposition:** Drag anywhere inside the bounding box to move the placed content. Enter precise X and Y values in the options bar for exact positioning.
*   **Skew:** Hold Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) and drag a side handle to create a skew effect.

If the placed file is larger than your document, photoeditor automatically scales it to fit while maintaining the aspect ratio.

## Embedded and linked placement

When placing files, you choose between two options:

*   Place Embedded**:**Stores the file data within your document. The file size increases, but all content is self-contained.
*   Place Linked: Maintains a reference to the external file. Changes to the original file update in photoeditor, and your document size stays smaller.

Choose embedded placement when you want a standalone document. Choose linked placement when you need to update content across multiple documents or maintain smaller file sizes.

## Edit a placed Smart Object

To edit a placed Smart Object later, double-click its thumbnail in the Layers panel.

Make your changes in the Smart Object, as the content opens in a new window.

Save the Smart Object file to see updates in your main document.


====================================================================================================

## 69. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/import-files/open-dicom-files.html

Configure frame display, adjust windowing settings, and control metadata visibility when opening medical imaging files in photoeditor.

DICOM (Digital Imaging and Communications in Medicine) files contain multiple slices or frames representing different layers of a scan. photoeditor supports files with .dc3, .dcm, .dic extensions, or no extension, and automatically converts 10-bit and 12-bit files to 16-bit during import.

## Select and configure DICOM frames

Before you begin, you need photoeditor with DICOM file support.

Select File>Open, choose a DICOM file, and select Open.

In the DICOM File Import dialog, select the frames to import by Shift-clicking for contiguous frames or Ctrl-clicking (Windows) / Command-clicking (macOS) for noncontiguous frames.

Under Frame Import, choose Import Frames As Layers to place each frame on a separate layer, or select N-Up Configuration and enter values in the Rows and Columns boxes to display frames in a grid on a single layer.

Under DICOM Dataset, select Anonymize to overwrite patient metadata, or select Show Overlays to display annotations, curves, or text embedded in the file.

Select Show Windowing Options to adjust image contrast and brightness:

*   Drag the Window Level tool vertically for brightness or horizontally for contrast
*   Choose a preset from the Window Preset menu: Default, Lung, Bone, or Abdomen
*   Select Reverse Image to invert brightness values if needed

Select Open to complete the import.

### Navigation tips

Scroll through frames using the mouse wheel (Windows) or the arrow buttons below the preview.

Zoom using the Select Zoom Level menu or plus/minus buttons.

Pan by selecting the Hand icon and dragging

### Measurement scales

photoeditor automatically imports embedded measurement scales. Without a scale, photoeditor defaults to 1 pixel = 1 mm. For details on working with measurement scales, see [Set the measurement scale](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/measurement.html#set_the_measurement_scale).

## Edit and save DICOM files

After opening a DICOM file, use any photoeditor tool to edit, annotate, or measure the image:

Add comments with the Notes tool

Mark specific areas with the Pencil tool

Remove artifacts with the Dust and Scratches filter.

Measure anatomical features with the Ruler or selection tools.

### Saving options

*   8-bit DICOM files: Save in any format supported by photoeditor 
*   16-bit files: Use DICOM, Large Document Format (PSB), photoeditor (PSD), photoeditor PDF, photoeditor Raw, PNG, or TIFF to preserve bit depth 

When you save as DICOM format, layer styles, adjustments, blend modes, and masks are discarded. To preserve these elements, save a copy in PSD format before exporting as DICOM.

To import a sequence of multiple single-frame DICOM files into a single multilayered document, see [Import image sequences](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/importing-video-files-image-sequences.html#import_image_sequences).


====================================================================================================

## 70. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/import-files/open-and-import-files-in-photoeditor.html

Bring images into photoeditor using File menu commands and format options.

Use these methods to open standard image files stored on your computer or network, including JPEG, PNG, TIFF, PSD, PDF, and other common formats.

photoeditor uses plug-in modules to support various file formats. If a format doesn't appear in the Open dialog box or File>Import submenu, install the required plug-in module. When transferring files between macOS and Windows, file format identification can fail. If photoeditor cannot automatically determine a file's format, you can manually specify the correct format during the open process.

## Open a file

Select File>Open.

Navigate to the file you want to open.

If the file doesn't appear, select the option for showing all files from the Files Of Type (Windows) or Enable (macOS) menu.

Select the filename, then Open.

If a color profile warning appears, specify whether to use the embedded profile as the working space, convert the document color to the working space, or discard the embedded profile.

For format-specific files such as Camera Raw or EPS, a dialog box may appear with additional options before the file opens fully.

## Open a recent file

Select File>Open Recent.

Choose a file from the submenu.

To change the number of files in the Open Recent menu, select Edit > Preferences > File Handling (Windows) or photoeditor > Preferences > File Handling (macOS), then adjust the Recent File List Contains value.

## Specify the file format

On Windows, select File>Open As and choose the file. On macOS, select File>Open and choose All Documents from the Show menu.

Select the file you want to open.

Choose the correct format from the Open As menu (Windows) or Format menu (macOS).

Select Open. If the file doesn't open, the format may not match the file's actual format, or the file may be damaged.

## Duplicate an open image

With the image open, select Image>Duplicate.

Enter a name for the duplicated image.

To merge all layers during duplication, select Duplicate Merged Layers Only. To keep layers separate, leave this option deselected.

Select OK.

The duplicated image opens in a new window with all layers, masks, and channels intact (unless merged). This creates a copy in memory without saving to disk.

Select File>Open and choose the EPS file.

Set the desired dimensions, resolution, and color mode.

To maintain the height-to-width ratio, select Constrain Proportions.

To reduce jagged edges on artwork, select Anti-aliased.

EPS files can contain both vector and bitmap data. When you open an EPS file, photoeditor rasterizes the vector artwork, converting lines and curves into pixels.

## Preserve Illustrator layers

To retain layers, masks, transparency, and editable type when bringing Illustrator artwork into photoeditor, export from Illustrator using the photoeditor (PSD) file format. If your Illustrator art contains elements that photoeditor doesn't support, the appearance is preserved, but layers are merged, and the artwork is rasterized.

## 75. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/create-images/use-reference-images-for-consistent-results.html

Learn how to use Reference Image to guide image generation for consistent composition in professional photoeditor on desktop.

Select professional models>Firefly Fill & Expand.

Set how the reference is used:

*   Reference to**:**
    *   Object**:** References a single subject.
    *   Whole image**:** References the entire scene.

*   Intent**:**
    *   Swap the selected area**:** Replaces an existing object in the selected area.
    *   Place into the selected area**:** Adds an object while preserving the background.

Select Generate.

Select Partner models, then enter a detailed prompt.

## 77. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/create-images/edit-images-with-prompt-to-edit.html

Learn how to make AI-powered edits to an image with Prompt to edit using simple text prompts in professional photoeditor on desktop.

Use Prompt to edit to make edits an entire image using simple text prompts. Describe the edits you want, and photoeditor will generate variations for you to preview and apply.

Select File>Open to open an image in photoeditor.

To edit the entire image, select a pixel or generative layer. If you're working with a generative layer, ensure the entire image is selected.

To edit only part of an image, use a selection tool and Generative fill instead.

In the Contextual Task Bar, select Prompt to edit, or Edit>Prompt to Edit.

![Image 1: Prompt to edit selected in the Contextual Task Bar over an open image. ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/ps-prompt-to-edit-updated?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Select Prompt to edit in the Contextual Task Bar, or choose Edit > Prompt to Edit

Enter a prompt describing the changes you want to make across the image.

Sample prompts include:

*   Change the lighting to a warm sunset tone.
*   Improve overall brightness and contrast.
*   Change the season to rainy, add rainfall and puddles.
*   Change the pose to standing and dancing.

Select an AI model from the model picker dropdown, then Generate to create a variation based on the prompt.

*   professional models**:**Firefly Image 5
*   Partner models**:**FLUX.2 pro, FLUX Kontext pro,Gemini 3.1 (with Nano Banana 2),Gemini 3 (with Nano Banana Pro), GPT Image 2

Google is deprecating Gemini 2.5 (with Nano Banana) starting October 2, 2026. photoeditor will begin removing this model support on September 10 and with the September 2026 build (v27.11) and later versions.For more information, refer to [Generative AI model retirement](https://helpx.professional.com/creative-cloud/apps/generative-ai/model-deprecation.html).

## Differences between Generate Image, Generative Fill, and Prompt to edit

Generate Image, Generative Fill, and Prompt to edit appear depending on whether you create a new image, edit the entire image, or work on a selected area.

**What you’re working on****What appears in the Contextual Task Bar****Purpose**
Empty or transparent layer Generate Image Create a new image from a text prompt.
Existing image with no selection Prompt to edit Make edits to an entire image with a text prompt.
Entire image selected Prompt to edit Make edits to an entire image with a text prompt.
Partial selection Generative Fill Edit only the selected area.

## 78. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/create-images/use-markup-for-ai-image-generation.html

Learn how to use text and drawing tools to guide AI edits in professional photoeditor on desktop.

_Use Markup to guide AI edits in Photoshop_

Markup lets you add instructions directly on your image to guide AI edits. Use text or drawing tools to show what you want to change, where changes should apply, or how new elements should appear.

Add notes to specify edits, draw rough shapes to indicate placement, and combine markup with text prompts for more precise results.

To use the text and brush markup tools, select a Gemini AI model from the model picker dropdown in Prompt to edit.

Select File>Open to browse and open an image.

Select a pixel or generative layer. If you select a generative layer, select the entire image.

Select a Gemini AI model from the model picker dropdown.

Select a markup tool and add instructions to your image.

*   Use the **text** tool to add notes describing the changes you want to make. 
*   Use the **brush** tool to draw marks or rough shapes that indicate where changes should appear. 

Tip

For best results, use colors like red (#FF0000) and electric blue (#00FFFF) for markups.

![Image 1: Image open with the Layers panel with Gemini model generating edits based on markups.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProdLoc/ps-ai-markup?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Use Prompt to edit with Gemini AI models and markup tools to guide AI edits.

![Image 2: Image open with the Layers panel with Gemini model generating edits based on markups.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProdLoc/ps-ai-markup?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Use Prompt to edit with Gemini AI models and markup tools to guide AI edits.

Add a text prompt to describe any additional edits you want to apply to the image.

Select Generate to generate the edited image.

Tip

A temporary Brush Markup layer is created upon selecting the brush tool. This layer is deleted once you’ve finished adding your markups and selected Generate.

## 79. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-open-import-images/create-images/create-documents-using-presets.html

Select predefined dimension and setting templates to quickly start new projects.

Blank document presets provide ready-made templates with predefined dimensions, color modes, and resolutions optimized for specific use cases like web design, mobile interfaces, or print projects.

Blank document presets are empty canvases with preconfigured settings for common design scenarios. Unlike professional Stock templates that include design assets, presets provide only technical specifications such as dimensions, color mode, resolution, and background settings. Use them when you need standard canvas sizes for iPad Pro designs, social media graphics, or print materials without additional artwork.

Presets are organized into six categories: Photo, Print, Art & Illustration, Web, Mobile, and Film & Video. You can customize any preset's settings before creating your document and save frequently used configurations for quick access.

## Open the New Document dialog

The New Document dialog displays preset categories across the top, with blank document presets shown as thumbnails. The Recents tab shows recently accessed presets, while the Saved tab displays your custom presets.

## Create a document from a preset

Hover over preset thumbnails to see their dimensions and settings before selecting.

## Customize preset settings

Before creating your document, modify preset specifications in the Preset Details panel to match your exact requirements.

## Adjust dimensions and basic settings

Changing the color mode converts the default document profile to match your selection. For print projects, use CMYK mode with 300 ppi resolution. For web and screen designs, use RGB mode with 72 ppi resolution.

## Configure advanced options

## Save custom presets for reuse

After configuring settings that you use frequently, save them as a custom preset to streamline future projects.

## Create a saved preset

Access your saved presets from the Saved tab in the New Document dialog. You can customize saved presets further when creating new documents, but you must save any modifications with a different preset name to preserve the original.

## Distinguish presets from templates

Blank document presets differ from professional Stock templates in content and purpose. Presets provide empty canvases with technical specifications only dimensions, color mode, resolution, and background settings. Both presets and templates open as standard .psd documents, but templates typically include multiple artboards and design components. Learn how to [Create artboard documents](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/artboards.html) to work with multiple canvases in one document. To learn more about templates, [explore professional Stock templates](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/create-documents.html#search-for-templates-professional-stock).


====================================================================================================

## 80. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/get-started-layers/layers-overview.html

Layers let you separate different parts of an image into individual components, making it easier to edit, rearrange, and design complex compositions.

## What are layers in professional photoeditor?

Layers are like transparent sheets stacked on each other, with each sheet holding a different part of your image. They combine to create the whole picture, but you can change one sheet without affecting the others.

This allows you to test ideas, make changes, and improve your image without damaging the original.

## Types of layers

photoeditor offers various kinds of layers, each serving different purposes:

**Type****Usage**
Image layer Holds pixel-based content such as photos, artwork, or painted elements. The most common type of layer.
Adjustment Layer Applies edits like brightness, contrast, or color balance without permanently altering the image.
Fill Layer Adds a solid color, gradient, or pattern that can be updated or removed anytime.
Type layer Contains editable text that can be styled, transformed, and repositioned without losing quality.
Shape layer Contains vector-based graphics and can be scaled to any size without pixelation.
Smart Object Preserves original content, allowing non-destructive scaling, warping, or filter application.
Video Layer Holds video clips or animation frames that can be edited directly within the document.
Background layer Created by default in new documents. Locked initially but can be converted into a regular layer for editing.


====================================================================================================

Select the group folder and choose Layer>Ungroup Layers to ungroup layers.

Learn how to access, organize, and manage your layers using the Layers panel for efficient editing and organization in professional photoeditor.

The Layers panel visually represents all layers in your document, allowing you to select, hide, reorder, and modify layers without affecting other elements.

To access the panel, select Window>Layers.

To show or hide a layer, select the eye icon next to the layer thumbnail.

To change layer order, drag a layer up or down in the Layers panel.

To rename a layer, double-click the layer name, type a new one, and press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS).

To create a new layer, select the Create a new layer icon in the Layers panel.

## 83. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/get-started-layers/video-layers-overview.html

Video Layers let you play, edit, and animate video directly in professional photoeditor, combining motion with still image workflows.

Video Layers allow you to work with moving images just like still images. You can apply adjustment layers, masks, filters, or paint on individual frames. They let you add motion content to your designs or create frame-by-frame animations.

## How video layers work

*   Video Layers appear in the Layers panel, just like standard layers.
*   Each frame of a video layer can be edited independently, allowing for precise control over motion and effects.
*   Adjustment Layer affect all frames without altering the original video.
*   Masks and Filters can be used on video layers to create dynamic effects.
*   Videos can be mixed with still images, graphics, and text to create complex motion compositions.

## Types of video layers

Video Layer: Plays video content and allows you to scrub through frames, paint on individual frames, and apply an Adjustment Layer or a Filter.

Adjustment Layer: Applies color or tonal corrections across all frames non-destructively.

Blank Video Layer: Creates an empty timeline for hand-drawn or frame-by-frame animation, allowing complete control over each frame.

## 88. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/get-started-layers/sample-from-all-visible-layers.html

Learn about how sampling from all visible layers enhances your editing capabilities when using tools like Clone Stamp and Healing Brush.

## What is multi-layer sampling?

Multi-layer sampling involves sampling pixel data from all visible layers at once rather than a single layer. This approach gives you greater flexibility when retouching or colour-correcting complex projects. It also lets you pull information from the entire image while keeping each layer non-destructive and editable.

This option is handy when:

*   Color matching across multiple adjustment and image layers with the Eyedropper tool.
*   Retouching with the Healing Brush tool or Clone Stamp tool to blend corrections into the overall composition.

Selecting the All Layers option from the Sample dropdown in supported tools gives you more control over how colors and textures are sourced, making your edits more natural and efficient.


====================================================================================================

## 89. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/get-started-layers/rasterize-layers.html

Convert vector-based or Smart Object layers to pixel-based layers using Layer menu options.

Rasterizing layers gives you access to painting tools and filters that require pixel-based data. The process converts the vector or Smart Object data into a flat, pixel-based image at your current document resolution. When your document contains vector-based content such as type layers, shape layers, or Smart Objects, you'll need to rasterize them before you can paint directly on the layer or apply certain filters.

Once rasterized, you can't edit the vector properties or Smart Object source. The layer becomes permanently pixel based. Duplicate your layer before rasterizing for non-destructive editing. Learn more about preserving flexibility in [Smart Objects overview and benefits](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/smart-objects-overview-and-benefits.html).

## Rasterize selected layers

In the Layers panel, select the layer or layers you’d like to rasterize.

Select Layer>Rasterize, and then choose an option from the submenu.

The Rasterize submenu offers the following options:

*   Type: Rasterizes the text on a type layer without affecting other vector elements on the same layer.
*   Shape: Rasterizes a shape layer, converting vector paths to pixels.
*   Fill Content: Rasterizes the fill of a shape layer while preserving the vector mask.
*   Vector Mask: Rasterizes a vector mask to a layer mask, turning vector paths into pixel-based mask data.
*   Smart Object: Rasterizes a Smart Object to a standard raster layer, permanently applying any transformations or Smart Filters.
*   Video: Rasterizes the current video frame to a static image layer.
*   Layer: Rasterizes all vector and generated data on the selected layers.
*   All Layers: Rasterizes every layer in the document that contains vector or generated content.

To rasterize linked layers, select a linked layer, choose Layer>Select Linked Layers, and then rasterize the selection.


====================================================================================================

## 91. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/transform-manipulate-layers/select-layers.html

Learn how to select layers in photoeditor to edit, move, or transform them.

You can select one or multiple layers in photoeditor depending on the task. Some actions, like painting or tonal adjustments, apply only to the active layer, while others, such as moving, aligning, or transforming, can involve multiple layers. You can select layers from the Layers panel or directly in the document window. Unlike selected layers, linked layers remain linked even when the selection changes.

## Select layers in the Layers panel

Open the Layers panel.

Do one of the following:

*   To select a single layer: Select the layer in the Layers panel.
*   To select multiple contiguous layers: Select the first layer, then Shift+select the last layer.
*   To select multiple non-contiguous layers: Ctrl+select(Windows) or Command+select(macOS) each layer in the panel.
*   To select all layers: Select Select**>**All Layers.
*   To select similar layers (for example, all type layers): Select one layer, then choose Select**>**Similar Layers.
*   To deselect a layer: Ctrl+select**(Windows) or**Command+select**(macOS)** the layer.
*   To deselect all layers: Select Select**>**Deselect Layers or select below the bottom layer in the panel.

Note

When selecting, Ctrl+select (Windows) or Command+select (macOS) outside the layer thumbnail. Selecting the thumbnail itself selects the non-transparent pixels in that layer.

## Select layers in the document window

Select the Move**tool**.

Do one of the following:

*   In the Options bar, enable Auto Select**>**Layer, then select the content in the document. The top layer containing pixels under the cursor is selected.
*   In the Options bar, enable Auto Select**>**Group, then select the content in the document. The top group containing pixels under the cursor is selected. If you select an ungrouped layer, it becomes selected.
*   Right-click (Windows) or Control+click (macOS) in the image, then select a layer from the context menu.

## Select a layer in a group

In the Layers**panel**, select the group.

Select the drop-down triangle to expand the group.

![Image 1: Drop-down triangle is highlighted in the blue top layers group in Layers panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Drop-down-triangle-is-highlighted-in-the-blue-top-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Select the tiny triangle to expand the group.

![Image 2: Drop-down triangle is highlighted in the blue top layers group in Layers panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Drop-down-triangle-is-highlighted-in-the-blue-top-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Select the tiny triangle to expand the group.

Select the layer within the group.

## 96. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/transform-manipulate-layers/reorder-and-move-layers.html

Change the layer stacking order in the Layers panel or use the Move tool to reposition content on the canvas.

You can organize layers by changing their stacking order in the Layers panel or moving their content in the document. You can apply these techniques to regular layers and layer groups.

The background layer stays locked at the bottom unless you convert it to a regular layer. For more control over layer positioning and protection, you can [Lock layers to prevent changes](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/moving-stacking-locking-layers.html).

## Change the layer stacking order

In the Layers panel, select the layer or group you want to move.

Drag the layer or group up or down until a highlighted line appears at your desired position.

Release to place the layer in the new position.

### Use the menu command

Select the layer or group.

Choose Layer>Arrange and select a command:

*   Bring to Front - Moves the layer to the top of the stack.
*   Bring Forward - Moves the layer up one position.
*   Send Backward - Moves the layer down one position.
*   Send to Back - Moves the layer to the bottom (or just above the background layer).
*   Reverse - Reverses the order of multiple selected layers

If your layer is in a group, the Arrange commands apply only within that group.

## Move a layer into a group

Drag a layer to the group folder  icon in the Layers panel.

Release when the group folder is highlighted.

If the group is collapsed, the layer is placed at the top of the group.

## Reposition layer content in the document

In the Layers panel, select one or more layers containing the content you want to move.

Select the Move tool from the toolbar or press V.

Drag any object on the selected layers to a new position in the document window.

All content on the selected layers moves together.

To nudge layer content:

*   Press an arrow key to move content 1 pixel.
*   Press Shift + arrow key to move content 10 pixels

In the Move tool options bar, enable Auto Select and choose Layer to automatically select layers when their content is selected in the document. Choose Group to select entire layer groups. Shift-click to add layers to your selection.

Use [Transform and rotate layers](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/moving-stacking-locking-layers.html) to understand more details about rotating layers.


====================================================================================================

## 97. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/color-adjustment-fill-layers/adjustment-and-fill-layers-overview.html

Discover how adjustment and fill layers let you make color and tonal changes without permanently altering your original image pixels in professional photoeditor.

Adjustment and fill layers are powerful tools in photoeditor that allow you to make changes to your images while preserving the original pixel data. Unlike direct adjustments that permanently alter your image, these special layers store modification instructions separately from the image content.

### Adjustment layers

An adjustment layer applies color and tonal adjustments to your image without permanently changing pixel values. For example, rather than making a Levels or Curves adjustment directly to your image, you can create a Levels or Curves adjustment layer. The color and tonal adjustments are stored in the adjustment layer and apply to all the layers below it.

### Fill layers

Fill layers let you add solid colors, gradients, or patterns to your composition. Unlike adjustment layers, fill layers don't affect the appearance of layers underneath them.

## Types of adjustment layers

photoeditor offers several types of adjustment layers, each designed for specific editing tasks:

*   **Levels and Curves** - For adjusting tonal range and color balance.
*   **Hue/Saturation** - For modifying color intensity and overall color.
*   **Black & White** - For converting color images to monochrome with control over how colors translate to grayscale.
*   **Brightness/Contrast** - For simple lighting adjustments.
*   **Color Balance** - For adjusting color mixtures in shadows, midtones, and highlights.
*   **Selective Color** - For fine-tuning specific color ranges.

## Types of fill layers

Fill layers come in three varieties:

*   **Solid Color** - Fills the layer with a single color you select using the Color Picker.
*   **Gradient** - Creates a gradual blend between multiple colors in various patterns (linear, radial, etc.)
*   **Pattern** - Fills the layer with a repeating pattern from the photoeditor pattern libraries.


====================================================================================================

## 98. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/color-adjustment-fill-layers/work-with-adjustment-and-fill-layers.html

Create and control adjustment and fill layers for non-destructive edits, precise color refinement, and creative effects.

## Create an adjustment layer

In adjustment layers, apply color and tonal corrections (like brightness, contrast, or vibrance) without permanently changing pixel data.

Select File>Open to browse and select an image.

In the Layers panel, select Create new fill or adjustment layer and choose an option:

*   Color & Vibrance: Enhances muted colors
*   Brightness/Contrast: Lightens/darkens and increases contrast
*   Levels: Adjusts shadows, midtones, highlights
*   Curves: Finetunes tonal range with curve control
*   Exposure: Corrects exposure, offset, gamma
*   Hue/Saturation: Shifts colors, adjusts saturation
*   Color Balance: Changes overall color cast
*   Black & White: Converts to monochrome, adjusts tones
*   Photo Filter: Applies warming/cooling lens effects
*   Channel Mixer: Rebalances color channels
*   Color Lookup: Applies cinematic/creative looks
*   Selective Color: Adjusts specific color ranges
*   Invert: Reverses colors (negative effect)
*   Posterize: Reduces image to flat color levels
*   Threshold: Converts to high-contrast black and white
*   Gradient Map: Maps tones to gradient colors
*   Solid Color: Fills with a single color
*   Gradient: Fills with a smooth gradient blend
*   Pattern: Fills with a repeated design 

In the Properties panel, adjust the sliders to adjust the colors, tones, or other settings.

Name the layer, set layer options, and press Enter (or Return on macOS).

## 99. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/color-adjustment-fill-layers/use-layer-masks-to-target-adjustment-or-fill-layers.html

Learn to apply color and tonal adjustments to specific areas of your image by painting on an adjustment layer's mask, creating precise non-destructive edits in professional photoeditor.

Layer masks work on a simple principle: white reveals and black conceals. Gray areas in the mask create partial transparency, allowing for smooth transitions between adjusted and unadjusted areas. This makes layer masks incredibly versatile for creating subtle, targeted adjustments that enhance specific parts of your image while leaving others untouched.

Remember that you can always refine your masks later by selecting the mask thumbnail and painting with black, white, or gray. This non-destructive approach gives you the flexibility to experiment with different effects without permanently altering your original image.

## Create a mask from a selection

Make a selection of the area where you want to apply the adjustment or fill. Use any selection tool like the Marquee, Lasso, or Quick Selection tool.

Select Layer>New Adjustment Layer and select your adjustment type.

The adjustment layer is automatically created based on your selection. White areas (the selection) show the adjustment, while black areas (outside the selection) hide it.

Make your adjustment in the Properties panel. Refine the mask if needed by painting on it with black or white.

## Use Color Range to create a precise mask

Select the layer you want to apply an adjustment to.

Select Layer>New Fill Layer and select your adjustment type.

Select the Color Range in the Masks section of the Properties panel.

Select Sampled Colors from the Color Range dialog.

Select the colors in your image that you want to target with the adjustment and adjust the Fuzziness slider to control how many similar colors are included.

Select OK to create a mask based on the selected color range. Fine-tune the mask by painting with black or white if needed.

Name the layer, set layer options, and press Enter(or Return on macOS).

## 101. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/color-adjustment-fill-layers/merging-adjustment-or-fill-layers.html

Learn how to merge adjustment or fill layers in photoeditor and understand how merging affects your edits and file structure.

Adjustment and fill layers can be merged in different ways. You can merge them with the layer directly below, with all layers inside a group, with selected layers, or with all visible layers. However, an adjustment or fill layer cannot serve as the target layer for a merge.

When you merge an adjustment or fill layer with the layer beneath it, the adjustments are rasterized and permanently applied to that layer. Similarly, a fill layer can be rasterized without merging, giving you more control over when edits become permanent.

It's worth noting that adjustment and fill layers whose masks contain only white values don't significantly increase file size. Merging them purely to save space is unnecessary.

Presets are available in the Adjustments **panel**. Hover over a preset thumbnail to preview its effect on your image before applying it. Once used, you can refine the preset further by editing its adjustments in the Properties**panel**.

The Adjustments panel includes default presets for Portraits**,**Landscape**,**Photo Repair**,**Creative**,**Black & White**, and**Cinematic**images**. These offer a quick starting point for creating polished images while saving time in complex editing workflows.

When you hover over a preset, its name and preview appear on the canvas. Selecting a preset displays the effect group in the Layers**panel** for easy management.

You can also switch how presets are displayed:

*   **List View** for a detailed, text-based view.
*   **Grid View** for thumbnail previews.

Use the **icon** in the Adjustments panel to toggle between the two views.

## 113. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/layer-style-effects-and-options-overview.html

View and edit layer effects and styles using the Layer Style dialog in professional photoeditor.

Access the Layer Style dialog by double-clicking a layer (outside the layer name or thumbnail) or selecting the layer style icon in the Layers panel.

Select the checkbox beside each effect name to apply it with the current settings.

*   Styles: Select from preset styles. 
*   Blending Options: Adjust layer blending options. 

Create custom styles by combining these effects.

## Effects available

Altitude Sets the height of the light source (0 is ground level and 90 is directly above the layer)
Angle Determines the lighting angle at which the effect is applied to the layer
Anti-alias Blends the edge pixels of a contour or gloss contour
Blend Mode Determines how the layer style blends with the underlying layers, which may or may not include the active layer
Choke Shrinks the boundaries of the matte of Inner Shadow or Inner Glow prior to blurring
Color Specifies the color of a shadow, glow, or highlight
Contour Controls transparency rings in solid glows, gradient repetitions in gradient glows, surface detail in bevels/embossing, and fade in shadows
Distance Specifies the offset distance for a shadow or satin effect
Depth Specifies the depth of a bevel. It also specifies the depth of a pattern
Use Global Light Sets one master lighting angle shared across Drop Shadow, Inner Shadow, and Bevel & Emboss
Gloss Contour Creates a glossy, metallic appearance
Gradient Specifies the gradient of a layer effect
Highlight or Shadow Mode Specifies the blending mode of a bevel or emboss highlight or shadow
Jitter Varies the application of a gradient's color and opacity
Layer Knocks Out Drop Shadow Controls the drop shadow's visibility in a semi-transparent layer
Noise Specifies the number of random elements in the opacity of a glow or shadow
Opacity Sets the opacity of the layer effect
Pattern Specifies the pattern of a layer effect
Position Specifies the position of a stroke effect as Outside, Inside, or Center
Range Controls which portion or range of the glow is targeted for the contour
Size Specifies the radius and size of blur or the size of the shadow
Soften Blurs the results of shading to reduce unwanted artifacts
Source Specifies the source for an inner glow
Spread Expands the boundaries of the matte prior to blurring
Style Specifies the style of a bevel
Technique Bevel & Emboss offers Smooth, Chisel Hard, and Chisel Soft styles, while Inner Glow and Outer Glow offer Softer and Precise
Smooth Blurs the edges of a matte slightly and is useful for all types of mattes, whether their edges are soft or hard
Texture Applies a texture

photoeditor comes with numerous preset layer styles organized into libraries by function. Whether you need web buttons, text effects, or other creative styles, loading the appropriate library will save you time and enhance your workflow.

Layer styles are non-destructive effects linked to layer contents. When you move or edit the content of a layer, the same effects are automatically applied to the modified content.

## 118. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/manage-contours.html

Learn how to use the Contour Editor to customize layer style effects like shadows, glows, and bevels in professional photoeditor.

Layer styles in photoeditor allow you to add effects like shadows, glows, and bevels to your layers. While the default settings work well for many situations, you can create more sophisticated and unique effects by customizing how these effects blend and fade using contours.

Contours control how layer effects transition from opaque to transparent areas or how they blend from one color to another. By modifying contours, you can create custom shadows with unique falloff patterns, distinctive bevels with special lighting effects, or glows with non-standard fading characteristics.

## Access and modify contours

## Understand contour applications

*   For Drop Shadow and Inner Shadow, the contour controls the opacity falloff of the shadow.
*   For Outer Glow and Inner Glow, the contour determines how the glow fades from its origin.
*   For Bevel & Emboss, the contour shapes how the highlight and shadow transition across the surface.
*   For Satin, the contour affects how the effect wraps around the edges of your layer content.

## 119. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/set-a-global-lighting-angle-for-all-layers.html

Learn how to configure a unified lighting direction that automatically applies to layer effects in professional photoeditor.

When creating designs with several layer effects in photoeditor, maintaining a consistent light source creates a more realistic and cohesive appearance. The global lighting feature ensures that shadows and highlights across different layers appear to come from the same direction.

Global lighting applies to layer effects that use directional lighting, such as Drop Shadow, Inner Shadow, and Bevel and Emboss. When you turn on global lighting for these effects, changing the angle in one location updates it everywhere, saving time and ensuring visual consistency throughout your design.

## Set global lighting from a layer effect

## 120. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/scale-layer-effects.html

Learn how to adjust the size of layer effects like shadows, glows, and bevels proportionally to maintain visual balance in professional photoeditor.

When you resize layers with effects applied, the effects may appear disproportionate to the new layer size. photoeditor allows you to scale these effects to maintain visual harmony in your design.

Layer effects are linked to layer contents and automatically update when you edit the layer. However, when you transform or resize a layer, the effects don't automatically scale with it. The Scale effect solves this problem by proportionally adjusting all effects in a layer style.

Select any layer with the effects you want to scale in the Layers panel.

Double-click the Add a layer style icon next to the layer name to open the Layer Style dialog.

Drag the Scale slider to adjust the scale percentage of all effects simultaneously..

Select OK.

When working with text layers, consider converting the text to a Smart Object before scaling. This preserves text quality while allowing you to scale both the text and its effects together.

Learn how to use various techniques to remove individual effects or all layer styles from selected layers without affecting the underlying content in professional photoeditor.

Layer effects remain linked to layer contents, automatically updating when you modify the layer. This non-destructive approach makes it easy to experiment with different styles while preserving your original work. When you no longer need these effects, you can remove them individually or all at once without affecting the underlying content.

## Remove individual layer effects

Select Layer>Layer Style>Clear Layer Style from the main menu.

Select the visibility  icon next to the Effects section for your layer.

## 123. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/convert-layer-styles-to-image-layers.html

Learn how to transform layer styles into regular image layers to customize effects or apply additional editing techniques in professional photoeditor.

Convert layer styles to image layers to customize or fine-tune the appearance of effects by treating them as regular layers. This technique is useful when you want to apply additional filters, paint directly on an effect, or modify effects in ways not possible through the Layer Style dialog.

Select the layer containing the layer style you want to convert in the Layers panel.

Select Layer>Layer Style>Create Layers. photoeditor converts each effect into a separate layer, grouped with the original layer. You can now edit these new layers just like any regular image layer.

Once you convert layer styles to image layers, the effects are no longer linked to the original layer content. If you edit the original layer, the effect layers won't automatically update as they would with layer styles.

## 124. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/create-texture-effects-with-knockout.html

Learn how to use the Knockout blending option to punch through layers and reveal underlying content for texture effects in photoeditor desktop.

The Knockout effect uses one layer to cut through other layers, revealing content beneath. Use it to add texture to text, enhance shapes, or show original colors through adjustment layers.

You'll need three elements: a top layer that defines the knockout shape, middle layers to punch through, and underlying content to reveal. Unlike layer masks, which hide content, Knockout removes visibility from intermediate layers.

## Arrange layers for the knockout effect

## Apply the Knockout blending option

You can modify the shape, adjust blend modes, or change Fill Opacity anytime. For more information about blending modes with layer groups and clipping masks, see [Group Blend Effects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/layer-opacity-blending.html#group_blend_effects).


====================================================================================================

## 125. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/apply-layer-effects/set-layer-opacity-and-blending-modes.html

Adjust transparency levels and choose blend modes to control how layers interact with underlying content.

Layer opacity and blending modes control how pixels on one layer interact with pixels on layers beneath. Opacity determines transparency level from fully opaque at 100% to nearly invisible at 1%. Blending modes use mathematical formulas to combine pixel values, creating effects ranging from subtle color shifts to dramatic composites.

Use these controls when compositing images, creating special effects, or fine-tuning adjustments. Opacity works on any layer type, while fill opacity affects only the layer content without changing layer effects like drop shadows. Opacity option will be active for regular and inactive for Background layers and locked layers. [learn how to convert background layers](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/layer-basics.html#convert_background_and_layers).

The Layers panel provides quick access to both opacity controls and the blending mode menu. Changes appear immediately on the canvas, letting you preview results as you adjust values.

## Adjust overall layer opacity

Select one or more layers or groups in the Layers panel.

In the Layers panel, locate the Opacity field in the upper-right area.

Enter a value between 1 and 100, or drag the slider to adjust transparency.

Overall opacity affects everything on the layer, including any layer effects you've applied. Lower values make the entire layer more transparent, revealing more of the layers beneath.

## Adjust fill opacity

Select a layer in the Layers panel.

Locate the Fill field directly below the Opacity control.

Enter a value between 0 and 100, or drag the slider to adjust the fill transparency.

Fill opacity affects only pixels, shapes, or text on the layer while preserving the full opacity of layer effects like drop shadows and strokes. This separation gives you precise control when combining layer content with styled effects. Fill opacity is not available for layer groups, only the overall Opacity control appears when a group is selected.

## Set a blending mode

Select a layer or group from the Layers panel.

Select the Blend Mode dropdown menu at the top of the Layers panel.

Scroll through the options to preview how each mode affects your image.

Choose the blending mode that achieves your desired effect.

Blending modes determine how pixel values on the active layer mathematically combine with pixels on underlying layers. The default mode is Normal, which displays pixels without blending calculations. [Explore all blending modes and their effects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/blending-modes.html) to understand which modes work best for darkening, lightening, adjusting contrast, or comparing pixel values.

For layer groups, the default blending mode is Pass Through, which means the group has no blending properties of its own, layers within the group blend individually with layers below. When you choose a different mode for a group, all layers in that group composite first, then the entire group blends as a single image with the rest of the document. This changes the stacking order of how blending calculations are applied.

When creating a new layer, select Fill With Mode-Neutral Color in the New Layer dialog box to fill the layer with a preset neutral color based on the chosen blending mode. This option is essential for filters like Lighting Effects that require pixel data to function. The neutral color remains invisible until you apply effects. This option is unavailable for layers using Normal, Dissolve, Hard Mix, Hue, Saturation, Color, or Luminosity modes.

Learn more about [advanced blending options](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/layer-opacity-blending.html#group_blend_effects) for knockout effects, channel exclusion, and tonal range blending with Blend If sliders,.


====================================================================================================

## 126. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/smart-objects-overview-and-benefits.html

#### Learn to use professional photoeditor to edit layers without losing image quality and easily update linked or embedded files.

Smart Objects store image data from various image types, including those created in professional Illustrator or photoeditor. They preserve the original image content and quality, allowing you to modify it without permanently altering it.

You can [embed](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-embedded-smart-objects.html) an image directly into a document or create [Linked Smart Objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-linked-smart-objects.html) that reference an external file. When the source file changes, photoeditor automatically [updates the linked content](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/update-linked-smart-objects.html). Linked Smart Objects differ from duplicate instances in the same document. With linked objects, you can share one source file across multiple photoeditor documents, which many web designers find familiar and useful.

## Advantages of Smart Objects

*   **Resize or reshape without losing quality**: Scale, rotate, skew, distort, apply perspective, or warp a layer without losing image quality because these changes don’t affect the original content. 
*   **Keep vector artwork sharp**: Use vector data, such as Illustrator artwork, which would otherwise be converted to pixels in photoeditor.
*   **Edit filters without losing quality**: Apply filters to preserve the original image and edit them whenever you need.
*   **Update all linked copies**: Edit one Smart Object to automatically update all linked instances.
*   **Use flexible layer masks**: Apply a linked or unlinked layer mask to the Smart Object layer.
*   **Test with placeholders**: Try various designs with low-resolution placeholder images, then replace them with final versions.
*   **Reduce file size with Linked Smart Objects:** Linked Smart Objects link to an external file rather than embedding it, which can make documents smaller. This helps keep projects lighter and faster, especially when using large images.  

When you adjust a Smart Object with a Smart Filter, photoeditor temporarily turns off the filter effect and reapplies it after the change.

## Limitations of Smart Objects

*   **Avoid converting to pixels**: You can’t paint, dodge, burn, or clone on a Smart Object layer until you change it to pixels. To work around this, edit its contents, add a new layer over it, duplicate the object, or create a layer for pixel-based edits.
*   **Watch file sizes**: High-resolution images or multiple linked assets in Smart Objects can increase file size and slow down large projects.
*   **Expect variable file savings:** Linked Smart Objects store a flattened and scaled version of the image for display, and in some cases, this data can be larger than the original file, making any space savings negligible.


====================================================================================================

## 127. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-embedded-smart-objects.html

#### Learn how to use professional photoeditor to convert layers to Smart Objects and edit them without changing the original image.

You can use JPEGs as [Smart Objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/smart-objects-overview-and-benefits.html), but PSD, TIFF, or PSB files are better. They keep layers editable and maintain quality. However, JPEGs flatten layers and compress every time you save, which can lower image quality.

Do one of the following to create an embedded Smart Object:

*   To import files as Smart Objects into an open photoeditor document:Select File>Place Embedded.
*   To create a new file with the Smart Object as the first layer: Select File>Open As Smart Object.
*   To convert a selected layer into a Smart Object: Select Layer>Smart Objects>Convert to Smart Object.
*   To select one or more layers and bundle them into one Smart Object:Select Layer>Smart Objects>Convert To Smart Object.
*   To add vector artwork or objects while keeping them editable:Drag PDF or professional Illustrator layers or objects into a photoeditor document.
*   To keep Illustrator artwork editable in photoeditor:Paste it into the document, select Edit>Paste  
 and select Smart Object in the Paste dialog.
*   To import a file as a Smart Object into an open photoeditor document in Bridge:Select File>Place>In photoeditor.

For best results, select Preferences>File Handling & Clipboard in Illustrator, and select PDF and AICB (No Transparency Support) before copying.

Double-click the Smart Object thumbnail in the Layers panel to [edit its contents](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/edit-the-contents-of-a-smart-object.html).

When you save your changes, photoeditor automatically updates every instance of that Smart Object in your document.


====================================================================================================

## 128. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-linked-smart-objects.html

#### Learn how to link external files to your document in professional photoeditor to reuse assets and update sources automatically.

Open or create a photoeditor document.

Select File>Place Linked.

Select the file you want to use as a linked Smart Object.

Right-click and select Place.

The linked Smart Object is created and displayed in the Layers panel with a link icon.

![Image 1: The photoeditor workspace shows green hills and sky. In the Layers panel, the linked GreenGrass Smart Object layer is selected.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-photoeditor-workspace-shows-a-landscape-of-green?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Double-click the link icon to open and edit the linked Smart Object.

Hold Alt-key (Windows) or option-key (macOS) and drag a file into a document to create a linked Smart Object automatically. Turn off Preferences>General>Always Create Smart Objects While Placing to place files as regular layers.


====================================================================================================

## 129. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/update-linked-smart-objects.html

#### Learn how to keep your documents in professional photoeditor updated automatically when linked source files change.

Linked Smart Objects update automatically when the photoeditor document is open, provided the source file has changed. If the document is closed, photoeditor highlights the affected Smart Objects in the Layers panel when you reopen the photoeditor document.

Open your photoeditor document. Linked Smart Objects whose source images have changed are visually highlighted in the Layers panel.

*   Right-click the highlighted linked Smart Object layer and select Update Modified Content to update only this smart object.
*   Select Layer>Smart Objects>Update All Modified Content to update every linked Smart Object in the document at once.

![Image 1: The Layers panel is open and is showing a Smart Object with a yellow warning icon, meaning the linked file has changed and needs an update. The yellow warning icon and the update commands are highlighted. ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Layers-panel-is-open-and-is-showing-a-Smart-Ob?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Select the Smart Object layer and update one or all together.

photoeditor only checks the file directly linked to the Smart Object. A linked file won't update automatically if nested inside another Smart Object.

The Layers panel flags Linked Smart Objects with missing source files. To fix the link, right-click the highlighted layer icon, select Relink to File, browse the file’s new location, and select Place.

![Image 2: The Layers panel is open and is showing a Smart Object with a red missing file icon, meaning the source file can’t be found and must be relinked. The missing file icon and the relink command are highlighted.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Layers-panel-is-open-and-is-showing-a-Smart-Ob_578724?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1380)

Locate the missing source file and relink it to restore your Smart Object.


====================================================================================================

## 130. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/view-linked-smart-object-properties.html

#### Learn how to access Smart Object details, including file path, dimensions, and position, in professional photoeditor.

[Linked Smart Objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-linked-smart-objects.html) in photoeditor maintain connections to external source files, allowing updates to be reflected across all documents. Viewing these properties helps you manage links and perform everyday actions.

Select Window>Layers.

Select the Linked Smart Object layer in the Layers panel.

Select Window>Properties to open the Properties panel.

Review the linked Smart Object properties displayed in the panel.

![Image 1: A photoeditor document shows a selected Smart Object layer with link, size, and position details in the Properties panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-photoeditor-document-is-open-and-the-Layers-panel-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Manage the Smart Object’s settings to maintain a consistent layout.

The Properties panel shows the file path of the external source, the Smart Object’s size, and its position (X, Y) in the document.

From the Properties panel, you can edit the source image (photoeditor will open the right app, such as professional Illustrator for AI files) or embed the linked Smart Object into your document.

## 131. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/embed-linked-smart-objects.html

#### Learn how to convert linked Smart Objects to embedded ones in professional photoeditor to make your document self-contained and portable.
[Embedded objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/create-embedded-smart-objects.html) ensure that your document remains intact even if the external source files are moved or deleted, as the complete file data is stored within your photoeditor document.

Select Window>Layers.

Select the Linked Smart Object layer in the Layers panel, right-click, and select Embed Linked to embed a single linked smart object.

![Image 1: The Layers panel shows a selected linked Smart Object layer with the Embed Linked option highlighted in the menu.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Layers-panel-is-open-with-a-Linked-Smart-Objec?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1380)

Apply updates from the source file directly to your document.

Optionally, select Layer>Smart Objects>Embed All Linked to embed all Linked Smart Objects in the photoeditor document.  
 After embedding, the Smart Object becomes part of your photoeditor document, and the link icon disappears from the layer thumbnail in the Layers panel.

![Image 2: The Layers panel shows the same selected layer without a link icon, confirming it’s now an Embedded Smart Object.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Layers-panel-is-open-with-the-same-layer-still?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1380)

View the Smart Object restored after reconnecting the missing file.

## 132. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/package-linked-smart-objects.html

Learn how to store your professional photoeditor document and linked files in one folder to stay organized and avoid losing external links.

Select File>Package.

Select a location to save the package.  
photoeditor saves your document with all linked source, audio, and video files in one folder.

Open the new folder to review your packaged document and its source files.

photoeditor first looks for linked files in their last-known relative location. If the file isn't found, it checks the last-known absolute location, a saved alias (available on macOS only), and then the same folder as the document.

## Relink Smart Objects

This process helps you move, copy, or share project folders without breaking links. When you open a photoeditor document with a missing source file, photoeditor automatically opens the relink dialog box and shows the last-known absolute path.

Select Relink in the dialog box that appears.

Browse to the file’s new location.

Select Place to confirm the file.

Select OK to restore the link.

![Image 1: The Relink dialog box shows the missing file’s last-known path with Relink, OK, and Cancel options visible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Relink-dialog-box-is-open--displaying-the-last?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Restore missing links with a few quick steps.

## 133. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/convert-embedded-smart-objects-to-linked.html

#### Learn how to make Smart Objects reference external files in professional photoeditor without losing existing transformations, filters, or effects.

[Smart Objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/smart-objects-overview-and-benefits.html) protect the original image from damage caused by editing. Changing an embedded Smart Object to a linked file preserves your edits, lowers file size, and makes it easier to share, reuse, and update across projects or with collaborators.

Select the embedded Smart Object layer to convert in the photoeditor Layers panel.

Right-click the layer and select Convert to Linked.

![Image 1: Smart Object layer is selected in the Layers panel, where the option Convert to Linked is highlighted in the context menu.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Smart-Object-layer-is-selected-in-the-Layers-panel?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Identify and select the Smart Object layer you want to convert.

Select a location on your computer to save the source file.

Enter a file name and include a supported extension, such as JPG or PSD.

Select Save.

photoeditor creates a separate source file and links it to your document. The Smart Object layer shows a link icon in the Layers panel, confirming the conversion.

![Image 2: Layers panel showing a selected Smart Object layer with a chain-link icon, indicating it’s a linked Smart Object.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Layers-panel-showing-a-selected-Smart-Object-layer?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Store the Smart Object externally to keep it linked to your document.

## 134. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/filter-the-layers-panel-by-smart-objects.html

Learn how to easily identify Smart Objects in professional photoeditor, whether they’re linked, embedded, missing, or out of sync, without digging through every layer.

Filtering keeps things simple, helping you focus and remove clutter in the Layers panel. It uses unique icons, making it easy to identify the type of Smart Object used in each layer.

In the Layers panel, select Smart Object from the filtering dropdown menu.

![Image 1: Layers panel with Smart Object filter activated, displaying icons for linked, embedded, missing, and out-of-date objects.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Layers-panel-with-Smart-Object-filter-activated--d?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Spot different Smart Object types with visual cues.

Select one of the following filter icons:

*   Filter for smart objects linked in library: Shows Smart Objects linked from Creative Cloud Libraries.

*   Filter for smart objects linked locally: Shows Smart Objects linked to files stored on your computer.
*   Filter for linked smart objects that are out of date: Shows Smart Objects that are not in sync with their source files.
*   Filter for linked smart objects that are missing: Shows Smart Objects whose source files can’t be found.
*   Filter for embedded smart objects: Shows Smart Objects stored inside the photoeditor document.

Use Turn layer filtering on or off to turn filtering on or off.

## 135. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/duplicate-an-embedded-smart-object.html

Learn how to make a copy of a Smart Object in professional photoeditor and decide if you want it to stay connected to the original or work independently.

Select Window>Layers.

In the Layers panel, select the Smart Object layer you want to copy.

To create a linked copy, select Layer>New>Layer Via Copy. When you edit the original, the copy updates too.

To create an independent copy, select Layer>Smart Objects>New Smart Object Via Copy. When you edit the original, your independent copy remains unchanged.  
 A new Smart Object appears in the Layers panel with copy added to its name.

![Image 1: The Layers panel shows the original, linked, and independent Smart Objects, along with the document background.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Layers-panel-shows-the-original--linked--and-i?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Create duplicates to reuse or edit Smart Objects in different ways.

## 136. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/edit-the-contents-of-a-smart-object.html

Learn how to modify the source file directly, save it, and have professional photoeditor update the Smart Object in your document.

Editing a Smart Object allows you to refine artwork, raw files, or linked logos without compromising quality, as all changes are reversible. When you save the source file, photoeditor automatically updates all linked instances to maintain consistency in your design.

Select Window>Layers.

Select the Smart Object layer in the Layers panel.

Select Layer>Smart Objects>Edit Contents.

Select OK, if a dialog box appears.

Edit the source file in the application that opens.   
Smart Objects open in the application that matches their format, such as photoeditor for PSD files or Illustrator for AI files.

Save the file to update the Smart Object in your photoeditor document.

The Smart Object in your photoeditor document updates with the saved changes, and all linked instances stay in sync.

## 137. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/replace-the-contents-of-a-smart-object.html

#### Learn how to update the source inside a Smart Object in professional photoeditor without losing the edits you’ve already made.

photoeditor preserves the original image and applies your changes when you replace content. This makes it easy to update a design with new artwork or swap a placeholder without having to start over.

Select Window>Layers.

Select the Smart Object in the Layers panel.

Right-click the layer and select Replace Contents.

![Image 1: A Smart Object layer is selected in the Layers panel. The Replace Contents option is highlighted in the attached context menu.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-Smart-Object-layer-is-selected-in-the-Layers-pan?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Quickly switch a file without losing your adjustments.

The new file replaces the original, retaining all transformations intact.
