## 202. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/automatic-color-based-selections/detect-subject-using-select-subject.html

Detect and select the most prominent subject in your image using Select Subject in professional photoeditor.

Select the main subject in your image.

Select Select Subject from the Options bar.

Select Add to selection or Subtract from selection to refine the selection.

Select Select and Mask to fine-tune the selection using the options in the Properties panel.

*   Use Select Subject to automatically select all main subjects in your image.
*   Use the Object Selection tool to select a specific object when the image contains multiple subjects.


====================================================================================================

## 204. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/automatic-color-based-selections/select-areas-by-color-with-the-magic-wand-tool.html

Use the Magic Wand tool to select areas of similar color without tracing edges in photoeditor.

Select the Magic Wand tool from the toolbar.

In the Options bar, choose one of the following selection modes:

*   Add to selection 
*   Subtract from selection 
*   Intersect with selection 

Adjust the following settings in the options bar:

*   Sample Size: Define how much of the image is sampled to influence selection sensitivity.
*   Tolerance: Set the range of the selected color values from 0 (narrow) to 255 (broad).
*   Anti-alias: Enable to create smoother edges.
*   Contiguous: Enable to select only adjacent areas of similar color.
*   Sample All Layers: Enable to base the selection on all visible layers.

Select the color area in your image to make a selection based on the defined settings.

Select Select and Mask to further refine or preview the selection as a mask.


====================================================================================================

## 205. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/freehand-selections/create-quick-selections-with-selection-brush-tool.html

Use the Selection Brush tool to paint selections with precision in professional photoeditor.

Select the Selection Brush**tool** from the toolbar.

Brush over the area on the canvas to create a selection overlay.

![Image 1: An image shows a person with flowers in their hair. The Selection Brush tool highlights their nose with a red overlay.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/An-image-shows-a-person-with-flowers-in-their-hair?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

A red color overlay will be displayed as the selection overlay.

Select the settings icon in the Options bar and choose a color from the Color dropdown to change the overlay color.

Set Opacity, Size, and Hardness of the brush in the Options bar.

Toggle between Add brush and Subtract brush modes to refine your selection.


====================================================================================================

## 207. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/freehand-selections/draw-freeform-segments-of-a-selection-border.html

Learn how to use the Lasso tool to draw and refine freeform selection borders in professional photoeditor.

Select the Lasso tool from the toolbar.

Enter a Feather value in the Options bar to soften the selection edges.

Select the Anti-alias checkbox to create smoother selection edges.

Select Add to selection, Subtract from selection, or Intersect with selection from the Options bar.

Drag freely to create a freehand selection border.

Hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) and select to set start and end points for straight-edged segments.

Release the mouse button to close the selection border.


====================================================================================================

## 208. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/freehand-selections/draw-straight-edged-segments-of-a-selection-border.html

Learn to use the Polygonal Lasso tool to draw precise, straight-edged selections in professional photoeditor.

Select the Polygonal Lasso tool from the toolbar.

Select a selection mode from the Options bar.

Type a Feather value and select the Anti-alias checkbox to smooth selection edges.

Select on the canvas to set the starting point of your selection.

*   To draw a straight segment, select again where you want the segment to end.
*   To constrain a segment to a 45° angle, hold Shift and select the next point.
*   To draw a freehand segment, hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) and drag.
*   To undo the last segment, press the Delete key.

To close the selection,

*   Select at the starting point when the circular icon appears over Polygonal Lasso icon.
*   Double-click or press Ctrl+click (Windows) or Command+click (macOS) to close the selection if you're not at the starting point.


====================================================================================================

## 209. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/freehand-selections/snap-to-image-edges-using-magnetic-lasso-tool.html

Learn to use the Magnetic Lasso tool in professional photoeditor to snap selection borders to the edges of clearly defined areas in an image.

The Magnetic Lasso tool in photoeditor lets you create precise selections with ease. As you trace along an object’s edge, the tool snaps the selection border to defined areas, making it useful for complex outlines against high-contrast backgrounds. This tool isn’t available for 32-bit images.

Select the Magnetic Lasso tool from the toolbar.

Select one of the selection modes in the Options bar.

![Image 1: Options bar displays selection modes and the Current Tool Only checkbox is selected.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Options-bar-displays-selection-modes-and-the-Curre?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Select the Current Tool Only checkbox.

Set the following settings in the Options bar.

*   Feather: Soften the edges of a selection by gradually fading it into the surrounding pixels.
*   Anti-alias: Smooth the selection boundary by blending the edge pixels with the background.
*   Width**:** Define how far the tool detects edges from the pointer.
*   Contrast**:** Adjust the tool’s sensitivity to edge differences in the image.
*   Frequency**:** Control how often fastening points are automatically placed.

Select Use tablet pressure to change pen width to reduce edge width with increased pen pressure when using a stylus.

Select in the image to set the first fastening point, then drag along the edge you want to trace.

Select to add fastening points manually if the border doesn't snap to the desired edge.

Press Delete to remove recently added segments or fastening points if needed.

Close the selection border by selecting back at the starting point when the circular icon appears. If you're not at the starting point, double-click or press Ctrl + click (Windows) or Command + click (macOS) to close the selection.

Press Caps Lock to change the lasso pointer so that it indicates the lasso width. You can change the pointer while the tool is selected, but not in use. Press the right bracket ]key to increase the Magnetic Lasso edge width by 1 pixel and press the left bracket [key to decrease the width by 1 pixel.

On an image with well-defined edges, try a higher width and contrast setting and trace the border roughly. For softer edges, use a lower width and contrast and trace more precisely.


====================================================================================================

## 210. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/freehand-selections/save-skin-tones-settings-as-a-preset.html

Learn to save and reuse Skin Tones settings as a preset in professional photoeditor.

Select Select**>**Color Range.

In the Color Range dialog, select Skin Tones from the Select menu.

Select Detect Faces for more accurate results.

Adjust the Fuzziness slider or enter a value.

Set the Display option to Selection and select a Selection Preview to view your selections.

Select Save.

In the Save window, type a file name for the preset, and then select Save.

## Load a Skin Tones preset

Select Select**>**Color Range.

In the Color Range dialog, select Load.

In the Load window, select the preset file you want.

Select Load.


====================================================================================================

## 211. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/move-selection-or-selection-border.html

Learn how to move a selection, adjust its border, hide or show selection edges, and invert a selection in photoeditor.

Select the Move tool from the toolbar.

Move the pointer inside the selection border, then drag the selection to a new position.If multiple areas are selected, they all move together.

## Move a selection border

Using any selection tool, select New Selection in the Options bar.

Position the pointer inside the selection border (the pointer changes to indicate movement).

Drag the border to enclose a different area.

*   You can drag partly beyond the canvas. When brought back, the original border reappears intact.
*   You can also drag the border to another image window.

You can apply geometric transformations to reshape the border.

## Control the movement of a selection

*   Hold Shift while dragging to constrain movement to 45° increments.
*   Press an **arrow key** to move by 1 pixel.
*   Hold Shift and press an arrow key to move by 10 pixels.

## Hide or show selection edges

*   Select View**>**Extras to toggle selection edges, grids, guides, and other extras.
*   Select View**>**Show**>**Selection Edges to toggle only the selection edges. Edges reappear with a new selection.

## Select the unselected parts of an image

Select the background with the Magic Wand**tool****.**

Choose Select**>**Inverse to select the object instead.


====================================================================================================

## 212. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/refine-your-selection-and-mask.html

Learn how to refine selections in photoeditor using View Modes and refinement methods for greater accuracy and control.

The Properties panel provides multiple settings to adjust visibility, precision, and refinement methods for more accurate results.

## View Mode settings

*   **View Mode**: Choose how your selection is displayed.
*   **Onion Skin**: Displays the selection as an onion skin overlay against the background, with adjustable transparency.
*   **Marching Ants**: Shows the selection border as marching ants.
*   **Overlay**: Displays the unselected areas in a transparent color overlay (default is red).
*   **On Black**: Places the selection on a black background.
*   **On White**: Places the selection on a white background.
*   **Black and White**: Displays the selection as a black and white mask.
*   **On Layers**: Surrounds the selection with transparency.
*   **Show Edge**: Displays the area of refinement.
*   **Show Original**: Displays the original selection before refinement.
*   **High Quality Preview**: Renders a more accurate preview of changes but may affect performance.
*   **Transparency/Opacity**: Adjusts the visibility of the selection against its background.
*   **Real-time Refinement**: Updates the preview while brushing with refinement tools. When deselected, results appear after releasing the mouse button.
*   Shortcuts: Press F to cycle through modes or X to temporarily turn off all modes.

## Refine modes

*   **Color Aware**: Best for high-contrast or straightforward backgrounds.
*   **Object Aware**: Best for refining selections of hair, fur, or objects against complex backgrounds.


====================================================================================================

## 213. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/hover-layer-bounds-in-the-move-tool.html

Use hover layer bounds in the Move toolExplore more about how to preview and customize layer boundaries on the canvas with hover layer bounds in professional photoeditor.

## Hover Layer Bounds in photoeditor

Hover layer bounds let you preview the boundaries of an object as you move your pointer over it on the canvas. This helps you quickly identify and select layers without selecting first.

When the boundary outline appears, the corresponding layer is highlighted in the Layers panel. For layers with pixel or vector masks, photoeditor shows both the visible and hidden layer data. Hover layer bounds respond to your chosen Move tool setting in Auto-Select:

*   Layer displays the outline of individual layer objects.
*   Group displays the outline of layer groups.

You can also trigger hover layer bounds by hovering over layers in the Layers panel. For overlapping layers, view additional layer boundaries that share the same pixel coordinates by pressing the [ or ] keys. You can change these shortcuts in Edit**>**Keyboard Shortcuts**>**Keyboard Shortcuts and Menus dialog box.

Once the boundaries are visible, you can select or drag to interact with that layer or group.

## Customizing Hover Layer Bounds

You can adjust how hover layer bounds appear and behave:

*   Change boundary color, thickness, and the Layers panel highlight color in photoeditor >Settings**>**Guides, Grid & Slices.
*   Show or hide hover layer boundaries and panel highlights from the Set additional Move tool options of the Move tool in the Options bar.
*   Choose whether selecting a layer from the canvas automatically expands layer groups from the Set additional Move tool options of the Move tool in the Options bar.


====================================================================================================

## 214. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/copy-and-paste-selections.html

Learn to copy and paste selections to duplicate and move image content in professional photoeditor.

You can copy and paste selections within or between images using the Move  tool or commands like Copy, Copy Merged, Cut, and Paste. Dragging with the Move tool avoids using the clipboard, saving memory.

## Copy a selection

Select the area you want to copy.

Select Edit>Copy or Edit>Copy Merged.

## Copy a selection while dragging

Select the Move tool from the toolbar.

Hold down Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) and drag the selection you want to copy.

If nothing is selected, the entire active layer is copied. As you drag the selection over another image window, a border highlights the window if you can drop the selection into it.

## Paste a selection

Select Edit>Copy or Edit>Copy Merged.

Select Edit>Paste to paste the copied selection as a new layer in the destination image.

When pasting between images of different resolutions, the pasted content retains its pixel dimensions, which may cause size mismatches. To fix this, match the image resolutions beforehand or use Free Transform to resize.


====================================================================================================

## 215. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/create-multiple-copies-of-a-selection-within-an-image.html

Learn more about how to create multiple copies of a selection within an image in professional photoeditor.

Select the Move  tool from the toolbar.

Hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) and drag the selection to copy it.

Hold Alt or Option and press an arrow key to copy the selection and offset it by 1 pixel.

Hold Alt+Shift (Windows) or Option+Shift (macOS) and press an arrow key to copy the selection and offset it by 10 pixels.

As long as you hold down Alt or Option, each press of an arrow key creates another copy, offset from the last duplicate on the same layer.


====================================================================================================

## 216. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/paste-one-selection-into-or-outside-another.html

Learn how to paste one selection into or outside another using layer masks in professional photoeditor.

Cut or copy the part of the image you want to paste.

In the same image or another, select the area you want to paste into or outside. If pasting outside, select an area smaller than the copied selection.

Select Edit>Paste Special>Paste Into to place the source selection within the destination selection, or Edit>Paste Special>Paste Outside to place it around the destination selection.

Select the Move tool from the Tools panel, or hold down Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) to temporarily activate it, and drag the source contents until the desired area appears through the mask.

Select the layer mask thumbnail in the Layers panel and use a painting tool to edit the mask: paint with black to hide more of the underlying image, white to reveal more, or gray for partial transparency.

Once you are satisfied with the results, merge the new layer and mask with the underlying layer by selecting Layer>Merge Down.


====================================================================================================

## 217. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/delete-or-cut-selected-pixels.html

Delete or cut selected pixelsLearn more about how to delete or cut selected pixels in photoeditor.

Select Edit>Clear or press Backspace (Windows) or Delete (macOS) to remove the selection.

Select Edit>Cut to remove the selection and place it on the clipboard.

Note

Deleting on a background layer replaces the selection with the background color, while deleting on a standard layer replaces it with transparency.


====================================================================================================

## 218. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/control-the-movement-of-a-selection.html

Learn to control selection movement with constrained angles and pixel-precise nudging in professional photoeditor.

Select the Move tool to reposition your selection on the canvas.

Choose the Select tool and then select the area you want to move.

Select the Move tool to reposition your selection on the canvas. To move the selected area, do one of the following:

*   Constrain the direction to multiples of 45° by beginning to drag, then holding Shift while you continue to drag.
*   Move the selection in 1-pixel increments by pressing an arrow key.
*   Move the selection in 10-pixel increments by holding Shift and pressing an arrow key.

## 223. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/refine-and-soften-selection-edges.html

Learn to soften harsh selection edges in professional photoeditor for effortless blending.

The Select and Mask workspace allows you to refine and improve selection edges for greater precision. This option allows you to extract objects more cleanly and refine layer masks. Selecting Select and Mask from the options bar or from Select**>**Select and Mask gives you access to dedicated tools for refining edges.

In addition to Select and Mask, photoeditor offers two key techniques to soften selection boundaries: **anti-aliasing**and**feathering**. These methods help achieve smoother, more natural transitions between selections and surrounding pixels.

## Anti-aliasing

Anti-aliasing smooths jagged edges by softening the color transition between edge pixels and the background. Only the edge pixels are modified, so no detail is lost. This method is beneficial when selections are cut, copied, or pasted into composite images.

Anti-aliasing can be enabled for tools such as the Lasso, Polygonal Lasso , Magnetic Lasso , Elliptical Marquee, and Magic Wand. You must enable this setting in the Options bar before making a selection.

## Feathering

Feathering softens edges by creating a transition zone between the selection and its surroundings. Unlike anti-aliasing, which may reduce sharp detail at the boundary, feathering introduces a blur effect.

Feathering can be applied when using tools like the Rectangular Marquee , Lasso, Polygonal Lasso , or Magnetic Lasso , and it can also be applied after a selection has been made. The effect becomes visible only after you move, cut, copy, or fill the selection.


====================================================================================================

## 224. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/select-pixels-using-anti-aliasing.html

Learn more about how to create smoother selection edges with anti-aliasing in photoeditor.

Select the Lasso tool from the toolbar.

Select Anti-alias checkbox in the Options bar.

Make your selection in the image. The resulting selection will have smoother edges.

Create smooth transitions and soften the edges with feathered edges in professional photoeditor.

Enter a value in the Feather box in the Options bar to define the width of the feathered edge.

Go to Select>Modify>Feather.

Enter a value for the Feather Radius.

Select OK.

## 226. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/create-selection-around-selection-border.html

The Border command lets you select a width of pixels inside and outside an existing selection border. This is useful for isolating a band of pixels around an image area, such as cleaning up halo effects around a pasted object.

Use a selection tool to make a selection.

Choose Select**>**Modify**>**Border.

Enter a value between 1 and 200 pixels for the border width.

Select OK.

The new selection surrounds the original area and is evenly centered on its border. For example, a border width of 20 pixels creates a selection that extends 10 pixels inside and 10 pixels outside the original border.

## 227. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/expand-or-contract-selection.html

Expand or contract selections by pixel values or include areas with similar colors in professional photoeditor.

## Expand or contract a selection by a specific number of pixels

Use a selection tool to make a selection.

Choose Select**>**Modify**>**Expand or Contract**.**

For Expand By or Contract By, enter a pixel value between 1 and 100.

Select OK.

The border is increased or decreased by the specified number of pixels. Any portion of the selection border running along the canvas edge is unaffected by the Expand.

## Expand a selection to include areas with a similar color

Choose Select**>**Grow to include all adjacent pixels within the tolerance range set in the Magic Wand options.

Choose Select**>**Similar to include pixels throughout the image, not just adjacent ones, that fall within the tolerance range.

You cannot use the Grow and Similar on Bitmap mode images or 32-bits-per-channel images.

## 228. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/clean-up-stray-pixels-in-color-based-selection.html

Use the Smooth command to remove stray pixels and soften jagged edges of a selection in professional photoeditor.

Make a selection using any selection tool.

Select Select**>**Modify**>**Smooth.

Enter a pixel value between 1 and 100 for Sample Radius.

Select OK.

photoeditor examines the pixels around each selected pixel to a distance specified in the radius. If more than half of the surrounding pixels are selected, the pixel remains, and unselected neighbours are added. If fewer than half are selected, the pixel is removed. This reduces patchiness and smooths sharp corners or jagged lines in the selection.

## 229. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/fringe-pixels-around-a-selection.html

Learn how fringe pixels affect selections and how to remove unwanted edges using photoeditor Matting for cleaner composites.

## What are fringe pixels?

When you move or paste an anti-aliased selection, some of the pixels surrounding the selection border are included with the selection. These additional pixels create what's known as a fringe or halo around the edges of the pasted selection. This fringe effect becomes particularly noticeable when you select against a background that differs significantly from the original.

Fringe pixels are a natural byproduct of anti-aliasing, which creates smooth edges by partially filling pixels along the selection border. While anti-aliasing improves the appearance of edges, these partially transparent pixels can retain colors from the original background, creating visible artifacts when moved to a new context.

## Why fringe pixels occur

Fringe pixels typically appear for several reasons:

*   **Anti-aliased selections:** Create smooth edges by partially filling edge pixels, while retaining some color from the original background.
*   **Contrast changes:** Make fringe pixels more visible when moving a selection to a background with a different color.
*   **Transparency blending:** Allow semi-transparent edge pixels to retain traces of the original background when blended with a new one.

## Matting commands for removing fringe pixels

*   **Color Decontaminate:** Replace background colors in fringe pixels with nearby thoroughly selected pixel colors to preserve natural edges while eliminating contamination.
*   **Defringe:** Replace fringe pixel colors with pixels farther inside the selection to create cleaner edges and smooth transitions.
*   **Remove Black Matte and Remove White Matte:** Remove black or white matte edges from anti-aliased selections, ensuring clean placement on different backgrounds.

You can also refine edges using the Advanced Blending sliders in the Layer Styles dialog box. Making black or white areas transparent can remove fringe pixels while keeping edges smooth. Alt-click (Windows) or Option-click (macOS) the sliders to split them, allowing finer control over the transition.


====================================================================================================

Select OK to apply the change.

## 232. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/use-selections-for-generative-editing.html

Learn how to use selections to make targeted generative edits in professional photoeditor on desktop.

### Understand selection-based generative editing

Selections let you define the specific area of an image that you want to edit. When you make a selection before using generative editing features, photoeditor applies the changes within that selected area, helping you maintain control over the rest of your image.

When you use Firefly Image 5, native mask support helps the model understand selected regions and blend generated edits naturally with the surrounding image. Instead of treating a selection as a simple boundary, it uses surrounding context to preserve details outside the selected area, including image structure, identity, and other unchanged elements.

Using selections can make editing more precise than describing the location of an edit in a prompt. You can select a specific area, such as a subject’s eyes, and describe the desired change while keeping the rest of the image intact.

### Apply generative edits using selections

## 233. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/select-areas-and-pixels-in-focus.html

Use automated focus detection with manual refinement to isolate sharp subjects from blurred backgrounds with Focus Area.

Use Focus Area to analyze focus data and detect the sharpest pixels. It creates an initial selection for refinement. This technique is ideal for images where the subject is in focus and the background is blurred, such as portraits or macro photography. For images with uniform focus, consider using color-based or object-based selection methods instead.

## Opening the Focus Area

With your image open in professional photoeditor, select Select>Focus Area.

Use the In-Focus Range slider to control selection sensitivity. Lower values include more areas, while higher values restrict selection to the sharpest regions.

Leave Auto selected to let photoeditor determine optimal values.

## Refining the selection

Select the Add Brush tool to paint over focused areas that weren't included in the automatic selection.

Select the Remove Brush tool to paint over incorrectly selected areas that should be excluded.

Adjust the Image Noise Level slider if your selection contains unwanted noise or speckled areas.

Select Preview to toggle between the refined selection and the original image.

Use brush tools to refine boundaries and correct selection errors.

## Choosing the output format

Select Soften Edge to apply feathering (softening the edges of a selection) for smoother transitions at boundaries.

Use the Select and Mask workspace if you need advanced edge refinement beyond the Soften Edge option.

Select your preferred output from the Output To menu: Selection (default), Layer Mask, New Layer, New Layer With Layer Mask, New Document, or New Document With Layer Mask.

Select OK to apply the selection with your chosen output format.

Select the layer or group in the Layers panel.

Select your image.

Select how you want the mask to affect the selected area:

*   To hide the selected area, Alt-click (Windows) or Option-click (macOS) the Layer masks icon or select Layer>Layer Mask>Hide Selection.

To directly edit layer transparency, select the layer in the Layers panel and select Layer>Layer Mask>From Transparency.

## Apply a layer mask from another layer

Select how to apply it to another layer:

## 239. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/layer-masks/alpha-channels-and-masks-in-photoeditor.html

Learn how to convert your selection into a permanent alpha channel mask that you can edit and reuse across projects.

Alpha channels store masks as permanent grayscale images in the Channels panel, letting you reuse complex selections across multiple editing sessions. This approach preserves your original selection work and enables non-destructive editing workflows.

## Create an alpha channel from a selection

A mask isolates and protects specific image areas while you apply changes to the rest. Think of a mask as a stencil that determines which parts are exposed to editing and which stay protected from adjustments, filters, or color changes.

Masks are valuable for complex editing scenarios. You can gradually apply effects across an image, blend multiple exposures, or create sophisticated composites where elements transition smoothly between layers. Selection tools alone can't provide this level of control.

## The relationship between selections and masks

Selections and masks represent the same concept from different perspectives. When you create a selection with the Marquee, Lasso, or Magic Wand tools, you're defining a boundary between editable and protected pixels. That boundary exists temporarily in your document—it disappears when you deselect.

Masks store these boundaries as visible, editable grayscale images. The selection you created becomes a pattern of black, white, and gray pixels that you can paint on, blur, or modify with filters. This transformation from selection to mask gives you more flexibility. You can refine the boundary with painting tools instead of recreating the selection.

## Alpha channels as mask storage

Alpha channels provide permanent storage for your masks in the Channels panel. While Quick Mask mode creates temporary masks for immediate editing, alpha channels save masks you want to reuse later or apply to different images.

Each alpha channel functions as an independent grayscale image within your document. You can create multiple alpha channels to store different selection boundaries, then load any saved channel as an active selection whenever needed. This becomes essential in production workflows where you repeatedly need the same selection—product photography, batch processing, or template-based designs.

## Quick Mask mode overview

Quick Mask mode bridges the gap between selections and permanent masks. When you activate Quick Mask mode, your current selection becomes a temporary colored overlay—typically red—that you can edit immediately with any painting or editing tool.

The colored overlay shows which areas are protected and which are exposed. You can paint with black to add to the protected area, paint with white to expose more pixels, or use gray tones to create partial transparency. Once you exit Quick Mask mode, the overlay converts back to an active selection with your refinements.

Quick Mask mode excels at creating selections that require soft edges, gradual transitions, or precision adjustments difficult to achieve with standard selection tools. The temporary nature means you can experiment freely without permanently affecting your channel structure.

## How grayscale values control masking

Masks operate on a simple principle: black conceals, white reveals, and gray creates partial transparency. This grayscale-to-opacity mapping enables precise masking control.

Black pixels in a mask represent fully protected areas where no editing occurs. These pixels remain completely shielded from adjustments, filters, or painting. White pixels represent fully exposed areas that receive 100% of any editing operations you apply. Gray pixels create partial masking effects where the darkness of the gray determines the degree of protection. Darker grays allow less editing through, while lighter grays permit more.

This grayscale mapping works identically whether you're editing a Quick Mask, painting on an alpha channel, or modifying a layer mask. Understanding this principle lets you create sophisticated effects like gradual transitions, soft vignettes, or areas where adjustments fade smoothly from full strength to zero impact.

## Channel options for masks

When creating or modifying alpha channels, you can configure how photoeditor displays and interprets the mask. These options don't change the grayscale information—they affect only how you view and interact with the mask.

**Masked Areas option** displays protected areas as black and editable areas as white in the channel thumbnail. When selected, painting with black expands the protected region while painting with white expands the editable region. The Quick Mask button in the toolbox shows a white circle on gray background.

**Selected Areas option** inverts the display so protected areas appear white and editable areas appear black. This reverses the painting behavior—white expands protection while black expands editability. The Quick Mask button shows a gray circle on white background.

**Spot Color option** converts the alpha channel to a spot color channel for printing with specialty inks. This option only appears for existing channels.

**Color settings** control the overlay color and opacity when viewing the mask. These visual adjustments help you see the mask more clearly against your image colors but don't affect how the mask functions. Changing the overlay from red to blue or adjusting its transparency makes no difference to which pixels are protected—it simply makes the mask easier to see.

The choice between Masked Areas and Selected Areas display modes is purely personal preference. Both modes create identical masks; they just reverse the visual representation to match your mental model of working with protected versus editable regions.

For step-by-step instructions on building masks, see Create and edit alpha channel masks. To preserve selections for future use, see Save and load selections.


====================================================================================================

## 240. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/layer-masks/create-and-manage-clipping-masks.html

Create and manage clipping masksLearn how to use the content of a base layer to control visibility of layers above it.

Clipping masks give you precise control over which parts of your layers remain visible by using one layer's content to define the visibility of layers above it.

A clipping mask uses the content of a base layer to mask the layers above it. The non-transparent areas of the base layer reveal the content of the clipped layers, while transparent areas hide it. This technique works well for filling text or shapes with images, creating photo frames, or applying effects within specific boundaries.

You can include multiple layers in a clipping mask, but they must be consecutive in the layer stack. The base layer name appears underlined in the Layers panel, and clipped layer thumbnails display with indentation and a clipping mask icon.

The Blend Clipped Layers As Group option in the Layer Style dialog determines whether the base layer's blending mode affects the entire group or only the base layer itself. For more information about how blending modes interact with layer groups, [see layer opacity and blending](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/layer-opacity-blending.html#group_blend_effects).

Alternatively, you can select the first layer above the base layer in the Layers panel and choose Layer>Create Clipping Mask.

To add more layers to an existing clipping mask, repeat the process with each additional layer immediately above the previously clipped layer. All clipped layers inherit the opacity and blending mode of the base layer.

If you create a new layer or drag an existing layer between layers in a clipping mask, that layer automatically becomes part of the clipping mask.

## Remove a layer from a clipping mask

You can also select any clipped layer in the Layers panel and choose Layer>Release Clipping Mask. This removes the selected layer and all layers above it from the clipping mask.

## Release all layers in a clipping mask


====================================================================================================

## 241. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/layer-masks/edit-and-refine-layer-masks.html

Paint with black, white, or gray to control visibility and create precise non-destructive edits.

This procedure modifies an existing layer mask by painting directly on the mask to adjust which areas of a layer are visible. If you need to create a new layer mask, see [Add layer masks](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/layer-masks/add-layer-masks.html). For advanced edge refinement and selection-based masking techniques, see [Refine your selection and mask](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/make-selections/refine-modify-selections/refine-your-selection-and-mask.html).

Layer masks work on a fundamental principle: white reveals, black conceals, and gray creates partial transparency. When you activate a mask for editing, photoeditor automatically sets your foreground and background colors to grayscale, making it easy to paint precise visibility adjustments.

## Paint on a layer mask

In the Layers panel, select the layer containing the mask you want to edit.

Select the mask thumbnail in the Layers panel to activate it for editing.

Select any painting or editing tool from the toolbar.

Paint on your image with white to reveal more of the layer, black to hide areas, or gray to create partial transparency. Darker grays make the layer more transparent, while lighter grays make it more opaque.

When painting on a mask, you're not changing the actual image - you're only adjusting what's visible. This non-destructive approach lets you experiment freely, knowing you can always reverse your changes by painting with the opposite color.

To verify which element you're editing, check the Layers panel. A border around the mask thumbnail indicates the mask is active. A border around the layer thumbnail means you're editing the layer itself.

## Switch between editing the mask and the layer

In the Layers panel, select the thumbnail you want to edit.

Verify a border appears around your selected thumbnail, confirming it's active.

You can also add copied content directly into a layer mask, which converts the pasted selection to grayscale values that control visibility.

## Paste a selection into a mask

Copy the content you want to add to the mask using Edit>Copy.

Alt + click (Windows) or Option + click (macOS) the layer mask thumbnail in the Layers panel to display the mask channel.

Select Edit>Paste to add the copied content into the mask.

Select Select>Deselect to remove the selection border.

Select the layer thumbnail in the Layers panel to return to normal editing view.

Press X to quickly swap your foreground and background colors while painting on a mask, letting you easily switch between revealing and concealing.


====================================================================================================

Vector masks provide scalable edges at any resolution for graphics, logos, and precise selections.

Vector masks use paths rather than pixels to define layer visibility, creating clean edges that scale without loss of quality. Use vector masks when you need precise geometric shapes or smooth curves. For organic selections, such as hair or soft edges, use[layer masks](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/masking-layers.html)instead.

Create vector masks that reveal or hide entire layers, build masks from shapes, or edit existing mask paths. Adjust, disable, or convert vector masks to layer masks as needed.

## 243. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/layer-masks/position-elements-with-snapping.html

Enable magnetic alignment to rulers, guides, grids, and layer edges for precise element placement.

Snap elements to rulers, guides, grids, and layer edges for precise positioning without manual measurement.

When you drag a selection, crop marquees, slice, shape, or path within approximately 8 pixels of a guide, grid line, document edge, or layer boundary, it automatically aligns to that target. This magnetic feature helps you create precise layouts quickly.

You can turn snapping on or off globally or hold a key while dragging to position elements freely.

## Enable or disable snapping

To temporarily disable snapping while using the Move tool, hold Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) while dragging.

## Configure snap targets

After you turn on snapping, choose which reference points work as snap targets. You can enable multiple targets simultaneously to create a flexible alignment system.

To enable only one target, turn off Snap, then select View>Snap To and choose your target. This turns on snapping for that target only and deselects all others.

### Snap targets

**Guides**: Snaps to guide lines you've placed in your document. Use this to align elements to layout boundaries, margins, or specific measurement points. [Learn how to create and manage guides](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/show-or-hide-guides-grids-and-smart-guides.html#grid_and_guides).

**Grid**: Snaps to the document grid. Use this to create symmetrical layouts, maintain consistent spacing, or align elements at regular intervals. This option isn't available when the grid is hidden. [Learn how to configure and display grids.](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/show-or-hide-guides-grids-and-smart-guides.html#grid_and_guides)

**Layers**: Snaps to the content boundaries of other layers. Use this to position elements relative to existing layer content, such as aligning text to an image edge or arranging multiple design elements with consistent spacing.

**Slices**: Snaps to slice boundaries in web or interface designs. Use this when dividing graphics for web export or preparing interface assets. This option isn't available when slices are hidden.

**Document Bounds**: Snaps to canvas edges. Use this to position elements flush with the document margins or to create full-bleed designs.

**All**: Turns on all snap targets at once for maximum alignment assistance when you want elements to snap to any available reference point.

**None**: Turns off all snap targets while keeping the Snap command enabled. Use this to clear your target selections quickly.

## Snapping in different workflows

Snapping adapts to your current tool and task. When you move layers with the Move tool, they snap to all enabled targets. When you create selections or use shape tools, the tool edges snap as you drag.

For numeric positioning without snapping, use transform controls or enter values in the Options bar. For visual alignment with distance measurement, enable [Smart Guides](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/show-or-hide-guides-grids-and-smart-guides.html#grid_and_guides) alongside snapping.


====================================================================================================

## 244. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/blend-images/auto-blend-layers-command-overview.html

Learn more about the Auto-Blend Layers command to merge layers and improve image composition in professional photoeditor.

The Auto-Blend Layers command in **professional photoeditor** allows you to stitch or combine images with smooth transitions in the final composite image. It applies layer masks to individual layers to mask out over or underexposed areas or content differences.

The Auto-Blend Layers command is only available for RGB or Grayscale images,and it**does not support**Smart Objects**,**Video Layers**,**3D Layers**, or**Background Layers**.**

You can use the Auto-Blend Layers command to blend multiple images of a scene with different areas in focus to achieve a composite image with an extended depth of field.

Similarly, you can create a composite by blending multiple images of a scene with different illuminations. In addition to combining images of a scene, you can stitch together images into a panorama.

Use the Photomerge command to create panoramas from multiple images for better results.


====================================================================================================

## 245. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/blend-images/create-a-composite-with-extended-depth-of-field.html

Learn to blend multiple images with different focus points using the Auto-Blend Layers feature in professional photoeditor.

Open professional Bridge and select the images you want to combine.

Select Tools>photoeditor>Load Files into photoeditor Layers

Within the Layers panel in photoeditor, select the layers you want to blend.

With the layers selected, select Edit>Auto-Blend Layers.

In the Auto-Blend Layers dialog box, set the Blend Method as Stack Images**.**

Use the Panorama blend method to blend overlapping layers into a panorama image.

Use the Stack Images blend method to blend multiple images of a scene with different areas in focus or different illuminations to achieve the best results of all the images.

Select OK.

## 246. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-masks/blend-images/combine-multiple-images-into-group-portrait.html

Use Auto-Align Layers and layer masking to blend two similar photos into a flawless composite.

This workflow combines the best parts of two nearly identical images into a single flawless portrait. It's ideal when you have multiple shots of the same scene taken moments apart, where subjects and backgrounds remain in nearly identical positions, but each photo contains minor flaws - someone blinks, looks away, or closes their eyes. Auto-Align Layers automatically matches common areas between images, while layer masking lets you selectively reveal the best content from each photo.For blending scenarios involving different exposures or focus points, see the Auto-Blend Layers command overview.

Before starting, ensure both source images have identical dimensions and resolution. If they differ, crop or resize them to match.

## Set up your composite document

## Align layers and add a mask

The Reposition Only option prevents unwanted distortion while ensuring that identical areas perfectly overlap.

## Paint the mask to blend images

After completing your composite, save a layered version (File > Save As > photoeditor format) to preserve editability. Then create a flattened copy (Layer > Flatten Image) and save it in your preferred format for sharing or printing.

For more detailed information about layer masking techniques, see [Edit layer masks](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/editing-layer-masks.html). To explore automated blending across different exposures or focus points, see [Create a composite with extended depth of field](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/create-composite-extended-depth-field.html).

Select the image layer in the Layers panel.

Ensure the layer containing the subject is active and unlocked.

Select the search icon in the workspace or select Help>photoeditor Help from the menu bar to open the Discover panel.

Select Browse>Quick Actions in the Discover panel, then select Remove Background.

photoeditor isolates the subject and creates a transparent background or a masked layer ready for compositing or export.

To apply the action to a different layer, select it in the Layers panel and then select Refresh in the Discover panel to re-run the quick action on it.

Revert changes if needed by selecting Revert in the Discover panel.

For best results, use high-contrast images. If the background removal isn’t perfect, refine the mask manually using the Brush tool on the layer mask.

## 252. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/adjust-content-aware-fill-settings.html

Learn about the settings in the Content-Aware Fill panel, including sampling area overlay, fill options, and output controls in professional photoeditor.

The Content-Aware Fill panel provides several options to refine how photoeditor samples source pixels and applies the fill. You can control the sampling area, adjust fill behavior, and choose how results are output.

## Sampling area overlay

| **Setting** | **Description** | **Options/Values** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Show Sampling Area | Displays the overlay on the image in the document window. The reset icon restores the default sampling area. | On or off. |
| Opacity | Controls the transparency of the overlay. | Slider, a percentage value between 0% to 100%. |
| Color | Assigns a display color to the overlay. Select the color swatch to open the professional Color Picker. | Any color. |
| Indicates | Defines whether the overlay shows the sampling area or the excluded area. | Sampling Area, Excluded Area. |

## Sampling area options

| **Setting** | **Description** | **Options/Values** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Auto | Uses content like the surrounding fill area. | On or off. |
| Rectangular | Uses a rectangular region around the fill area. | On or off. |
| Custom | Allows manual definition of the sampling area using the Sampling Brush tool. | On or off. |
| Sample All Layers | Collects source pixels from all visible layers in the document. | On or off. |

| **Setting** | **Description** | **Options/Values** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Color Adaptation | Adjusts contrast and brightness for a smoother match. Useful for gradual color or texture changes. | None, Default, High, Very High. |
| Rotation Adaptation | Aligns rotated or curved patterns with surrounding content. | None, Low, Medium, High, Full. |
| Scale | Resizes content to match repeating patterns of different sizes or perspectives. | On or off. |
| Mirror | Flips the content horizontally to match symmetrical images. | On or off. |

The reset icon restores all fill settings to default values.

## Output settings

| **Setting** | **Description** | **Options/Values** |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Output To | Determines where the filled content is applied. | Current Layer, New Layer, Duplicate Layer. |


====================================================================================================

## 254. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/apply-or-cancel-fill-changes.html

Learn how to apply, cancel, or reset changes made to your image with Content-Aware Fill in professional photoeditor.

Select the object to remove. You can use the Object Selection tool, the Quick Selection  tool, or the Magic Wand tool to select your subject.

Select Content-Aware Fill by right-clicking within the selection. This opens the Content-Aware Fill workspace.

Select Apply in the Content-Aware Fill panel to commit a fill and keep the workspace open.

Select the Lasso tool or the Polygonal Lasso tool in the toolbar to make a new selection for another fill.

Select OK in the Content-Aware Fill panel to commit the fill and close the workspace.

You can also press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS).

Select Cancel in the Content-Aware Fill panel to cancel the fill and close the workspace.

You can also press Esc.

Select the Reset at the bottom of the Content-Aware Fill panel to reset all fill settings.

## 255. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/view-full-resolution-preview-in-the-preview-panel.html

Select the object to remove. You can use the Object Selection tool, the Quick Selection  tool, or the Magic Wand tool to select your subject.

Select Content-Aware Fill by right-clicking within the selection. This opens the Content-Aware Fill workspace.

Select the zoom slider in the Preview panel of the Content-Aware Fill workspace to adjust magnification.

Select the zoom value box in the Preview panel and type a value between 9% and 3200%. A higher percentage will result in greater magnification.

Select the Zoom tool in the toolbar and select an area on the image to adjust magnification directly on the image.

Select the Hand tool, then select the image and drag in any direction to pan across the preview image.

You can also press H while using another tool to switch temporarily to the Hand tool.

## 257. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/remove-tool-known-issues-and-workarounds.html

Learn about known issues with Remove tool in professional photoeditor and recommended workarounds to ensure stable usage.

**Issue****Resolution / Workaround**
photoeditor crashes when you use the Remove tool*   Update your[GPU drivers](https://helpx.professional.com/x-productkb/multi/update-graphics-driver.html)to the latest version.
*   Select Preferences>Image Processing and then choose More stable under Remove Tool Processing.
A message appears Required components are being downloaded. Check back later to use the tool once the download is complete.Wait for the download to complete. The required components are downloaded only once. Do not cancel the download or select Go back to previous tool.
An error message appears There was an error downloading the components.*   Check that your computer is connected to the Internet.
*   Ensure you have at least 1 GB of free disk space.
*   Restart photoeditor and select Remove tool again.
photoeditor crashes when you select Remove tool on macOS Sonoma 14.0 running on Apple silicon.Update macOS to the latest available version.
The Device option is unavailable in the Remove tool.*   Verify that your system meets the required hardware specifications for the on-device model.
*   If your system is not supported, use Cloud mode instead.
The Device option is unavailable in the Remove tool.

The on-device model fails to download or install from the Creative Cloud desktop app.*   Check your internet connection and available disk space.
*   Retry the download from the Creative Cloud desktop app.
The on-device model is downloaded but does not work in the Remove tool.1.   Quit photoeditor.
2.   Open the Creative Cloud desktop app.
3.   Select photoeditor, then Get add-ons.
4.   Remove the downloaded module.
5.   Reinstall the module.
6.   Relaunch photoeditor and try again.

## 262. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/fill-selections-with-multi-image-content-aware-fill.html

Learn how to use pixels from a second image to fill selected areas and remove unwanted objects.

Multi-Image Content-Aware Fill gives you more control over complex removals by letting you source pixels from a different photograph of the same scene.

This feature works when you have multiple photographs of the same location with slightly different elements—such as tourists walking through a landmark or vehicles passing through a street scene. Instead of sampling only from the current image, you can use pixels from a second photograph that doesn't contain the unwanted object. The two images should share significant content overlap in the region being filled, with minimal camera movement between shots.

Before you begin, open both the primary image you want to edit and the additional image that will serve as your pixel source. The Content-Aware Fill workspace remains open throughout this process, so you can make selections, apply fills, and refine results without switching between workspaces.

*   Two images of the same scene with overlapping content
*   Primary image containing the object or area you want to remove
*   Additional image without the unwanted object

## Select and open the Content-Aware Fill workspace

The workspace displays your primary image on the left with a preview of the fill result, and the Content-Aware Fill panel on the right with adjustment options.

## Choose the additional image source

After you select the additional image, photoeditor generates a preview showing how the fill will look. The fill quality depends on how well the two images align and how much content they share in the selected region.

When you add a document, the Sampling Area overlay and the Sampling Brush tool are disabled. photoeditor automatically chooses pixels from the additional image to complete your fill.

## Adjust fill settings to match surrounding pixels

If you need to reset your adjustments, select the reset icon next to the Additional Document header to restore the default fill settings.

## Use alignment points to improve results (optional)

Alignment points help photoeditor understand how the two images relate to each other, particularly when the camera angle or position differs slightly between shots. You can place up to four pairs of alignment points. Each pair is numbered and color-coded for clarity.

Place alignment points close to the region being removed, but distribute them relatively evenly around the area for best results.

## Apply the fill and finish

photoeditor creates a new layer containing the filled result, preserving your original image. You can make additional refinements using standard retouching tools or repeat the process with different alignment points if needed.

For best results, use images taken with minimal camera movement and rotation. Less than 10 degrees of camera rotation around the subject produces the most seamless fills. The camera position can vary slightly between shots, but less translation between positions creates better results.


====================================================================================================

## 263. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/remove-objects-fill-space/use-the-content-aware-patch-tool.html

Use the Content-Aware Patch tool in photoeditor to remove unwanted elements and blend textures.

Learn to use the Content-Aware Patch tool to remove unwanted elements from your images.

Removes unwanted image elements by blending them with surrounding content using the Content-Aware tool. This works best when the surrounding area has similar textures and patterns.

## Before you begin

*   Make sure your image or background layer is editable
*   Make sure your image is rasterized

## Configure Patch tool settings

In the toolbar, hold down the Spot Healing Brush and select the Patch tool .

In the options bar, select Content-Aware from the Patch dropdown and then choose from the given options:

*   Patch: Choose patch option.
*   Structure: Enter a value between 1 and 7 to specify how closely the patch should reflect existing image patterns. Higher values (7) make the patch adhere strongly to existing patterns. Lower values (1) create loose adherence.
*   Color: Enter a value between 0 and 10 to control how much photoeditor blends colors in the patch. At 0, color blending is off. At 10, color blending is at its maximum.
*   Sample All Layers: Enable this option to create the result of the move in another layer using information from all layers. Select the target layer in the Layers panel.

Extend selections slightly beyond the unwanted element for better blending.

## Create and apply the patch

Select an area on the image to replace.

Use a selection tool, like Object Selection, to draw a selection around the area to replace.

Drag the selection over the area you want to fill.

Release to apply the patch.

When dragging your selection, position it over areas with similar textures and patterns. photoeditor blends the source area with the edges of your selection.


====================================================================================================

## 264. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/heal-clone/clone-source-panel.html

Learn how the Clone Source panel helps you clone precisely by managing multiple sample sources, scaling, rotating, and aligning content in professional photoeditor.

## Overview of the Clone Source panel

The Clone Source panel lets you manage how your sample behaves when using the Clone Stamp or Healing Brush tool. It allows you to scale, rotate, or align your sample and view an overlay to guide accurate cloning. You can save up to five sources and switch between them without needing to resample.

Benefits of using the Clone Source panel

The panel gives you more control and efficiency when retouching images. It’s especially helpful for symmetrical edits, repeating patterns, or complex compositions across multiple layers.

## Key capabilities of the Clone Source panel

*   **Multiple sources**: Save up to five sample sources and switch as needed.
*   **Transform tools**: Scale, rotate, or flip the sample to better match your target area.
*   **Overlay preview**: View a live overlay to align content before applying it.
*   **Sampling behavior**: Choose whether sampling restarts or continues from the last point.
*   **Layer sampling control**: Sample from the current layer, all layers, or visible layers.

When cloning in video or animation frames, the Clone Source panel lets you sample across different frames or even from other documents.


====================================================================================================

## 265. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/heal-clone/retouch-images-with-the-clone-stamp-tool.html

Select the Clone Stamp tool from the toolbar.

In the Options bar, adjust:

Mode: Control how cloned pixels blend with the target (for example, Normal fully replaces pixels, Multiply darkens, Lighten only replaces darker areas).

Opacity: Set the transparency of the cloned pixels. Lower values create more subtle retouching.

Flow: Control the speed at which paint builds up, allowing smoother, gradual blending.

Select the alignment and sampling settings:

*   Select Aligned to paint the most recent sampling point.
*   Deselect Aligned to restart sampling from the original point each time.
*   In the Sample menu, select a source:
    *   Current Layer: Clone from the active layer only.
    *   Current & Below: Clone from the active layer and the layers beneath.
    *   All Layers: Clone from all visible layers.

Select Alt-click (Windows) or Option-click (macOS) in the image to set the source point.

Note

The Clone Stamp tool does not work on adjustment layers.

Drag over the target area to apply the cloned pixels.

## 266. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/heal-clone/set-sample-sources-for-cloning-and-healing.html

Learn how to set up and switch between multiple sample sources using the Clone Stamp or Healing Brush tools in professional photoeditor.

In professional photoeditor, you can sample pixels from multiple sources to clone or heal content more precisely. Sampling sources can come from the same document, a different open document, or even different frames when working with video or animation. The Clone Source panel stores up to five sources at a time until you close the document.

If you're working on video or animation content, open the Timeline panel and move the current-time indicator to the frame you want to sample from.

Select the Clone Stamp or Healing Brush tool, then Alt-click (Windows) or Option-click (macOS) on the area you want to sample in any open document.

Select Window>Clone Source to display the panel if it's not visible.

To define another sampling point, open the Clone Source panel, select a different Clone Source icon, and repeat the sampling step.

You can update any Clone Source button by sampling again after selecting it.

Paint over the target area to clone the pixels. In the Clone Source panel, choose the corresponding button to switch between sampling sources.

## 269. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/adjust-light-tone/blending-mode-descriptions.html

Explore how different blending modes in professional photoeditor affect your images when using painting and editing tools.

Blending modes determine how pixels in your image are affected when using painting tools, editing tools, or multiple layers. Understanding these modes helps you achieve precise creative effects and solve specific editing challenges.

## Normal group

These modes either replace or randomly mix pixels without complex calculations.

## Darken group

These modes create darker results by preserving shadows, midtones, or dark colors from either the base or blend color.

## Lighten group

These modes create lighter results by preserving highlights, midtones, or light colors from either the base or blend color.

## Contrast group

These modes increase contrast by darkening areas darker than 50% gray and lightening areas lighter than 50% gray.

**Mode****Description**
Overlay Multiplies or screens the colors, depending on the base color. Patterns or colors overlay the existing pixels while preserving the highlights and shadows of the base color. The base color is not replaced but is mixed with the blend color to reflect the lightness or darkness of the original color.
Soft Light Darkens or lightens the colors, depending on the blend color. The effect is similar to shining a diffused spotlight on the image. If the blend color is lighter than 50% gray, the image is lightened. If the blend color is darker than 50% gray, the image is darkened. Blending with pure black or white produces a distinctly darker or lighter area but does not result in pure black or white.
Hard Light Multiplies or screens the colors, depending on the blend color. The effect is similar to shining a harsh spotlight on the image. If the blend color is lighter than 50% gray, the image is lightened, as if it were screened. If the blend color is darker than 50% gray, the image is darkened, as if it were multiplied. This mode is useful for adding shadows to an image.
Vivid Light Burns or dodges the colors by increasing or decreasing the contrast, depending on the blend color. If the blend color is lighter than 50% gray, the image is lightened by decreasing the contrast. If the blend color is darker than 50% gray, the image is darkened by increasing the contrast.
Linear Light Burns or dodges the colors by decreasing or increasing the brightness, depending on the blend color. If the blend color is lighter than 50% gray, the image is lightened by increasing the brightness. If the blend color is darker than 50% gray, the image is darkened by decreasing the brightness.
Pin Light Replaces the colors, depending on the blend color. If the blend color is lighter than 50% gray, pixels darker than the blend color are replaced, and pixels lighter than the blend color do not change. If the blend color is darker than 50% gray, pixels lighter than the blend color are replaced, and pixels darker than the blend color do not change.
Hard Mix Adds the red, green, and blue channel values of the blend color to the RGB values of the base color. If the resulting sum for a channel is 255 or greater, it receives a value of 255; if less than 255, it receives a value of 0. Therefore, all blended pixels have red, green, and blue channel values of either 0 or 255. This changes all pixels to primary additive colors (red, green, or blue), white, or black.

## Comparative group

These modes create results based on comparing or inverting colors between layers.

## HSL group

These modes affect specific HSL (Hue, Saturation, Luminosity) components of colors.

## Blending modes availability

Not all blending modes are available for all tools or in all color modes:

*   Blending modes available for 32-bit images are: Normal, Dissolve, Darken, Multiply, Lighten, Linear Dodge (Add), Difference, Hue, Saturation, Color, and Luminosity.
*   The Behind mode is only available for the Brush tool when working on layers without Lock Transparency.
*   The Clear mode is only available for the shape tools (with fill region selected), Paint Bucket tool, Brush tool, Pencil tool, Fill command, and Stroke command when working on layers without Lock Transparency.

## 270. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/adjust-light-tone/dodge-or-burn-image-areas.html

Explore how to use the Dodge and Burn tools to selectively adjust exposure in specific image areas without affecting the entire image in professional photoeditor.

Select the Dodge tool or Burn tool from the toolbar.

Select a Brush Preset in the Options bar and adjust Size, Hardness, and other brush settings.

Select the area you want to change in the Range menu:

*   Midtones – affects mid-range grays.
*   Shadows – affect darker areas.
*   Highlights – affects lighter areas.

In the Exposure field, enter a percentage to control how strongly the effect is applied.

Select the Airbrush in the Options bar for a gradual effect.

Select the Protect Tones checkbox to help maintain color and prevent clipping in highlights and shadows.

Paint over the image area to apply the Dodge or Burn effect.

## 271. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/adjust-light-tone/darken-the-edges-of-your-image-to-bring-focus-to-its-center.html

Learn how to draw attention to the center of your image in professional photoeditor by applying a vignette using quick actions.

Apply a vignette effect in photoeditor to subtly darken the edges of an image and enhance focus on the center. Use the built-in quick action for fast results, with options to refine the effect further.

Select the Search icon in the upper-right corner of the photoeditor interface.

Select Quick Actions under the Browse section of the discover panel, then scroll down to Add a vignette.

Select Apply. The vignette darkens the edges and brings soft focus to the center of your image.

To apply the effect on a different layer, select the layer and then select Refresh in the Discover panel.

Select Filter >Camera Raw Filter to adjust settings like Exposure, Contrast, Highlights, and more.

Select Revert to undo the applied vignette.

## 273. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/adjust-light-tone/adjust-shadow-and-highlight-detail.html

Adjust image shadows and highlights in photoeditor.

Apply Shadow/Highlight adjustments to correct backlighting and brighten dark areas by analyzing each pixel's surrounding area and adjusting tones locally rather than globally.

The Shadow/Highlight command applies adjustments directly to the image layer and discards information.

## Apply shadow and highlight corrections

Select Image>Adjustments>Shadow/Highlight.

Select the Preview checkbox to see adjustments update in real time.

Move the Amount slider in the Shadows or Highlights section, or enter a percentage value. Higher values lighten shadows or darken highlights.

Select Show More Options to access Tonal Width, Radius, and other refinement controls.

![Image 1: Screenshot of photoeditor Shadows/Highlights panel with sliders to set values.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Screenshot-of-photoeditor-Shadows-Highlights-panel-w?$png$&jpegSize=100&wid=561)

Select Save As Defaults to store your current settings for future use, or Shift-click Save As Defaults to restore factory defaults.

For well-exposed images that need only subtle shadow recovery, try values between 0–25% for both Shadows Amount and Shadows Tonal Width.

You can save and reload custom settings by selecting Save to export a .ahu file, then using Load to apply those settings to other images.


====================================================================================================

## 274. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/repair-retouch/adjust-light-tone/use-the-adjustment-brush-tool.html

Paint non-destructive local adjustments to specific areas of your image.

Use the Adjustment Brush Tool to make specific adjustments, such as brightening your subject's face or darkening the sky.

The Adjustment Brush tool applies changes only where you paint. This approach works best when you want to enhance specific areas while maintaining full control over your edits. Use this tool when you need to make localized adjustments such as, brightening or darkening a section without affecting the entire image.

The Adjustment Brush tool creates a new adjustment layer with a layer mask automatically, for each new adjustments to enforce non-destructive editing. The Contextual Task Bar or options bar helps you choose the adjustment options to refine the output.

Select the Adjustment Brush tool from the toolbar or press K.

Choose your desired Adjustment type, set the brush size, and hardness from the options bar.Adjustments are applied with default values; you can modify the values in Properties panel.

Select the Apply to object to apply your adjustment to a specific object in your image in one go.

Add/subtract from your adjustment, view on overlay, invert your selection, replace your existing adjustment type with a different adjustment, or add a new adjustment with the next Contextual Task Bar.

Start with a larger, softer brush to build up adjustments gradually. You can refine details later with a smaller brush.

To learn more on brush behavior and appearance, see [brush presets](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/brush-presets.html).To understand how masks work across different layer types, see [layer masks](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/masking-layers.html).

Learn how to improve the quality, sharpness, and clarity of your images in professional photoeditor.

![Image 1: Profile of a cat enhanced using Generative Upscale in photoeditor ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProdLoc/nfs-MAX-ps-d-gen-upscale-sept2025.jpg-1?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1920)

Enhance image resolution and improve detail with Generative Upscale.

Select File>Open to open a low-resolution or low-detail image.

Select Image>Generative Upscale.

In the Generative Upscale dialog, select the desired output scale (2x or 4x) and adjust parameters as needed.

Select the AI model to power the upscaling. Each model delivers a distinct type of enhancement.

*   Firefly Upscaler**:** Restores low-resolution images (up to 6144 px × 6144 px)
*   Topaz Gigapixel**:** Preserves existing detail (available for users with a premium subscription; up to 56 megapixels)
*   Topaz Bloom**:** Adds new, creative detail(available for users with a premium subscription; up to 9 megapixels)

Select Upscale to create the upscaled image in a new document with an updated name.
