## 346. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/fill-objects-selections-layers/content-aware-fills.html

Learn how to replace selected areas in professional photoeditor with context-aware content, custom patterns, or previous states.

photoeditor offers several powerful fill options that help you replace unwanted objects, add creative elements, or restore previous states of your image.

Select the area you want to fill in your image.

![Image 1: photoeditor interface showing a selected object on a lake image, prepared for removal using Content-Aware Fill.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-interface-showing-a-selected-object-on-a?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1299)

Start with a precise selection around the object you want to remove or replace.

Select Edit>Fill. The Fill dialog box opens.

Select the Contents dropdown menu in the Fill dialog box.

*   Content-Aware: Automatically fills the area based on surrounding pixels. 
*   Pattern: Fills the area with a selected pattern.
*   History: Fills the area using a previous History state.

For Pattern, select Custom Pattern to choose from saved patterns. For History, ensure the History Brush source is set correctly.

Select Pattern to fill the area with repeated patterns, and then select a pattern from the Custom Pattern dropdown menu.

Select Content-Aware to automatically fill the area based on surrounding pixels. For the best results, create a selection that extends slightly into the area you want to replicate.

Select History to restore the selected area to the source state or snapshot set in the History panel.

Under Blending, select the Mode and Opacity for your fill. For full coverage, use Normal mode and 100% opacity.

Select OK. photoeditor fills the selected area with your chosen content, pattern, or history state.

## 348. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/fill-objects-selections-layers/fill-selection-layer-color.html

Learn how to add a solid color fill to any selection or layer in professional photoeditor to enhance your designs and speed up your editing process.

In photoeditor, you can quickly apply solid color fills to backgrounds, shapes, or specific areas within a selection or layer. Use this method to set base tones, change backgrounds, block out composition zones, or build masks and effects efficiently.

Select a foreground or background color.

Select the area you want to fill using any selection tool. To fill an entire layer, select it in the Layers panel.

Select Edit>Fill. The Fill dialog box opens.

Under Contents, you can select:

*   Foreground Color
*   Background Color
*   Black, 50% Gray, or White
*   Color
*   Pattern

For CMYK images, select Foreground Color with the foreground color set to an appropriate black to avoid exceeding printer ink limits.

Under Blending, select the Mode and Opacity for the paint.

Select Preserve Transparency to fill only the non-transparent areas of a layer.

Select OK to apply the fill.

![Image 1: photoeditor interface showing fill options including color selection, blending mode, opacity, and Preserve Transparency. ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-interface-showing-fill-options-including?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1173)

Choose a fill color, blending mode, and opacity to apply color cleanly while preserving transparency.

### Keyboard shortcuts

*   Fill non-transparent areas with the foreground color: Press Alt + Backspace(Windows) or Option + Delete(macOS). 
*   Fill with the background color: Press Ctrl + Backspace(Windows) or Command + Delete(macOS).

## 349. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/fill-objects-selections-layers/create-a-new-layer-when-brushing.html

Learn how to automatically add a new pixel layer when you paint with the Brush or Pencil tool in professional photoeditor.

Select Edit>Preferences>General (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>General (macOS).

Locate and check the box next to Create a new layer when brushing.

Select OK to save your preferences.

Select the Brush tool and begin painting.

Note

When you turn on this option, photoeditor creates a new pixel layer if you paint on a non-paintable layer, such as a Smart Object or adjustment layer.

## 350. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/create-fill-with-patterns/pattern-preview-best-practices.html

Explore tips and techniques to improve Pattern Preview workflows.

## Performance tips

Pattern Preview uses the graphics card (GPU) for display, but may slow down when:

*   Using complex brushes for pattern creation.
*   Working with large documents.
*   Editing patterns at high zoom-out levels.

For best results in Pattern Preview, convert raster layers to Smart Objects before transforming, rotating, and scaling pattern elements

## Limitations

Keep these Pattern Preview limitations in mind:

*   It is disabled in Artboard documents.
*   It performs best when GPU is enabled, but remains functional without it. 
*   It is disabled when tools and commands (like Crop tool and Canvas Size) that change document dimensions are used. 
*   It may not work well with features like Symmetry, Liquify, or Vanishing Point.


====================================================================================================

## 351. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/create-fill-with-patterns/create-a-new-pattern.html

Learn how to design repeating patterns with Pattern Preview in professional photoeditor.

Open a document in photoeditor.

Select View>Pattern Preview to enable Pattern Preview.

Pattern Preview works best with smart objects.Once enabled, the area outside the canvas automatically repeats the canvas content.

Add text, images, colors, or shapes.

Select Edit>Define Pattern.

Enter the pattern name in the Pattern Name dialog box.

Select OK.The saved pattern will be visible in the Patterns panel, ready for use.


====================================================================================================

## 352. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/brushes-presets/get-started-with-brush-presets.html

#### Understand how professional photoeditor brush presets save your favorite brush characteristics for quick access and consistent results in your creative workflow.

[Brush presets](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/brushes-presets/create-a-new-preset-brush.html) are saved brush tips with predefined characteristics such as size, shape, and hardness. They allow you to store frequently used brush configurations for quick access, maintaining consistency across your projects and streamlining your creative workflow.

When you [select a preset brush](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/brushes-presets/select-a-preset-brush.html) in photoeditor, you choose a brush with specific settings that determine how it will behave when applied to your canvas. These presets save you time by eliminating the need to reconfigure brush settings for everyday tasks.

## Types of presets you can save

photoeditor offers two main types of saved brush configurations:

*   **Brush presets**: These store only the brush tip characteristics (size, shape, hardness, and more). Tool presets can be converted to brush presets using the Options bar >Tool Preset Picker> settings  icon >Convert All to Brush Presets, allowing you to integrate them into your brush library.
*   **Tool presets**: These save the brush tip characteristics and additional Brush tool settings from the Options bar, such as opacity, flow, and blending modes. You can save tool presets for the Brush tool by choosing Options bar > settings  icon >Tool Preset Picker>New Tool Preset.

## Temporary and permanent brush settings

The change is temporary if you modify a preset brush, such as changing its size or hardness. The next time you choose that preset, it returns to its original settings. To keep your changes, save them as a new preset. This way, you keep your original brushes intact while building a custom library that suits your needs.


====================================================================================================

The Brush Settings panel in photoeditor allows you to customize brushes and control paint application. You can adjust shape, texture, and dynamics to enhance your workflow. These settings apply to tools like the Eraser tool,the Clone Stamp tool, and various adjustment tools, allowing for precise and expressive editing.

Select Window>Brush Settings.

In the Brush Settings panel, select the checkbox beside each set, such as Scattering to turn them on or off.

Select a setting group name to view and customize settings like size, hardness, and spacing.

Custom brush tips from images offer distinct textures and painting styles. Use any image with interesting details to create brushes for digital art, photo retouching, and pattern design.

*   For soft-edged brushes, increase the Feather value.

Select OK to save your new brush tip. Your new brush appears in the Brushes panel.

photoeditor converts color images to grayscale when defining a brush tip.

## 357. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/apply-painting-techniques/brushes-presets/create-a-new-preset-brush.html

photoeditor stores all current brush settings (size, shape, hardness, and more) to maintain consistency in your artwork.

Learn about the different drawing tools available in professional photoeditor.

| **Tools** | **Description** |
| --- | --- |
| Pen tool | Draw smooth-edged paths. |
| Freeform Pen tool | Draw freeform paths. |
| Curvature Pen tool | Draw curved paths. |
| Add Anchor Point tool | Add anchor points to existing paths. |
| Delete Anchor Point tool | Delete anchor points from existing paths. |
| Convert Point tool | Convert straight anchor point to curved and vice versa. |
| Rectangle tool | Draw rectangular and square shapes. |
| Ellipse tool | Draw circular and elliptical shapes. |
| Triangle tool | Draw triangular shapes. |
| Polygon tool | Draw polygonal shapes. |
| Star tool | Draw star shapes. |

| Custom Shape tool | Draw custom shapes or select from the Shapes panel (Window>Shapes). |

## 363. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/create-shapes/create-shapes.html

To draw a shape on the canvas, click and drag any tool from the shapes tool group in the Tools panel.

Hold the Shift key while drawing the shape to make it proportional.

Select the Contextual Task Bar(Window>Contextual Task Bar) to edit shape properties:

*   Fill**:** Select a color to fill the shape.
*   Stroke**:** Select the color, width, and type of stroke.
*   Duplicate layer**:** Create an exact copy of a selected layer so it can be reused or modified without changing the original.

You can also select Shape Properties from the Properties panel or the Options bar to edit shape properties.

![Image 1: A rectangle has been drawn on the canvas in professional photoeditor on the desktop app, where the Rectangle tool has been highlighted in the shapes tool group in the Tools panel. ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-rectangle-has-been-drawn-on-the-canvas-in-professional-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Select Fill or Stroke from the Contextual Task Bar(Window>Contextual Task Bar) to adjust the fill or stroke color:

*   Gradient: Applies a gradient to the shape layer upon selecting a gradient preset from the given options.

The Contextual Task Bar is present, showing the Fill dropdown menu.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Rectangle-tool-is-highlighted-in-the-Tools-pan?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Draw custom shapes using built-in options or create your own from paths and text in professional photoeditor.

Use custom shapes to add reusable vector graphics to your designs. You can draw shapes from the built-in library, import additional ones, or create and save your own.

## Draw and edit a custom shape

Select Edit>Define Custom Shape and enter a name for the new custom shape in the Shape Name dialog.

View the new shape in the custom shape picker in the Options bar.

## 366. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/create-shapes/draw-star-shapes.html

Select the Star tool from the shapes tool group in the Tools panel.

Drag on the canvas to draw a star.

Use the settings in the Contextual Task Bar to customize the shape.

*   Fill and Stroke:Selects the desired colors.
*   Stroke width:Adjusts the thickness of the stroke.
*   Set number of sides (or points on a star):Selects the number of tips of the star.
*   Corner radius:Adjusts the sharp corners.
*   Inner Radius:Defines the depth of the indents between the spikes.
*   Outer Radius:Defines the length of the star's spikes.

You can also adjust the points, corner radius, and inner/outer radii from the Options panel.

To draw a line, select and drag anywhere on the canvas.

## 370. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/shape-path-and-pixel-mode-options.html

Find detailed information about drawing modes when using the Line tool and shape tools in professional photoeditor, including available settings for each mode.

When using the Line tool or other shape tools in photoeditor, you can choose between three different drawing modes: Shape, Path, or Pixels. Each mode determines how your lines and shapes are created and what editing options are available afterward.

*   **Fill color**: Select a color from the color picker or swatches to fill the center of shapes or arrowheads.
*   **Stroke color**: Select a color for the line and outline of shapes.
*   **Stroke width**: Set the thickness of the stroke in pixels.
*   **Stroke type**: Select between solid, dashed, or dotted lines, and set the following:
*   Align: Select Inside, Center, or Outside alignment.
*   Caps: Select between Butt, Round, or Square line cap shapes for the ends of lines.
*   Corners: Select how corners appear for shapes with corners (not applicable to the Line tool).
*   Weight: Set the width of the path in pixels (visual reference only).

### Live Shapes controls

*   Enable on-canvas transform controls by selecting the gear  icon and selecting Live Shapes Controls.
*   Adjust shape properties directly on the canvas.
*   Access additional options in the Properties panel.

## Path mode options

*   **Operation**: Select how new paths interact with existing paths (Add, Subtract, Intersect, or Exclude).
*   **Path arrangement**: Set the stacking order when working with multiple paths.
*   **Path alignment**: Align and distribute path components.
*   Weight: Set the width of the path in pixels (visual reference only).

After creating a path, you can:

*   Convert it to a selection using Select>Load Selection.
*   Fill it with color using Edit>Fill.
*   Stroke it with color using Edit>Stroke.
*   Save it as a named path in the Paths panel for future use.

## Pixel mode options

*   Mode: Select the blend mode to determine how pixels interact with underlying content
*   Opacity: Set the transparency level from 1-100%
*   Weight: Set the width of the line or shape edge in pixels
*   Anti-alias: Enable to create smoother edges (automatically applied)

## Additional settings

For all drawing modes, you can access some of these additional settings:

*   Path Operations icon: Show or hide path operation buttons.
*   Path Arrangement icon: Show or hide path arrangement buttons.
*   Path Alignment icon: Show or hide path alignment buttons.
*   Set additional shape and path options icon: Set additional Path options, Live Shapes Controls, Arrowheads, and more.
*   Set number of sides (or points of a star) icon: Set the number of sides while using the Polygon tool, or points while using the Star tool.
*   Set radius of rounded corners icon: Set the curve width of rounded corners (not applicable to the Line tool).


====================================================================================================

Learn how to add arrowheads to a line to draw an arrow in professional photoeditor.

Select Line tool from the shapes tool group in the Tools panel to draw a line.

Select Start or End in the Arrowheads section of the Contextual Task Bar(Window>Contextual Task Bar).

Enter values for Width and Length (in pixels) of the arrowhead.

Enter a value for Concavity, between -50% and +50%, to control the curve where the arrowhead meets the line.

## 372. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/trace-images-easily-with-the-content-aware-tracing-tool.html

Quickly create vector paths or selections by detecting and tracing object edges of an image in professional photoeditor.

The Content-Aware Tracing tool is available as a Technology Preview feature in professional photoeditor. To enable it, select Preferences>Technology Previews and restart photoeditor.

Select Content-Aware Tracing tool from the Pen tool group in the toolbar.

Position the pointer close to the object edge to automatically detect and highlight it.

Move the Detail slider in the Options bar to the right to detect edges and to the left to simplify edge detection.

Select a tracing option from the options bar:

*   Create paths from detected edges**:** Generates a new path.
*   Extend currently selected path with detected edges**:** Adds to an existing path. 
*   Trim traced paths**:** Removes unnecessary anchor points from the generated path. 

Select once to generate a vector path that follows the detected edge.


====================================================================================================

## 373. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/draw-curves-and-straight-segments-intuitively.html

Learn how to draw and edit smooth curves and straight line segments by selecting points on the canvas to create a path in professional photoeditor.

Select Curvature Pen tool from the Pen tools group in the toolbar.

Select anywhere on the canvas to create the first anchor point.

Select again to place the second anchor point, and complete the first segment of the path.

*   Select once to draw a curved segment.
*   Double-click to draw a line segment.
*   Continue selecting to add more points and create a path.

Select the first point to close the path.

Double-click an anchor point to toggle between a curved point and a corner (straight) point.

![Image 1: A triangle has been drawn on the canvas in professional photoeditor on the desktop app, where the Curvature Pen tool has been highlighted in the Tools panel. ](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-triangle-has-been-drawn-on-the-canvas-in-professional-P?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

Draw curves and segments using the Curvature Pen tool in the Pen tools group.


====================================================================================================

## 374. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/draw-paths-with-the-pen-tool.html

Learn how to use the Pen tool to draw straight segments and curves in professional photoeditor.

The Pen tool is one of the most powerful drawing tools in photoeditor. It allows you to create precise vector paths with complete control over curves and straight segments.

## Draw straight line segments

Select with the Pen tool where you want to start your first line segment. This creates a corner point.

Select again where you want the segment to end. Continue selecting to create additional straight segments.

Hold Shift while selecting to constrain a line to 45-degree angles.

Select the starting point to close the path. Press Ctrl + Enter (Windows) or Command + Return (macOS) to leave the path open.

## Draw curved segments

Select with the Pen tool where you want to start your curve, then drag to create direction handles that shape the curve.

Position your cursor where you want the curve to end, and drag to create another curve, or just select to end with a corner point.

Hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) and drag a direction handle to adjust a curve while drawing.

Drag the second point in the opposite direction from the first point's direction handle to create an S-curve.

Drag the starting point to close the path with a curve.

## Combine straight and curved segments

Draw straight segments with the Pen tool as needed.

Apply any of the following:

*   **Transition from a straight segment to a curve:** Select where the straight segment should end, then drag to create a smooth point with direction handles.
*   **Transition from a curve to a straight segment:** Hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) while selecting to convert a smooth point to a corner point.


====================================================================================================

## 375. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/draw-shapes-paths/draw-lines-curves/overview-of-pen-tool-settings.html

Explore the complete range of Pen tool settings and options available in professional photoeditor to create precise paths, curves, and shapes for your design projects.

The Pen tool in photoeditor provides precise control for creating paths, shapes, and selections. Understanding the various Pen tool settings helps you work more efficiently and achieve better results in your design projects.

When you select any Pen tool, the Options bar (Window>Options) displays these important settings:

### Drawing modes

**Mode****Description**
Shape Creates a shape layer with a vector mask
Path Creates a work path that can be stroked or filled later
Pixels Creates a rasterized shape directly on the current layer

### Auto Add/Delete

When enabled, this option allows you to:

*   Add anchor points by selecting a path segment.
*   Delete anchor points by selecting them.
*   Prevent changing the shape of the path when selecting an anchor point.

### Rubber Band

When turned on, this option displays a preview of path segments as you move the pointer, before selecting to create the next anchor point. This helps visualize the path you're creating. Select the Set additional pen and path options icon to select Rubber Band.

## Path creation settings

### Anchor point types

**Point Type****How to Create****Behavior**
Corner point Select without dragging Creates sharp corners with straight segments
Smooth point Select and drag Creates curved segments with direction handles
Combination point Varies based on adjacent points Can connect curved and straight segments

### Direction handles

Direction handles control the curve's shape:

*   Longer handles create flatter curves
*   Shorter handles create sharper curves
*   Asymmetrical handles create uneven curves
*   Handles pointing in opposite directions create S-curves


====================================================================================================

## 376. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/get-started-with-text/add-text.html

Learn to add point and paragraph text in professional photoeditor to create professional flyers, posters, or magazines.

## Add point text

Point text is a single, editable text layer that appears in a straight line without boundaries or a bounding box. It can include line breaks, and it works well for headlines, buttons, or simple annotations.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the toolbar.

Select anywhere in the document and start typing to add point text.

A text layer is added in the Layers panel, and you can identify it with the Type icon.

![Image 1: Screenshot of a photoeditor document showing horizontal and vertical point text. The Layers panel shows multiple layers, with one type layer highlighted.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Screenshot-of-a-photoeditor-document-showing-horizon?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1333)

Select anywhere in your document with the Horizontal or Vertical Type tool to add point text.

## Add paragraph text

Paragraph text is perfect for longer content, such as paragraphs and descriptions. It creates a bounding box around the text, allowing you to edit, resize, shape, and format entire paragraphs within a single layer.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the toolbar.

Drag anywhere in the document to add paragraph text.

A text layer is added in the Layers panel, and you can identify it with the Type icon.

![Image 2: Screenshot of a photoeditor document showing horizontal and vertical paragraph  text. The Layers panel shows multiple layers, with one type layer highlighted.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Screenshot-of-a-photoeditor-document-showing-horizon_55850?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Drag anywhere in your document with the Horizontal or Vertical Type tool to add paragraph text.

Learn how to edit existing text in professional photoeditor to fix typos, improve clarity, or update text directly in any text layer.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the Tools panel.

Select within the text to place the insertion point where you want to make changes. To select specific text, select and drag over the characters you want to modify.

The Layers panel shows multiple layers, with one type layer highlighted.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Screenshot-of-an-open-photoeditor-document-with-a-se?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1333)

Make the necessary text changes.

Select the Commit icon from the Options bar.

Select Window>Options to view the Options bar.

Select the Bulleted list icon or Numbered list icon from the Bullets and Numbering section.

Learn how to repurpose your content by copying and pasting text from any source file or another photoeditor document.

## Copy and paste text from any source file

Copy the text from a source file, such as Word, PDF, or a web page.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool, then select the text frame where you want to paste the copied text.

Select Edit>Paste (Command + V (macOS) or Ctrl + V (Windows)).

## Copy and paste text from another photoeditor file

Open the source file.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool, then select the text layer from the Layers panel.

Select Edit>Copy (Command + C (macOS) or Ctrl + C (Windows)).

Open the destination file where you want to paste the copied text layer.

Select Edit>Paste (Command + V (macOS) or Ctrl + V (Windows)).

Learn how to adjust the size of text within a text layer or the size of the entire text layer.

## Resize selected text within a text layer

Select the text you want to resize using the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool.

Select Window>Character, then select a size from Set font size. If you can’t find the size you need, type a custom number.

![Image 1: A sample text file with the first line selected. The Character panel is open with a dropdown list of available sizes. The original font size of 20 pt is at the top, and the size 24 pt is selected.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-text-file-with-the-first-line-selected--T?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Select any text and set the font size from the Character panel.

## Resize a text layer

Select the Move tool, then select the text layer you want to resize in the Layers panel.

![Image 2: A photoeditor file where the sample text is surrounded by a bounding box. The Layers panel is open with the text layer selected.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-photoeditor-file-where-the-sample-text-is-surround?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Drag any anchor point to resize the bounding box and the text within.

While dragging, you can:

*   Hold Shift to resize the text non-proportionally within the text layer.
*   Hold Alt to keep the center of the text layer in place.
*   Hold Command (macOS) or Ctrl (Windows) to explore skewed angles.

Select Commit icon in the Options bar.

Select Window>Options to view the Options bar.

Select anywhere inside the bounding box and drag to the desired location.

Use the arrow keys to move the text frame precisely. Press Shift when using the arrow keys to move in larger increments.

The frame has been moved 8px left and 23px down from its original position.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-photoeditor-layers-panel-has-the-type-layer-sele?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

## 385. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/get-started-with-text/update-cjk-text-layers.html

Learn how to update Chinese, Japanese, and Korean (CJK) text layers in professional photoeditor for accurate display and printing.

When you open a document, photoeditor may detect differences in how Chinese, Japanese, or Korean (CJK) text was rendered, especially if it was created in another version or uses different fonts. photoeditor may then prompt you to update the text layers to ensure accurate display and vector-based output.

Select File>Open to open a document containing CJK text in photoeditor.

When prompted, select Update to refresh text layers for vector-based output. Most changes are minor and visible only when zoomed in.

![Image 1: Document with CJK text layers and a dialog box prompting to update text layers for vector-based output.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProdLoc/ps-add-edit-text-text-panel-updated-blurred?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

photoeditor prompts users to update text layers for accurate vector-based output when opening a document with CJK text. 

Review the document for text changes, such as shifted, reflowed, or missing text.

Adjust the text frame, spacing, or alignment, or press Esc to cancel and keep the original text appearance.

If you don’t update text layers, photoeditor uses the saved bitmap version for display and printing. Update layers to ensure crisp, vector-based CJK text.


====================================================================================================

## 386. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/about-fonts.html

Learn about common font terms, font types, and how to identify them using icons in professional photoeditor.

## Font terminology

**Term****Description**
Font A complete set of characters with a common weight, width, and style.
Typeface A family of fonts that look similar and are meant to be used together. Many include special characters beyond the keyboard, such as ornaments, ligatures, and fractions.
Glyph A specific form of a character. For example, in certain fonts, the capital letter A is available in several forms, such as swash and small caps.
Type style A variation of a single font within a family, like bold, italic, or semibold. If a font doesn’t include a style you need, you can apply faux styles, such as simulated versions of bold, italic, superscript, etc.

## Font types and their icons

You can preview fonts in the Character panel, Contextual Task Bar, and Properties panel. Use the following icons to identify font types:

*   professional fonts  icon (previously Typekit  icon)
*   OpenType _icon_
*   Type 1 _icon_
*   TrueType _icon_
*   Multiple Master _icon_
*   OpenType SVG _icon_
*   OpenType variable fonts _icon_

Select Type>Font Preview Size to turn off the font preview, or change the point size of font names.


====================================================================================================

## 387. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/replace-missing-fonts.html

Learn how to replace missing fonts in professional photoeditor to prevent print and export issues.

When you open a file with missing fonts, photoeditor automatically activates them from professional Fonts if you're connected to the internet. A blue sync  icon appears on the text layer in the Layers panel during activation. Once the font is available, the icon changes to the standard text layer  icon. The activated font becomes available across professional apps.

## Replace missing professional fonts while activation is in progress

If you edit a text layer with a missing font while the font activation is in progress, photoeditor displays a prompt to replace the missing font with a default font and continue editing. In the dialog, you can select one of the following:

Select a text layer from the Layers panel where the font activation is in progress.

Select any of the following options:

*   Replace**:** To replace missing fonts with the default font. For Roman text, the default font is Myriad Pro Regular.
*   Cancel**:** To exit text-editing mode and resume activation of your missing professional Fonts.

## Replace missing non-professional fonts

You can replace missing non-professional fonts with the default font or a font already used in the document.

Select Type>Manage Missing Fonts.

Use the drop-down options in the Manage Missing Fonts panel to:

*   Replace with the default font. For Roman text, the default font is Myriad Pro Regular.
*   Replace with a font already used in the document.
*   Don't replace.

Select Replace All Missing Fonts With the Default Font to replace all missing fonts in the document with the default font.

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

Learn how to identify existing fonts in an image and match them with licensed fonts to create editable templates in professional photoeditor.

Select the text in your photo with the Rectangular Marquee tool.

Select Type>Match Font. professional photoeditor displays a list of fonts similar to the text in your selection.

In the Match Font dialog, you can select a Type Option.

Uncheck Show fonts available to add from professional Fonts to hide fonts from professional Fonts and only view the ones available locally on your computer.

Select the font closest to the one you selected in the image.

![Image 1: A sample document showing an image with the text selected. The Match Font dialog is open, showing a list of fonts similar to the selected text.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-showing-an-image-with-the-text-s?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Select a font from the list of similar fonts in the Match Fonts dialog box.

Select OK.

## 389. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/search-for-and-apply-a-specific-font-style.html

Learn how to find and use particular font styles to speed up your workflow when using large font libraries in professional photoeditor.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool from the toolbar (Window>Tools).

Select the text layer where you want to apply the new font from the Layers panel.

Select Search and select fonts from the Options bar (Window>Options) and type the font name you want to use.

Filter the font list by Your fonts, More fonts, or select between the Show professional Fonts icon, Show Variable Fonts icon, Show favorite fonts icon, or Show similar fonts icon.

If multiple font variants, such as Regular, Italic, Bold, and Bold Italic, are available, they’re grouped under the same menu item. You can expand and select the desired variant.

Select a font style from the list.

## 390. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/overview-of-opentype-fonts.html

Learn how OpenType fonts provide enhanced typography features and cross-platform compatibility for your professional photoeditor projects.

OpenType fonts work seamlessly across Windows and macOS using a single font file. You can move your photoeditor files between systems without worrying about font substitution or text alignment issues.

These fonts offer more than cross-platform compatibility. They support advanced typographic features like swashes, ligatures, fractions, small caps, and old-style numbers. You can activate these stylistic options in photoeditor when working with OpenType fonts.

Some fonts come with expanded character sets and broader language support. For example, professional OpenType fonts with Central European (CE) support include Pro in their names, while standard versions use Std. You can use OpenType fonts alongside PostScript Type 1 and TrueType fonts.


====================================================================================================

## 391. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/apply-opentype-features.html

Learn to apply OpenType features in professional photoeditor to access alternate characters and glyphs for customizing decorative fonts without distortion.

With OpenType features, you can use the built-in alternates instead of manually transforming characters. You can also use fractions, ordinals, or case-sensitive forms for clean, accurate typography.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the toolbar.

Select Window>Character to open the Character panel, and then select an OpenType font. OpenType fonts typically have Pro in their name.

Select the text you want to modify. If you don't select any text, the setting will apply to the new text you create.

Select the more options icon from the Character panel, and then select OpenType.

Select a feature from the OpenType submenu, such as Standard Ligatures, Contextual Alternates, Discretionary Ligatures, Swash, Old Style, etc.

![Image 1: Character panel showing OpenType submenu with Standard Ligatures, Contextual Alternates, and other typography features available for text formatting.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Character-panel-showing-OpenType-submenu-with-Stan?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Apply OpenType features to your text using the Character panel.

The selected OpenType feature will be applied to the text immediately.


====================================================================================================

## 392. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/use-opentype-variable-fonts.html

Learn to use OpenType variable fonts in professional photoeditor for creating bold typographic effects without switching fonts, perfect for flexible and responsive designs.

OpenType variable fonts give you precise control over text thickness and spacing while keeping text fully editable. You can adjust font styles without cluttering your Layers panel.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the toolbar (Window>Tools).

Select an OpenType variable font from the Options bar (Window>Options). They typically have the word Variable in their name.

Select the text you want to modify. If no text is selected, the settings apply to the new text you create.

Select the Variable font settings icon from the Character section of the Properties panel.

Adjust Weight and Optical size using the sliders.

![Image 1: A sample document with selected text and Properties panel Character section showing variable font controls.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-with-selected-text-and-Propertie?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Set the Weight and Optical size of OpenType variable fonts using Properties panel.

Changes to OpenType variable font settings apply to your text immediately.


====================================================================================================

## 393. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/select-manage-fonts/change-the-font-on-multiple-layers.html

Learn how to modify fonts across several layers in professional photoeditor to fix font mismatch in reused templates or maintain design consistency across all text elements.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool from the toolbar (Window>Tools).

Hold Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) and then select multiple type layers from the Layers panel.

Select the down arrow  beside Search and select fonts from the Options bar (Window>Options) to view the available fonts.

Select the desired font.

## 394. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/type-layers-creation/fill-text-with-image.html

Learn to create striking visual effects by filling text shapes with images in professional photoeditor using the Convert to Frame feature.

Add text to your document using the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool from the toolbar (Window>Tools).

Add the image you want to use for filling the text.

In the Layers panel, drag your image layer under the type layer. Ensure that the image is large enough to fill the text.

Right-click the text layer in the Layers panel and select Convert to Frame. The image appears inside the text shape.

![Image 1: photoeditor document with text layer selected, image beneath text, and Convert to Frame option highlighted in context menu.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-document-with-text-layer-selected--image?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1333)

Select Covert to Frame to fill text with image.

Drag the image using the Move tool to reposition the image within the text frame.

## 395. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/add-text-along-paths-or-inside-shapes.html

Create dynamic typography by adding text along curved paths or inside shapes in professional photoeditor for creative text effects.

Add text along paths or inside shapes to create dynamic, creative typography effects. This allows text to flow along curves, circles, custom paths, or to fill the interior of closed shapes.

Text on a path flows along the edge of an open or closed work path created with the Pen tool or any shape tool. When you add text to a path, it follows the direction in which the anchor points were added. For closed shapes, text can be placed either along the edge or inside the shape's boundaries.

## Add text along closed or open paths

Create a path or shape in your document.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool.

Position the cursor over the path until you see the type cursor with a wavy line .

Select the path where you want to add the text and start typing.

![Image 1: A photoeditor document showing text flowing along circular and curved paths with Layers panel visible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image933705?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1380)

![Image 2: A photoeditor document showing text flowing along circular and curved paths with Layers panel visible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image933705?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1380)

_Select the Horizontal or Vertical Type tool to add text along a closed or open path._

Select the Commit icon from the Options bar (Window>Options).

## Add text inside shapes

Create a path or shape in your document.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool.

Place the cursor inside a shape until you see the type cursor enclosed in a circle .

Select inside the shape and start typing to fill the shape with text.

![Image 3: A photoeditor document showing vertical text inside a rectangle and horizontal text inside a circle with the Layers panel visible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image394546?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1323)

![Image 4: A photoeditor document showing vertical text inside a rectangle and horizontal text inside a circle with the Layers panel visible.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image394546?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1323)

_Select the Horizontal or Vertical Type tool to add text inside a shape._

Select the Commit icon from the Options bar (Window>Options).


====================================================================================================

## 396. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/flip-or-move-text-along-a-path.html

Flip text to opposite sides of paths or reposition text along path curves in professional photoeditor for precise typography control.

After you add text along a path, you can flip it to the opposite side of the path or move it to any position along the path. This works with both open paths (like lines or curves) and closed paths (like circles or shapes). The text maintains its relationship to the path while you reposition it.

Select the Path Selection tool or the Direct Selection tool from the Tools panel.

Select the text layer from the Layers panel.

Hover over the text path to see the type cursor with a right arrow .

To flip text to the opposite side of the path, select the text and drag it across the path.

Select anywhere on the path to change the text's beginning position. Drag the cursor along the path to move the text.

For precise control, hover over the end of the text path for the type cursor with a left arrow . Drag it to adjust where you want to end your text.

![Image 1: Text on path with beginning and ending markers visible, and type layer selected in Layers panel for editing path text.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Text-on-path-with-beginning-and-ending-markers-vis?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1313)

Drag text with the Direct or Path Selection tool to flip it to the other side of the path or reposition it along the path.


====================================================================================================

## 398. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/warp-and-unwarp-text.html

Add warp effects to bend and distort text into creative shapes while keeping text fully editable in professional photoeditor.

Warping text allows you to create eye-catching typographic effects by bending and distorting text into shapes like arcs, waves, or flags. Unlike other distortion methods, warped text remains fully editable. You can change the text content, font, or color while maintaining the warp effect.

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool.

Select the text you want to warp, and then select Type>Warp Text.

In the Warp Text dialog, select a warp style from the Style drop-down menu. Options include Arc, Arc Lower, Arc Upper, Arch, Bulge, and more.

Select the Horizontal or Vertical orientation of the warp effect.

Adjust the Bend slider to control the intensity of the warp effect. Higher values create more dramatic curves.

Use the Horizontal Distortion and Vertical Distortion sliders to apply perspective to your warp.

![Image 1: Warp Text dialog showing Wave style applied to text, with Bend and Distortion sliders and type layer selected in Layers panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Warp-Text-dialog-showing-Wave-style-applied-to-tex?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1328)

Set a Warp Style, and apply Bend and Distort effects using the Warp Text dialog.

Select OK to apply the warp effect to your text.

## Unwarp text

Select the Horizontal Type tool or the Vertical Type tool.

Select the warped type layer in the Layers panel, and then select Type>Warp Text.

In the Warp Text dialog, select None from the Style drop-down menu.

Select OK to remove the warp effect from your text.


====================================================================================================

## 399. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/convert-text-to-shapes-or-work-paths.html

Transform text into editable vector shapes or work paths in professional photoeditor to create custom designs and special effects with your typography.

Converting text to shapes permanently changes your text layer into a vector shape layer. This method is best when you want to modify letter shapes directly.

Converting text to work paths creates a separate path in the Paths panel while keeping your original text layer intact. This method is best when you want to use text outlines for selections or effects..

Select the text you want to convert to a shape with the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool.

Select Type>Convert to Shape. You can see that the type layer icon changes to a shape layer icon, indicating successful conversion.

Use the Direct Selection tool to select and modify individual anchor points and paths of your converted text.

![Image 1: A sample document showing text converted to shape with the anchor points visible. The Layers panel is open.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-showing-text-converted-to-shape-_724562?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Drag individual anchor points to modify your text as a vector shape.

After converting text to a shape, you can modify each letter as a vector shape with adjustable anchor points, fills, strokes, and path operations.

## Convert text to work paths

Select the text you want to convert to a shape with the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool.

Select Type>Create Work Path. A new path appears in the Paths panel while your original type layer remains intact.

Select the Path Selection tool or Direct Selection tool to work with the path.

![Image 2: A sample document showing a work path with anchor points and a text layer. The Layers and Paths panels are open.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-showing-a-work-path-with-anchor-?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Drag any anchor point using the Direct Selection tool to change the letter paths.

After converting text to a work path, you can create selections, stroke with color, or fill with patterns. The original text layer remains editable while maintaining a separate path version.


====================================================================================================

## 400. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/create-text-selection-borders.html

Learn how to create text-shaped selections that can be manipulated like any standard selection in professional photoeditor.

Text selection borders allow you to create selections in the shape of text characters. You can move, copy, fill them with color, or add a stroke outline. Unlike regular type layers that remain editable, type selection borders create selection outlines on your image layer. You can use these to create text effects that interact directly with the pixels of your image, rather than floating over it as a separate layer.

Select the layer on which you want to add the type selection border. For best results, use a normal image layer, not a type layer. If you plan to fill or stroke the selection, consider creating it on a new blank layer.

Select the Horizontal Type Mask  tool or the Vertical Type Mask tool from the toolbar.

Select the image where you want to place the text. A red mask overlay will appear as you type.

Type your text and adjust the font, size, and other text attributes in the Options bar (Window>Options) as needed.

Select Commit icon from the Options bar. A selection border in the shape of your text appears on the active layer.

![Image 1: A photoeditor document with a sample text selection border on an image. The Layers panel is open, showing that the background image is selected. The Paths panel is open and empty.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-photoeditor-document-with-a-sample-text-selection-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Use the Horizontal or Vertical Type Mask tool to add text selection borders.

## 401. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/text-on-paths-shapes/add-drop-shadows-to-text.html

Learn how to use layer styles to add professional-looking drop shadows in professional photoeditor that create depth and dimension to your text elements.

Adding drop shadows to text in professional photoeditor is a simple yet effective way to make it stand out from the background. This creates the illusion of depth by making the text float above the surface, improving readability and visual appeal.

Select the text layer in the Layers panel that you want to add a drop shadow to.

Select the Add a layer style icon >Drop Shadow.

![Image 1: Text with Drop Shadow effect applied. The Layer Style panel displays Drop Shadow settings.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Text-with-Drop-Shadow-effect-applied--The-Layer-St?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Adjust the Drop Shadow settings to customize the shadow effect.

Adjust the Opacity, Distance, Spread, and other settings in the Layer Style dialog as needed.

You can view the effect listed under your text layer in the Layers panel and toggle the effect visibility by selecting the eye icon next to Drop Shadow.

Select OK to apply the effect.

## 403. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/characters-glyphs/add-emoji-glyphs.html

Insert colorful emoji into text using the Glyphs panel or system shortcuts in professional photoeditor for enhanced designs.

professional photoeditor supports emoji through the Noto Color Emoji SVG font, which replaced the previously bundled EmojiOne font. This OpenType SVG font displays emojis in full color with gradients, making them perfect for adding visual interest to your designs.

## Using the Glyphs panel

Select the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool to create a new type layer or select an existing one.

Select the font Noto Color Emoji SVG from the Options bar, Character panel, or Properties panel. This will automatically open the Glyphs panel.

If you don’t see the Glyphs panel, select Window>Glyphs.

Browse through the Glyphs panel to find the emoji you want to use, and select on any emoji glyph to insert it into your text layer at the current cursor position.

![Image 1: A sample document showing tree emojis added as point text with the Glyphs panel open.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-showing-tree-emojis-added-as-poi?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1333)

Double-click any emoji glyph from the Glyphs panel to add it as text in your document.

## Using keyboard shortcuts on Windows

Select a type layer and position your cursor where you want to insert an emoji.

Press Windows + . (period) to open the on-screen emoji keyboard.

Select an emoji to add it or use the category tabs to browse different emoji types.

Press Esc to close the emoji keyboard.

## Using Character Viewer on macOS

Select a type layer and position your cursor where you want to insert an emoji.

Press Control + Command + Space to open the character viewer.

Select a category from the list, and then double-click an emoji to add it to your text layer.

Select the close x button to close the Character Viewer.

## 404. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/characters-glyphs/enable-glyph-protection.html

Prevent incorrect character display when working with international fonts by activating the Glyph protection feature in professional photoeditor.

Glyph protection helps maintain text readability when working with documents containing fonts not installed on your system. This feature automatically selects an appropriate font when you enter non-Roman text, such as Japanese or Cyrillic, after selecting a Roman font, preventing incorrect or unreadable characters from appearing.

By default, professional photoeditor enables glyph protection to ensure text remains readable when working with international characters.

Select Edit>Preferences>Type (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>Type (macOS).

Select Enable Missing Glyph Protection.

![Image 1: A sample document with the Preferences panel open with the Enable Missing Glyph Protection option highlighted and checked.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/A-sample-document-with-the-Preferences-panel-open-?$png$&jpegSize=200&wid=1338)

Check Enable Missing Glyph Protection to replace unavailable international fonts with appropriate fonts.

Select OK to apply the changes.

Insert special characters, symbols, and alternative glyphs into your text in professional photoeditor.

The Glyphs panel provides special characters, symbols, and alternative character forms that might not be easily accessible from your keyboard. This feature is especially useful when you need to add punctuation marks, currency symbols, mathematical symbols, or characters from other languages to your text.

The Glyphs panel works with any active text layer and supports Latin, Greek, and Cyrillic scripts. It has limited support for Hebrew, Arabic, and other complex scripts, such as Indic scripts.

View and select alternative character glyphs directly on your canvas without opening the Glyphs panel for faster typography workflows.

When working with typography in professional photoeditor, you often need alternative character forms like ligatures, swashes, or stylistic variants. The on-canvas glyph alternatives feature displays these options directly on your canvas for faster access. This works with OpenType fonts that include alternative glyphs.

Select Edit>Preferences>Type (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>Type (macOS).

In the Preferences dialog, check Enable Type layer glyph alternates.

Select a character with alternative glyphs using the Horizontal Type tool or Vertical Type tool. You can view the alternatives grid near the selected character.

Select any alternative glyph in the grid to replace the selected character. To access the full Glyphs panel, select the right arrow  icon.

![Image 1: Text with selected character showing alternate glyph grid. Layers panel displays active text layer.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Text-with-selected-character-showing-alternate-gly?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

## 407. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/international-text-languages/overview-of-unified-text-engine.html

Understand how the Unified Text Engine enables advanced typography for international languages and scripts in professional photoeditor.

## What is the Unified Text Engine?

The Unified Text Engine is a modern text processing system that replaces the legacy text engines previously required for different language scripts. Introduced in photoeditor 23.0 (October 2021), it enables advanced typographic features for international languages and scripts, including Arabic, Hebrew, Indic scripts, Japanese, Chinese, and Korean.

This unified approach eliminates the need to switch text engines when working with different languages, streamlining your workflow and making multilingual design more accessible.

## Key enhancements and benefits

### Improved text shaping

The Unified Text Engine uses the open-source HarfBuzz text shaping engine to provide superior text rendering for Arabic, Hebrew, Chinese, Japanese, Korean, and all non-Latin scripts. This ensures proper character formation, ligatures, and contextual alternates across multiple writing systems.

### Better bidirectional text support

With integrated FriBidi technology, the Unified Text Engine handles right-to-left and bidirectional text more effectively. This is particularly valuable when working with Arabic and Hebrew scripts, especially when they're mixed with left-to-right languages in the same text block.

## Working with the text layer Properties panel

The text layer Properties panel serves as the central hub for accessing all typographic features in the Unified Text Engine. This panel organizes options into logical sections, making it easier to find and apply the specific typographic controls you need for your text.

When working with different scripts, the relevant typographic options will automatically appear in this panel, eliminating the need to search through multiple menus or switch between different text engines.


====================================================================================================

## 408. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/international-text-languages/create-documents-using-international-languages-scripts-and-type.html

Use the Unified Text Engine to create documents with international languages and scripts in professional photoeditor.

The Unified Text Engine helps you to work with international languages and scripts from around the world. This feature eliminates the need to switch between different text engines when working with various languages in your designs.

The Unified Text Engine replaces legacy text engines and provides advanced typographic features for international languages and scripts. This includes support for Arabic, Hebrew, Indic scripts, Japanese, Chinese, Korean, and other non-Latin scripts. With this unified approach, you can create multilingual designs without the complexity of changing text engines for each language.

## Create a document with international text

Set the desired language in the Character panel. Language selection is needed only for hyphenation and spelling.

Select an appropriate font based on the selected language. For example, if you choose Thai language, set a Thai font such as professional Thai or Noto Sans Thai.

Copy and paste text composed in your chosen language. If you have a language-specific keyboard, simply type the text directly.

With the Unified Text Engine, photoeditor automatically handles the text shaping and layout for your selected language without requiring you to switch between different text engines.

## Set language using the Properties panel

Select your text layer in the Layers panel.

Select Window>Properties to open the Properties panel.

Select your desired language from the Language dropdown menu.

The text layer Properties panel provides access to all advanced typographic features for your selected language. These features are automatically grouped together based on the language you're using.

## Install language packs on Windows

To use these languages properly on Windows, install the Language and Font packs for the particular language you want to use.

For details, check Microsoft's Help documentation:[Language Packs](https://support.microsoft.com/en-us/help/14236/language-packs).


====================================================================================================

## 409. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/international-text-languages/supported-international-scripts.html

Find the list of languages supported by the Unified Text Engine in professional photoeditor.

photoeditor supports scripts from around the world with shaping and basic common attributes (no advanced typographic features). photoeditor only supports advanced typographic features for Latin, Arabic, and Japanese. For all other scripts, only shaping and common type attributes are supported.

Text features across professional applications do not necessarily have feature parity, as each application can rely on different text engines. Also, text features can vary according to the application's primary usage (image editing, page layout, illustration, and so on), so the preservation of formatting, style, and editability across applications can also vary according to the type of treatments you choose to apply.

The Unified Text Engine composer supports the following languages and scripts:

**Indic**

*   Bengali
*   Gujarati
*   Hindi
*   Kannada
*   Malayalam
*   Marathi
*   Oriya
*   Punjabi
*   Tamil
*   Telugu

**Middle Eastern**

*   Arabic
*   Hebrew

**South East Asian**

*   Thai
*   Sinhalese
*   Burmese
*   Khmer
*   Lao

**CJK**

*   Chinese
*   Japanese
*   Korean

South East Asian (SEA) languages or scripts are not currently supported on all professional products. Moving SEA text from an professional app that currently supports SEA scripts to an application that does not support them is likely to result in the loss of some or all text copy and formatting edits.In this case, note the following:

*   Image files may need to be flattened before moving from photoeditor to other applications.
*   Text files from professional Illustrator may need to be converted to outlines before usage in other applications.
*   Text conversions may result in a loss of editability when moved from one application to another.


====================================================================================================

## 410. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/work-with-dynamic-text/dynamic-text-overview.html

Learn how Dynamic Text can make your text resize automatically for shapes and paths in professional photoeditor on desktop.

Dynamic Text automatically forces text to span the length of a given shape or path, so when the letters change, they will recalibrate their size to fit the specified range of the shape or path. You may use presets, or create your own shapes or paths to use with the Dynamic fit option.

## Dynamic Text presets

*   Block**:** Text scales and reflows to fit within a rectangular bounding box.
*   Circle**:** Text follows a circular path and scales between defined start and end points.
*   Arch**:** Text follows an upward-curving path and scales between defined points.
*   Bow**:** Text follows a downward-curving path and scales between defined points.

Block scales and reflows text to fit within a rectangular bounding box. Circle, Arch, and Bow position text along curved paths and scale it between defined start and end points. Dynamic fit is also available for text placed on any path or shape, automatically resizing the text to fit

Dynamic fit works when text is placed on any path or shape. Draw a path using the Pen tool, Curvature Pen tool, or Shape tool, then select the Type tool and select the path or near it to add text. Select Dynamic fit to automatically resize text to fill the path or shape.

## Properties

*   Block
    *   Set spacing between words**:** Adjusts gaps between words.
    *   Set spacing between lines**:** Adjusts gaps between lines.

*   Circle, Arch, and Bow
    *   Adjust text position**:** Controls where the text sits relative to the path (above, center, below).
    *   Adjust text direction**:** Toggles the reading direction of the text.

*   Dynamic fit**:** Automatically resizes text so it fills the selected path or shape. As you edit the text, photoeditor automatically adjusts its size to keep it fitted to the path.


====================================================================================================

## 411. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/work-with-dynamic-text/adjust-formatting-and-resize-text-with-dynamic-text.html

Learn how to resize and reflow text to fit a bounding box, shape, or path using Dynamic Text in professional photoeditor on desktop.

_Adjust formatting and resize text with Dynamic Text_

Dynamic Text automatically adjusts text size to fit within the defined boundaries on shapes and paths.

You can apply Dynamic Text as Block, Circle, Arch, or Bow, or use Dynamic fit to automatically resize text placed on any path and shape.

Select File>Open to select and open an image.

Select the Type tool, then select existing text or add new text.

Select Dynamic Text from the Contextual Task Bar.

You can also select Type>Convert to Dynamic Text or Dynamic Text from the Options bar.

Select the text shape:Block,Circle,Arch, or Bow. To apply Dynamic fit to any path or shape, create or select a path, add text to it, and then select Dynamic fit. To understand how each shape resizes and behaves, see [Dynamic Text in photoeditor](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/work-with-dynamic-text/dynamic-text-overview.html).

From the Properties panel, in the Dynamic Text section:

*   For Block, adjust word and line spacing.
*   For Circle, Arch, and Bow, adjust the text position and text direction.
*   Select Dynamic fit to automatically resize text so it fills the selected path or shape.

For best results, enter or paste your final text before selecting Dynamic fit. This lets you preview the actual content before photoeditor automatically resizes it to fit the path.

To quickly adjust the size of the section of the shape or path where you want the text to start and end, hold down the control key, and drag the start, middle, or end 'delimiters' to different positions on the shape or path.

Also, when applying text to a path or shape, the paragraph alignment options will determine how the text will be initially positioned. To center text on a shape, select the center preset for paragraph alignment in the options bar, and select the center of where you want the text to appear.


====================================================================================================

## 412. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/text-typography/work-with-dynamic-text/reposition-start-and-end-points.html

Learn how to reposition start and end points for Circle, Arch, and Bow Dynamic Text in professional photoeditor on desktop.

Select File>Open to select and open an image.

In the Layers panel, select the text layer with the Dynamic Text .

Select the Path Selection tool from the toolbar.

Position the pointer at the beginning of the text. When the cursor changes, drag to move the start point along the path.

Position the pointer at the end of the text. When the cursor changes, drag to move the end point along the path.

Release the mouse when the start or end point is in the desired position. The text resizes to fit the updated span.

## 413. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/filters-overview.html

Learn about filters in professional photoeditor, how they affect layers and selections, and how Smart Filters allow non-destructive editing.

## What are filters?

Filters change how pixels look in your image. You can use them to apply a wide range of visual effects, from subtle corrections to creative styles.

You can find all filters in the Filter menu. When applied to a Smart Object, some filters become Smart Filters, letting you edit or remove them later without permanently changing your image.

## How filters work

*   Filters apply effects to the active layer or to a selection within a layer.
*   They can be used for creative effects, such as Oil Paint in the Stylize category, or for corrections, such as Sharpen or Reduce Noise.
*   To edit non-destructively, apply your filter to a Smart Object layer. This preserves your original content.

## Things to know about filters

*   Filters in the Filter menu are grouped into categories, such as Blur, Distort, Noise, Stylize, and Render.
*   Some filters are available only in specific color modes, such as RGB Color.
*   Specific filters won’t work on images larger than 30,000 x 30,000 pixels.
*   If a filter is unavailable, check your document’s color mode, bit depth, or layer type.
*   Third-party filters and plug-ins also appear in the Filter menu.

## When to use filters

Filters are most effective when used with purpose and restraint. Consider these best practices:

*   Use filters to enhance your creative vision, not replace it.
*   Apply filters to selected areas using masks, not to the entire image.
*   Lower filter opacity for subtle effects.
*   Combine multiple filters for unique looks.
*   Adjust settings based on your final output.


====================================================================================================

## 414. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/apply-filters.html

Learn how to apply filters in professional photoeditor to add creative effects or corrections to a layer or selection.

Filters are commands that modify the appearance of an image. They are often used for enhancements, corrections, or creative transformations. For non-destructive editing, convert your layer to a Smart Object before applying a filter.

Select the target layer from the Layers panel.

Select Filter and then select a filter category.

Select a specific filter from the submenu.

Some filters apply instantly. Others open a dialog box where you can adjust settings and select OK to apply the effect.

Select Filter>Filter Gallery.

In the Filter Gallery dialog box, expand a category folder and select a filter.

## 416. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/filter-gallery.html

Learn about the Filter Gallery in professional photoeditor, where you can preview and apply multiple creative filters to an image.

The Filter Gallery lets you apply and combine artistic, brush stroke, sketch, stylize, and texture effects. You can preview filters, adjust their settings, reorder them, and apply multiple effects non-destructively when working with Smart Objects.

## Filter categories

The Filter Gallery organizes effects into the following categories:

**Category****Examples of filters****Purpose**
Artistic Colored Pencil, Cutout, Watercolor Painting and artistic media effects
Brush Strokes Accented Edges, Crosshatch, Sprayed Strokes Brush and ink stroke effects
Distort Diffuse Glow, Glass, Ocean Ripple Geometric distortion effects
Sketch Bas Relief, Chalk & Charcoal, Reticulation Pencil, charcoal and paper effects
Stylize Glowing Edges, Solarize, Wind Stylization and edge effects
Texture Craquelure, Grain, Texturizer Surface texture effects

## Gallery interface

**Component****Description**
Preview area Central panel showing how filters affect your image
Filter thumbnails Visual examples of each filter effect
Filter settings Adjustable parameters for the selected filter
Applied filters list Stack of currently applied filters
