## 417. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/blend-and-fade-filter-effects.html

Learn how to fade a filter’s strength or blend it with a layer using blending modes for more control.

When you [apply a filter](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/apply-filters.html)in professional photoeditor, you might want to reduce its intensity or creatively combine it with the original image. You can:

*   Fade the filter effect by lowering its strength with the Opacity slider.
*   Blend the filter with the layer using different blending modes such as Multiply, Screen, or Overlay.

Apply a filter to a layer.

Select Edit>Fade from the menu.

The Fade command is only available immediately after applying a filter, paint stroke, or adjustment.

Adjust Opacity to fade the effect in the Fade dialog box.

Select a blending mode from the Mode dropdown to control how the filter interacts with the layer.

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 418. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/tips-for-creating-special-effects.html

Learn about techniques and filter combinations in professional photoeditor that help you create unique and artistic visual effects.

photoeditor filters can be combined and adjusted to create everything from textured looks to dramatic lighting or motion effects. To get the best results, experiment freely, work on separate layers, and use non-destructive editing methods so you can fine-tune changes anytime.

## Improve filter performance

When you’re working with large files or complex effects, try these tips to help photoeditor run smoothly:

**Technique****Description****Benefits**
Test on small areas Apply filters to a small portion of the image first Saves time and allows for experimentation
Apply to individual channels Try applying effects to separate RGB channels rather than the composite channel Can reduce memory requirements and create unique effects
Free up memory Use the Purge command before running heavy filters Provides more available RAM for filter processing
Increase memory allocation Go to Preferences>Performance to let photoeditor use more memory.Speeds up filter performance and responsiveness.
Close other applications Exit unnecessary programs when working with complex effects Makes more system resources available to photoeditor

Filters may look different when applied to individual channels rather than the full composite image, especially those that create random effects.

## Tips for consistent results

*   Work on a duplicate layer to preserve your original image.
*   Keep effects subtle to maintain image realism.
*   Save successful filter combinations as Actions to reuse them easily.


====================================================================================================

## 419. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/smart-filters/sharpening-overview.html

Learn about enhancing edge definition and improving clarity in photos and artwork with professional photoeditor tools.

## What sharpening does

Sharpening in photoeditor makes details appear clearer by increasing contrast where different tones meet. It can correct slight softness caused by a camera, a scanner, or resizing, giving photos and artwork a more defined look.

## Best practices for effective sharpening

To get the most out of sharpening, keep a few guidelines in mind:

*   Sharpening cannot restore missing detail or fix areas that are out of focus, so focus only on areas that should be sharp.
*   Apply sharpening gradually to avoid halos, jagged edges, or noise.

## Sharpening methods

Once you’re ready to apply sharpening, photoeditor offers several options. Each one provides a slightly different approach, so you can pick the method that best suits your image and workflow:

*   Sharpen applies basic sharpening to the entire image.
*   Sharpen Edges targets only the edges while leaving flat areas mostly unchanged.
*   Sharpen More applies stronger overall sharpening than the basic option.
*   Smart Sharpen provides precise control and reduces noise.
*   Unsharp Mask offers a fast, traditional sharpening method.


====================================================================================================

## 420. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/smart-filters/sharpen-controls-with-smart-sharpen.html

Learn how to adjust the sharpness of an image or pixel-based layer using the Smart Sharpen filter in professional photoeditor.

*   Convert your layer to a Smart Object so you can edit or remove sharpening later. To do this, right-click the layer in the Layers panel and select Convert to Smart Object.
*   Zoom in to 100% to accurately see the sharpening effect.

Select the layer you want to sharpen in the Layers panel.

Select Filter>Sharpen>Smart Sharpen to open the dialog box.

Adjust the sharpening controls:

*   Amount: Increase or decrease the overall strength of the sharpening. Higher values add more contrast along edges.
*   Radius: Set the number of pixels around each edge that are affected. A small value sharpens fine details, and a larger value sharpens broader areas.
*   Reduce Noise: Minimize grain or artifacts while sharpening.

Select a blur type under Remove:

*   Gaussian Blur: Standard sharpening, like Unsharp Mask.
*   Lens Blur: Detects edges and details for finer sharpening with fewer halos.
*   Motion Blur: Reduces blur from camera shake or subject movement.

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 421. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/smart-filters/sharpen-images-with-unsharp-mask.html

Learn how to enhance detail and improve clarity in professional photoeditor using the Unsharp Mask filter.

*   Make sure your image is in **RGB Color**, **CMYK Color**, or **Grayscale** mode.
*   To preserve the original image, work on a duplicate layer.

Select the layer you want to sharpen in the Layers panel.

Select Filter>Sharpen>Unsharp Mask.

In the Unsharp Mask dialog box, adjust the following settings:

*   Amount: Set the intensity of the sharpening effect. You can enter values from 1 to 500%.
*   Radius: Set how many pixels around edges are affected. Enter a value between 0.1 and 250 pixels.
*   Threshold: Set how different pixels must be from surrounding areas before sharpening is applied. Enter a value between 0 and 255.

Select OK to apply the filter.


====================================================================================================

## 422. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/smart-filters/sharpen-a-selection.html

Learn how to sharpen only a selected part of your image in professional photoeditor for more precise control.

Open your image and make a selection with a tool such as the Lasso tool, or Quick Selection tool.

With the selection active, select and hold the Blur tool, then select the Sharpen tool.

In the options bar, adjust the following:

*   Set an appropriate Size and Hardness in the Brush Setting dialog box.
*   Set the Strength of the sharpening effect to control how pronounced the sharpening appears.
*   Select Protect Detail to minimize pixelation and maintain image quality.
*   Select Sample All Layers if you want sharpening to use data from all visible layers, and leave it unchecked to sharpen only the active layer.

Drag the Sharpen tool over the selected area.

Press Ctrl + D (Windows) or Command + D (macOS) to deselect.


====================================================================================================

## 423. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/smart-filters/sharpen-image-using-edge-mask.html

Learn how to sharpen an image in professional photoeditor using an edge mask created with the High Pass filter for controlled, selective sharpening.

Edge masks are ideal for sharpening only the outlines of objects while keeping smooth areas like skies or skin free of artifacts.

Duplicate your image layer by pressing Ctrl + J (Windows) or Command + J (macOS).

Select the duplicated layer and then select Filter>Other>High Pass.

In the High Pass dialog, set a radius between 1-3 pixels and select OK.

Change the layer blend mode to Overlay in the Layers panel to create your edge mask.

Select the Layer masks icon from the Layers panel.

Select the layer mask, then choose Filter>Blur>Gaussian Blur with a small radius of 0.5-1.0 pixels to soften the edge transitions.

Use a black brush on the layer mask to paint over areas where you don't want sharpening applied.

Adjust the layer Opacity to control the overall intensity of the sharpening effect.


====================================================================================================

## 424. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/blur-sharpen-filters/overview-of-adding-blur-to-images.html

Learn about blur effects in professional photoeditor and how they can soften edges, reduce detail, or create depth and artistic focus.

Blurring changes how sharpness is perceived in an image. You can use blur to smooth distracting details, draw attention to a subject, or create artistic effects such as motion or shallow depth of field.

## Types of blur effects

photoeditor offers several ways to apply blur, depending on your creative or corrective needs:

*   Blur Gallery: Interactive, on-canvas tools like Field Blur, Iris Blur, and Tilt-Shift that let you apply blur selectively and control it visually.
*   Lens Blur: Simulates the depth-of-field and bokeh of real camera lenses for realistic results.
*   Traditional blur filters: Found under Filter>Blur, including options like Gaussian Blur and Motion Blur.
*   Blur Tool: A brush-based tool that lets you paint blur onto specific areas of an image.

## Creative uses of blur

*   Direct attention by blurring less critical areas and keeping the subject sharp.
*   Create depth by applying different blur levels to foreground, midground, and background elements.
*   Simulate camera effects such as Motion Blur or Tilt-Shift looks.
*   Add artistic expression with dreamy, abstract, or textured styles.

## Best practices

*   Blur does not restore lost detail but only reduces sharpness.
*   Over-blurring can make images look unnatural or lose context.
*   For flexibility, apply blur to a Smart Object or a duplicate layer so you can adjust or remove it later.


====================================================================================================

## 425. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/blur-sharpen-filters/create-depth-of-field-with-lens-blur.html

Learn how to apply the Lens Blur filter in professional photoeditor to create a depth-of-field effect.

Select the layer you want to blur in the Layers panel.

Select Filter>Blur>Lens Blur.

Customize your settings in the Lens Blur dialog box:

*   Depth Map**:** Select None from the Source menu for an even blur across the image or pick Transparency or Layer Mask if you’ve set up a depth map.
*   Blur Focal Distance**:** Drag the slider to control which part of the image stays sharp. Pixels near this value remain clear, while others blur gradually.
*   Iris**:** Select a shape from the Shape menu and adjust Radius to control blur strength, Blade Curvature to smooth edges, and Rotation to change orientation.
*   Specular Highlights**:** Increase Brightness to enhance highlights and adjust Threshold to define which bright areas become highlights.
*   Noise**:** Drag the Amount slider to add texture to the image. 
*   Distribution: Select Uniform or Gaussian and check Monochromatic to keep it in grayscale.

![Image 1: photoeditor Lens Blur dialog box with controls for Depth Map source, Iris shape, Specular Highlights, and Noise displayed in the right panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-Lens-Blur-dialog-box-with-controls-for-D?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1280)

Adjust the Lens Blur dialog box's depth map, iris shape, and blur settings for custom focus effects.

Select OK to apply the effect.


====================================================================================================

## 426. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/blur-sharpen-filters/blur-specific-areas-with-the-blur-tool.html

Learn how to use the Blur tool in professional photoeditor to soften edges or reduce detail in selected parts of an image.

Select the Blur tool from the Tools panel.

In the Options bar, set up your tool:

*   Select Toggle Brush Settings panel>Brush Settings to adjust the brush Size and Hardness.
*   Drag the Strength slider to control the blur effect, 10–30% for subtle softening, 50% or higher for stronger blur.
*   Select Sample All Layers if you want to blur based on all visible layers, or leave it unchecked to affect only the active layer.

Drag across the areas you want to blur, intensifying the effect with each stroke.

If you blur too much, press Ctrl+Z (Windows) or Command+Z (macOS) to undo your last stroke.

For natural results in portraits, use a low Strength setting, around 10–20% and build up gradually to keep skin texture intact.


====================================================================================================

## 427. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/blur-sharpen-filters/enhance-edge-contrast-with-the-sharpen-tool.html

Learn how to sharpen specific areas in professional photoeditor by enhancing edge contrast with the Sharpen tool.

Select and hold the Blur tool, then select the Sharpen tool.

In the Options bar, set up your tool:

*   Select Toggle Brush Settings panel>Brush Settings to adjust the brush Size and Hardness.
*   Drag the Strength slider to control sharpening intensity.
*   Select Protect Detail to minimize pixelation.
*   Select Sample All Layers to sharpen based on all visible layers or leave unchecked to sharpen only the active layer.

Drag over the areas you want to sharpen, and the effect builds with each stroke.


====================================================================================================

## 428. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/reshape-and-distort-images-with-transform-warp.html

Learn how to use professional photoeditor to reshape and refine images using flexible mesh grids, control points, and adjustable warp settings for precise results.

Use the Transform Warp feature to visually reshape and fine-tune image layers. Adjust the mesh grid, split or move control points, and apply custom or preset settings to achieve smooth, detailed transformations.

Select Window>Layers and then select the layer or area you want to warp in the Layers panel.

Select Edit>Transform>Warp.

Select the gear  icon in the Options bar to see additional visual Guide options:

*   Set the warp guide display to Auto Show Guides, Always Show Guides (default), or Never Show Guides.
*   Change the Color and Opacity of the visual guides.
*   Use the Density option to set how many lines appear between each Split Warp line. The default value is 2.

Select a style from the Warp dropdown menu in the Options bar to apply a preset.

Create a custom warp mesh by choosing a grid size from the Grid dropdown in the Options bar:

*   Select a grid size - Default, 3x3, 4x4, or 5x5.
*   Select Custom and enter the number of columns and rows in the Custom Grid Size dialog box.

Add grid lines by splitting the warp:

*   In the Options bar, select a Split icon to add predefined splits: Split Warp Horizontally, Split Warp Vertically, or Split Warp Crosswise. 
*   Select the mesh to add a grid line.

Refine the warp.

![Image 1: photoeditor workspace showing mesh grid and control points used to refine image transformation.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/photoeditor-workspace-showing-mesh-grid-and-control-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Adjust the mesh grid to fine-tune specific image areas for smoother transformations.

When finished, press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS).

## Edit and adjust the warp

Manipulate the mesh:

*   Drag control points, a segment of the bounding box or mesh, or an area within the mesh to reshape.
*   Use control point handles to adjust curves, like those in vector graphics.
*   Select a grid line to activate control points or select an anchor point (where grid lines intersect) to edit its surrounding points.
*   Drag points to warp the image.

Select or deselect points:

*   Select multiple points by Shift-clicking anchor points or dragging over them while holding Shift. A rectangle appears around selected points.
*   Deselect points by Shift-clicking active anchor points or Shift-dragging over the active points.

Delete grid lines or points:

*   Press the Delete key to remove a grid line when its control points are visible.
*   Press the Delete key to remove both horizontal and vertical lines through an anchor point after selecting the anchor.

Adjust warp setting:

*   Change the orientation of a warp style using the Change Warp Orientation icon in the Options bar.
*   Change the reference point by clicking a square in the Reference Point Locator icon in the Options bar.
*   Enter numeric warp values in the Bend, H, and V boxes in the Options bar. (Not available if you choose None or Custom from the Warp dropdown.)

## Customize warp guide display options

With the Transform Warp active, select the gear  icon in the Options bar to access visual guide options.

Select when to show visual guides: Auto Show Guides, Always Show Guides, or Never Show Guides.

Adjust the Color and Opacity of the visual guides as needed.

Set the Density option to control how many lines appear between each Split Warp line (default is 2).

To toggle the visibility of the warp mesh and control points while working, select View>Extras.

## Work with warp control points

Select a grid line to activate control points for editing that section of the warp.

Select an anchor point (at grid line intersections) to edit the control points surrounding that anchor.

To delete a selected grid line with visible control points, press Delete.

To delete both horizontal and vertical grid lines passing through an anchor point, select the anchor point and press Delete.


====================================================================================================

## 429. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/warp-a-layer-wth-cylindrical-transform.html

#### Learn how to curve flat designs in professional photoeditor so they fit naturally on round objects with resizing, curvature, and perspective controls.

![Image 1: photoeditor warp dropdown with Cylinder selected, showing a floral label wrapped around a dropper bottle. A bottle with a dropper.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/whats-new-detail-transform-warp-cylindrical-ps?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=200&wid=1200)

The Cylindrical Transform Warp is beneficial for packaging design and product mock-ups, allowing you to see how labels and artwork appear on bottles, cans, and other cylindrical products. You can also apply it to text layers for creative layouts.Apply the Cylinder warp style to wrap artwork naturally around a bottle for realistic packaging previews.

Select a layer or region in the image you want to warp.

Select Edit>Transform>Warp.

Select the Switch between free transform and warp modes icon in the Options bar.

Select the Warp dropdown menu and then select Cylinder.

Use the corner controls (located at the bottom-left and top-right) to resize the entire selection. Hold Shift-key while dragging corner controls to constrain proportions.

Adjust the curvature using the center controls. Top-center changes both upper and lower boundary arcs together, while bottom-center adjusts only the lower boundary arc.

Control the perspective by moving the center control point to the right or left.

Press Enter (Windows) or return (macOS) to apply the warp.

## 430. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/get-precise-distortions-with-split-warp.html

Understand the ways to refine warps in professional photoeditor by splitting meshes, adjusting Bezier handles, and controlling anchor point behavior.

Find keyboard shortcuts and techniques for precise control when using Split Warp to create complex distortions in photoeditor.

### Split Warp gestures and modifiers

**UI option****Windows****macOS**
Split warp crosswise at the cursor location Ctrl-click on the mesh command-click on the mesh
Split warp vertically near the horizontal grid line Ctrl-click near the edge of the horizontal grid line command-click near the edge of the horizontal grid line
Split warp horizontally near the vertical grid line Ctrl-click near the edge of the vertical grid line command-click near the edge of the vertical grid line
Quickly toggle through split warp options without returning to the menu bar.Ctrl-click command-click

### Convert Warp handle modes and toggles

**UI option****Windows****macOS**
Bezier handle modes Move control handles independently while applying Split Warp using shortcuts.Move control handles independently while applying Split Warp using shortcuts.
Unison (circle icon)All handles move together when one is moved.All handles move together when one is moved.
Independent (square icon)Each handle moves independently of the other handles at the anchor point.Each handle moves independently of the other handles at the anchor point.
Toggle handle state Alt-click on the anchor point.option-click on the anchor point.
Convert Warp Anchor Point Ctrl-right-click on the anchor point and then select Convert Warp Anchor Point.ctrl-right-click on the anchor point and then select Convert Warp Anchor Point.

### Anchor point defaults and multi-select

**UI option****Windows****macOS**
Corner anchor points Independent movement by default.Independent movement by default.
Edge/Side anchor points Unison movement of the vertical/horizontal pair by default.Unison movement of the vertical/horizontal pair by default.
Interior/Inner anchor points Unison movement for each handle by default.Unison movement for each handle by default.
Multi-select Shift-click multiple anchor points to change movement for all.shift-click numerous anchor points to change movement for all.

## 431. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/distort-specific-image-areas-with-puppet-warp.html

Learn how to use professional photoeditor to reshape parts of an image with precision pins while keeping the surrounding areas unaffected.

Puppet Warp overlays a mesh that you can adjust for subtle edits, such as shaping hair, or major changes, like repositioning arms or legs. It works on image layers, layer masks, and vector masks. To distort non-destructively, apply Puppet Warp to [Smart Objects](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/create-manage-layers/smart-objects/smart-objects-overview-and-benefits.html).

Select Window>Layers and in the Layers panel, select the layer or mask you want to transform.

Choose Edit>Puppet Warp.

Adjust the mesh settings in the Options bar:  
• Mode: Set the mesh elasticity. Select Distort for flexible warping of wide-angle images or texture maps.  
• Density: Control the spacing of mesh points. More points give precision but slow performance.  
• Expansion: Expand or contract the mesh edge.  
• Show Mesh: Turn off to view only pins for a cleaner preview. Press H to temporarily hide adjustment pins for a clearer view.

Add pins in the image window to transform the required areas and anchor others in place.

Drag pins to warp the mesh. To reveal overlapped mesh areas, select Pin Depth icons in the Options bar.

Delete a pin by selecting it and pressing the Delete key, or remove all pins using the Remove All Pins option in the context menu.

To rotate the mesh around a pin:  
 • For fixed-degree rotation, press Alt (Windows) or o ption (macOS), position the cursor near, and drag when a circle appears.

• For automatic rotation based on the selected Mode, select Auto from the Rotate dropdown in the Options bar.

Press Enter to complete the action.


====================================================================================================

## 432. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/overview-of-liquify-filter.html

Learn about the Liquify filter in professional photoeditor, which lets you reshape images for retouching and creative effects.

## What is the Liquify filter?

The Liquify filter provides an interactive workspace where you can push, pull, rotate, pucker, or bloat pixels, often used to:

*   Adjust features in portraits.
*   Create artistic distortions.
*   Apply exaggerated design effects.

## Key features

*   Use brush-based tools to push or warp pixels directly on the canvas.
*   Adjust brush Size, Pressure, and Density in the Liquify panel for more precise control.
*   Apply the filter to a Smart Object for non-destructive editing.
*   Toggle the Preview checkbox to compare original and edited results.

## Availability

*   Works on RGB, CMYK, and Grayscale images.
*   Can be applied to layers and Smart Objects.
*   Performance improves with GPU acceleration.


====================================================================================================

## 433. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/overview-of-distortion-tools.html

Learn about the distortion tools in professional photoeditor that let you reshape, stretch, and reposition parts of an image for creative or corrective edits.

photoeditor includes tools for both correction and creative transformation. These tools help fix lens-related issues such as barrel distortion or vignetting and let you warp or reshape content for artistic effects.

## Types of distortion tools in photoeditor

Distortion tools can be categorized into two main groups:

### Correction-focused distortion tools

*   Lens Correction: Fix barrel distortion, pincushion distortion, chromatic aberration, and vignetting.
*   Perspective Warp: Adjust perspective in photos of buildings, architecture, or objects.
*   Adaptive Wide Angle: Correct distortions in panoramic or wide-angle photos.

### Creative distortion tools

*   Liquify: Reshape specific areas of your image with brush-based tools. Push, pull, twirl, bloat, or pucker parts of your image for creative effects. Use Face-Aware Liquify to easily adjust facial features with intuitive sliders.
*   Warp: Bend and stretch your image using a flexible grid. Adjust control points to create custom distortions and transformations.
*   Puppet Warp: Reposition specific parts of your image while keeping others in place. Place a mesh over your image and move points to create natural-looking deformations.
*   Distort and Skew: Transform specific areas of your image with precision. Stretch or slant parts of your content to adjust perspective or create custom visual effects.

## Lens distortion types

*   Barrel Distortion: Straighten lines that curve outward from the center. Correct the fish-eye effect often seen in wide-angle shots.
*   Pincushion Distortion: Adjust lines that bend inward toward the image center. Fix the pinched look used in telephoto images.
*   Chromatic Aberration: Remove unwanted color fringes along high-contrast edges. Aligning color channels accurately enhances image clarity.
*   Vignetting: Balance brightness across your entire image. Lighten dark corners to create a more even exposure throughout your photo.


====================================================================================================

## 434. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/use-liquify-to-distort-an-image.html

Learn how to apply distortions with the Liquify filter in professional photoeditor for creative effects or subtle retouching.

## Apply distortions with the Liquify filter

Open your image and select the layer you want to distort.

Select Filter>Liquify from the menu bar to open the Liquify dialog box.

In the Brush Tool Options panel, adjust brush settings such as Size and Pressure.

Drag over the image to apply distortions:

*   Use Forward Warp to push pixels in the direction you drag.
*   Use Pucker to contract pixels toward the center of the brush.
*   Use Bloat to expand pixels outward.
*   Use Twirl to rotate pixels clockwise or counterclockwise.

Select OK to apply the changes to your image.

Convert your layer to a Smart Object to edit distortions non-destructively.

For optimal performance, turn on the graphics processor.


====================================================================================================

## 435. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/freeze-or-thaw-areas.html

Learn how to protect parts of your image from distortion or restore them for editing in professional photoeditor using the Liquify filter.

Select the layer you want to distort in the Layers panel.

Select Filter>Liquify to open the Liquify dialog box.

Select the Freeze Mask icon from the toolbar.

Adjust the brush size using the Size slider in the Brush Tool Options area.

Paint over the areas you want to protect. Frozen areas are covered with a red overlay.

To remove frozen areas, select the Thaw Mask icon from the toolbar and paint over the overlay to clear it.

Select OK to apply the changes and exit the Liquify dialog box.

## 436. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/work-with-meshes.html

Learn about meshes in professional photoeditor, which provide a flexible grid that lets you precisely distort or reshape parts of an image while keeping other areas intact.

## What are meshes in photoeditor?

Meshes are grid patterns that appear over your image in tools such as Liquify, Warp, and Puppet Warp. They act as a visual framework, showing how pixels move, stretch, or compress as you edit.

## Benefits of using meshes

*   Meshes offer several advantages when working with distortion effects:
*   Visual feedback: Track how your edits affect the image structure.
*   Precision: Maintain proportions and alignment more easily.
*   Consistency: Apply the same distortion pattern across multiple edits.
*   Progress tracking: See which areas have been modified and to what extent.

## Mesh options in Liquify

*   In the Liquify dialog box, you can:
*   Select Show Mesh to toggle mesh visibility.
*   Adjust Mesh Size for finer or coarser grids.
*   Change Mesh Color to improve contrast.
*   Save meshes for reuse or load previously saved meshes for consistent effects.


====================================================================================================

## 437. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/work-with-backdrops.html

Learn about backdrops in professional photoeditor, which let you compare distortions with the original image or other layers in the Liquify filter.

## What are backdrops?

Backdrops display a reference image behind your working layer in the Liquify filter. They help you make precise edits, align distortions, and compare before and after states while you work.

## Backdrop options in Liquify

In the Liquify dialog box, expand the Backdrop section:

*   Use: Select a source layer as your backdrop.
*   Mode: Choose how the backdrop blends with your current layer, for example, Behind or In Front.
*   Opacity: Adjust the transparency to balance the visibility of your edits and the reference image.

## Tips for working with backdrops

*   Lower opacity, around 50% is helpful for portrait retouching to maintain natural proportions.
*   Toggle the backdrop on and off to check your progress.
*   Use backdrops with multi-layer projects to align edits more accurately.

Backdrops are separate from the Show Mesh option. Meshes display a grid to help you track distortions, while backdrops provide a visual reference image.


====================================================================================================

## 438. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/reconstruct-distortions.html

Learn how to restore parts of a distorted image to their original appearance using the Reconstruct tool in the Liquify filter in professional photoeditor.

Use the Reconstruct Tool when you’ve applied distortions with Liquify and want to restore specific areas or reset the entire image to its original form. It’s helpful for fine-tuning facial adjustments or softening overdone effects without starting over.

Select Filter>Liquify.

In the Liquify dialog, select the Reconstruct tool from the toolbar.

In the Brush Reconstruct Options section, do one of the following:

*   Select Reconstruct to gradually restore the areas you brush over.
*   Select Restore All to reset all distortions in the image.

Adjust your brush size to control the restoration area.

Select OK to apply the changes.

## 439. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/replace-the-sky-in-images.html

Learn more about replacing and enhancing skies in professional photoeditor using the Sky Replacement.

## Replace a sky in your image

Select Edit**>**Sky Replacement.

In the Sky Replacement dialog, select a sky from the presets or add your own.

Adjust the sliders to refine the sky and blend the foreground with the background.

*   Shift edge**:** Determines where the border between the sky and the original image begins.
*   Fade edge**:** Sets the amount of feathering from the sky image to the original photo along the edges.
*   Brightness**:** Adjusts the brightness of the sky.
*   Temperature**:** Adjusts the temperature of the sky warmer or cooler.
*   Scale**:** Resizes the sky image.
*   Flip**:** Flips the sky image horizontally.
*   Lighting mode**:** Determines the blending mode used for lighting adjustments.
*   Foreground lighting**:** Controls contrast in the foreground.
*   Edge lighting**:** Adjusts lighting applied to object edges; a setting of zero makes no adjustments.
*   Color adjustment**:** Adjusts how strongly the foreground is harmonized with sky colors.
*   Output**:** Selects whether the result is placed on new layers or a duplicate flattened layer.

You can also adjust the sky by following tools:

*   Sky Move : Moves the sky within the frame for better composition.
*   Sky Brush: Paints over areas to refine where the new sky is applied.
*   Hand : Pans around the image at higher magnifications.
*   Zoom<insert icon here>: Zooms in or out to adjust details with precision.

Select OK.

## Improve edge quality in sky replacement

In the improved sky replacement workflow, the foreground-background contrast around thin objects is better preserved. In comparison, halo artifacts are reduced when bright skies are replaced with darker ones or vice versa. Additionally, a new Edge Lighting Group is added to the layer stack, which includes adjustment layers and a refined mask to enhance the overall quality of the result further.

To control the edge adjustments:

Open the Sky Replacement dialog box.

Use the Edge Lighting slider to set contrast on object edges. Larger values provide higher contrast and reduce halos.


====================================================================================================

## 440. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/select-and-manage-sky-presets.html

Learn more about managing, importing, and downloading skies in professional photoeditor.

Select Edit>Sky Replacement>Sky Preset.

Select Setting icon, then in the Sky Preset**menu**, you can set the following options:

*   Get More Skies:
    *   Import Images: Creates one or more new sky presets from your own sky images. You can import multiple images at once as new presets.
    *   Import Presets: Imports existing sky presets from one or more .sky files.
    *   Download Free Skies: Opens [professional Discover](https://www.professional.com/learn/photoeditor/web/free-professional-photoeditor-sky-presets?learnIn=1&locale=en), where you can view and download additional free sky images or sky presets.

*   Create New Sky Group: Creates a new group of presets for various sky use cases you frequently work with.
*   Rename Sky: Changes the name of a sky from the list.
*   Delete Sky: Deletes one or more skies from the list.
*   Show Recents: Displays recently accessed skies in the list.
*   Append Default Skies: Adds the default skies back to the list of sky presets.
*   Export Selected Skies: Exports chosen sky presets to a .sky file and saves them on your machine.

## Import downloaded skies

After downloading a .sky file, import it into Sky Replacement by doing any of the following:

*   Use the sky preset menu to import manually (Mac/Win).
*   Drag and drop .sky files onto the photoeditor canvas while Sky Replacement isn’t open (Mac/Win).
*   Drag and drop .sky files onto the blank title bar area (Mac only).

Sharpen and denoise images with unmatched precision in professional photoeditor, with Topaz Labs.

Recover detail and sharpness while reducing blur and shake with AI Sharpen.

## 442. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/apply-texture-and-surface-effects-to-filters.html

Use custom textures and surface controls to create realistic material effects in artistic and sketch filters.

Texture and surface controls add realistic material qualities to filtered images, making them appear painted on canvas, brick, or other textured surfaces. These settings are available in Rough Pastels, Underpainting, Glass, Conté Crayon, and Texturizer filters.

These texture options work with filters that simulate traditional art media or view images through textured surfaces. Each filter provides controls for scaling, relief depth, light direction, and texture inversion. For an overview of all available filters and their categories, see [Filter Gallery overview](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/filter-gallery.html). Before applying filters, learn how to [apply filters in photoeditor](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/get-started-with-filters/apply-filters.html).

All custom textures must be saved in photoeditor format. Most filters use only the grayscale information from color files to create the texture effect.

## Load a custom texture

After loading a texture, you can adjust its appearance using the surface controls. These controls determine how prominently the texture appears and how light interacts with its surface.

## Configure texture surface controls

Preview your changes in real time within the filter dialog box. Try inverting the texture if the lighting effect appears opposite to your desired result.

The Relief control is not available in all filters. Glass and some other filters offer alternative depth or distortion controls instead. Experiment with different texture sources and settings to achieve unique surface effects that complement your image.


====================================================================================================

## 443. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/artistic-stylize-filters/correct-lens-distortion-and-perspective.html

Apply automatic or manual corrections using lens profiles and custom adjustments to fix optical defects.

The Lens Correction filter addresses optical defects that occur during capture, enabling you to straighten lines, balance exposure, and eliminate color fringing for more accurate images.

Use the Lens Correction filter to fix barrel distortion, pincushion distortion, vignetting, chromatic aberration, and perspective issues caused by camera tilt. The filter offers automatic correction through lens profiles matched to your camera and lens combination, plus manual controls for precise adjustments. Works with 8-bit and 16-bit per channel images in RGB or Grayscale mode.

## Understanding common lens defects

**Barrel distortion** causes straight lines to bow outward from the image center, creating a bulging effect common in wide-angle lenses. **Pincushion distortion** produces the opposite result, with straight lines bending inward toward the center, typically seen in telephoto lenses.

**Vignetting** darkens the corners and edges of an image due to light falloff around the lens perimeter. **Chromatic aberration** appears as colored fringes along high-contrast edges, caused by the lens focusing different wavelengths of light at slightly different distances.

**Perspective distortion** occurs when you tilt the camera up or down, causing vertical lines to converge or horizontal lines to appear skewed.

## Apply automatic correction using lens profiles

Select Search Online in the Lens Profiles section to download additional profiles created by the photoeditor community. Store downloaded profiles locally by clicking the panel menu and choosing Save Online Profile Locally.

For lens profiles not available in photoeditor or for non-supported camera models, create custom profiles using the [professional Lens Profile Creator](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/digital-negative.html#resources). A complete list of supported lenses is available in the [Lens profile support article](https://helpx.professional.com/x-productkb/multi/lens-profile-support.html).

## Correct distortion and aberration manually

Zoom in on the image preview when adjusting chromatic aberration to see color fringing more clearly and make precise corrections.

You can also use the Remove Distortion tool directly on the image—drag toward the center to correct barrel distortion or toward the edges to correct pincushion distortion.

## Adjust perspective and rotation

When adjusting perspective or angle, deselect Auto Scale Image on the Auto Correction tab if you want to avoid unintended scaling of your final image.

## Customize the preview and grid

## Save and load correction settings

The Lens Default option in the Settings menu becomes available when you open an image with matching camera, lens, focal length, and f-stop metadata. The Previous Conversion option applies the settings used in your last lens correction session.

For more advanced perspective corrections in architectural photography, explore [Neural Filters](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/neural-filters.html) for AI-powered adjustments. To address image noise and JPEG artifacts that may become visible after lens corrections, see the separate article on reducing image noise.


====================================================================================================

## 444. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/neural-filters/overview-of-neural-filters.html

Learn how Neural Filters in professional photoeditor use professional Sensei AI to colorize images, edit portraits, adjust depth, and apply other creative image effects.

## What are Neural Filters?

Neural Filters are AI-powered tools in photoeditor that use machine learning to render creative and corrective effects to images. They provide a dedicated workspace that offers a library of filters to retouch, enhance, and transform images in a few clicks.

You can experiment with looks, restore photos, or test upcoming technology without permanently changing your original image.

## Neural Filter categories

photoeditor organizes Neural Filters into three distinct categories based on their development status:

*   Featured: Released filters ready for everyday use.
*   Beta: Filters still in development that you can try and provide feedback on.
*   Wait list: Preview filters that are planned for future release.

## Why use Neural Filters

*   Retouch portraits by adjusting expression, age, or gaze.
*   Apply artistic or stylistic changes such as colorization or style transfer.
*   Enhance depth or atmosphere with blur and lighting adjustments.
*   Explore experimental filters and upcoming technology.


====================================================================================================

## 446. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/effects-filters/neural-filters/neural-filter-categories-and-output-options.html

Learn about the types of Neural Filters available in professional photoeditor and how each output option affects your edits.

## Neural Filters categories

**Category****Description**
All filters All available filters, including both stable and Beta versions.
Beta Filters available for testing and still under development. Results may be unpredictable.
Wait list Filters planned for future release. Preview available with an option to select I’m Interested to vote for features.

**Output****What it does**
Current layer Applies the Neural Filter directly to the active layer, replacing its pixels.
New layer Creates a duplicate layer with the filter applied independently of the original.
New layer masked Duplicates the layer with a mask isolating the filter effect for easy masking and blending.
Smart filter Converts the layer to a Smart Object and applies the filter non-destructively as a Smart Filter.
New document Opens a new photoeditor document containing only the filtered result.

## When to use each option

*   Current layer: For edits that do not require preserving original pixels or non-destructive editing.
*   Smart filter: For non-destructive workflows with adjustable settings.
*   New layer masked: For manual control over blending or precise masking.
*   New document: For using the filtered result as the start of a new file.
*   New layer: For simple edits while keeping the original layer intact.

## 448. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/overview-of-guides-grids-and-smart-guides.html

Understand how guides, grids, and smart guides help you precisely position and align elements in your professional photoeditor projects for professional layouts and designs.

Precise alignment and positioning are essential when creating professional designs in photoeditor. photoeditor offers three powerful alignment tools: guides, grids, and smart guides, which help you create perfectly aligned layouts without guesswork.

These visual aids appear as non-printing lines or points that float over your image, providing reference points for positioning elements with precision. Each serves a distinct purpose while working together to help you create polished, professional designs.

## Guides

Guides are customizable, movable lines that help you position specific elements with precision. Unlike grids, which have a fixed structure, guides can be placed exactly where you need them.

### Key characteristics of guides:

*   Appear as non-printing colored lines that float over your image
*   Can be positioned horizontally or vertically at precise coordinates
*   Can be moved, locked, or removed as needed
*   Can be customized with different colors to organize your layout
*   Elements snap to guides when dragged within 8 screen pixels

Guides are particularly useful when you need to align elements across different layers or create custom layouts that don't follow a regular grid pattern. For example, when designing a website mockup, you might place guides to mark the boundaries of different content sections.

## Grids

The grid provides a consistent, evenly-spaced pattern of lines across your entire canvas. It's ideal for creating symmetrical layouts or ensuring consistent spacing between elements.

### Key characteristics of grids:

*   Display as a uniform pattern of non-printing lines or dots across the entire canvas
*   Maintain consistent spacing between lines
*   Help create symmetrical layouts and maintain proportions
*   Apply the same grid pattern to all images
*   Elements snap to grid lines when the snap feature is enabled

Grids are particularly valuable when designing interfaces, layouts with repeating elements, or when you need to maintain consistent proportions throughout your design.

## Smart guides

Smart guides are dynamic alignment aids that appear automatically when needed. They help you align objects relative to each other without requiring manual guide placement.

### Key characteristics of smart guides:

*   Appear automatically when creating or moving shapes, selections, or layers
*   Help align objects to each other's edges or centers
*   Show distances between elements when using modifier keys
*   Provide instant feedback about alignment relationships
*   Can be toggled on or off as needed

Smart guides are especially useful when you need to align multiple elements quickly or position objects at specific distances from each other.


====================================================================================================

## 450. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/create-guides.html

Position elements precisely using nonprinting guide lines and grids that help align objects and create symmetrical layouts in professional photoeditor.

Guides in photoeditor help you position elements precisely in your design. They appear as nonprinting lines that float over your image, making it easier to align objects and create balanced layouts.

Unlike the grid, which displays a fixed pattern across your entire canvas, guides can be placed exactly where you need them. Guides can be horizontal or vertical, and you can create as many as you need for your project.

## Create guides from the horizontal or vertical ruler

Select View>Rulers.

Select anywhere in the horizontal or vertical ruler and drag into the image, to create a horizontal or vertical guide.

Hold Shift while dragging to snap the guide to the ruler ticks.

## Create guides from the View menu

Select View>Guides>New Guide.

Select the Orientation and Color, and enter a Position value in the New guide dialog.

Select OK.

## 451. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/move-guides.html

Reposition guides precisely using the Move tool to align elements in your professional photoeditor document.

Guides help you position elements with precision in your photoeditor documents. Once placed, you can easily move guides to adjust your layout as needed.

Guides appear as non-printing lines that float over your image. Moving guides allows you to create precise layouts and alignments without affecting your actual image content. Guides can be repositioned at any time during your editing process.

## Move a guide

Select the Move tool.

Position your pointer over the guide you want to move, you’ll view a double-headed arrow **(icon will be added here)** cursor.

Drag the guide to a new position.

Hold Alt (Windows) or Option (macOS) while dragging the guide to change a guide from horizontal to vertical (or vice versa).

Hold Shift while dragging to make the guide snap to ruler ticks.

## Move multiple guides

Select the Move tool.

Hold Shift + Alt (Windows) or Shift + Option (macOS) and select on the guides you want to select.

Drag to move all selected guides together.

Release the mouse button when the guides are in the desired position.

## Edit guide position numerically

Select a guide with the Move tool.

Select View>Guides>Edit Selected Guides.

Adjust the Position value and select OK.

## 452. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/alignment-grids-guides/edit-guides.html

Modify guide properties such as position, orientation, and color to enhance your layout precision and workflow efficiency in professional photoeditor.

Editing guides allows you to customize their appearance and position without having to delete and recreate them. This is particularly useful when working on complex layouts that require precise alignment or when collaborating with others who may need visual cues with specific colors.

Select View>New Guide to open the New Guide dialog.

Set the Orientation to either Horizontal or Vertical and specify the Position value.

Select the Move tool.

Hold Alt + Shift (Windows) or Option + Shift (macOS) to select multiple guides.

Right-click on any selected guide and choose Edit Selected Guides.

Adjust the Orientation (Horizontal or Vertical).

Select a preset color or select Custom, to choose a color in the Color picker (guides color), and select OK.

Select OK.

## 457. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/manage-measurement-scales.html

Learn more about setting a measurement scale, creating presets for frequently used scales, and deleting presets in photoeditor.

## Set measurement scale

Open an image.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Set Measurement Scale**>**Custom. The Ruler tool is automatically selected.

Enter a value in the Pixel Length text box.

Enter the Logical Length and Logical Units to set equal to the Pixel Length.  
**Example**: If the Pixel Length is 50 and you want a scale of 50 pixels per micron, enter 1 for Logical Length and type microns as the unit.

Select OK in the Measurement Scale dialog box to apply the scale to image.

Select File**>**Save to save the measurement scale setting with the document.

## Create a measurement scale preset

Open an image.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Set Measurement Scale**>**Custom**.**

Define a measurement scale.

Select Save Preset and enter a name for the preset.

Select OK. The new preset is added to Image**>**Analysis**>**Set Measurement Scale submenu.

## Delete a measurement scale preset

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Set Measurement Scale**>**Custom.

From the list of presets, select the one you want to delete.

Select Delete Preset and then select OK.

## 458. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/manage-scale-markers.html

Learn more about creating a scale marker, adding or replacing markers, and deleting them in photoeditor to represent measurement scales visually.

## Create a scale marker

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Place Scale Marker**.**

In the Measurement Scale Marker dialog box, set the following options

*   Length**:** Enter a value to set the marker length. The pixel length depends on the active measurement scale.
*   Font**:** Select the font for the display text.
*   Font Size**:** Select the font size for the display text.
*   Display Text**:** Select this option to show the logical length and units.
*   Text Position**:** Display the caption above or below the marker.
*   Color**:** Set the marker and caption color to black or white.

Select OK. The marker is placed in the lower-left corner of the image as a layer group containing a text layer (if display text is enabled) and a graphic layer.

Use the Move**tool** to reposition the marker, or the Text**tool** to edit the caption, font, size, or color.

## Add or replace scale markers

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Place Scale Marker.

In the dialog box, select whether to Remove or Keep existing markers.

Enter new settings for the marker.

Select OK to place the new marker.

Additional markers are placed in the exact location and may obscure each other. To view underlying markers, turn off the scale marker layer group in the Layers panel.

## Delete a scale marker

In the Layers panel, select the Measurement Scale Marker layer group.

Right-click the group and select Delete Group or the Delete Layer.

In the confirmation dialog box, select Group and Contents.


====================================================================================================

## 459. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/measurement-log-for-measurements.html

Learn more about measuring selections, distances, angles, and object counts in professional photoeditor, and record results in the Measurement Log.

You can measure using the photoeditor **selection tools**, Ruler**tool**, or Count**tool**. Select a measurement tool that matches the type of data you want to record in the Measurement Log.

*   Create a **selection area** to measure height, width, perimeter, area, and pixel gray values. You can measure one or multiple selections at once.
*   Use the Ruler**tool** to measure linear distance and angles.
*   Use the Count**tool** to count items in the image, then record the number.

Each measurement records one or more **data points**. The data points you select determine the information captured in the Measurement Log.

*   Data points for selections: area, perimeter, height, width.
*   Data points for the Ruler tool: length, angle.
*   Data point presets: data points you can save and reuse for faster workflows.

## Measure and record data

Open an existing document.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Set Measurement Scale, then select a measurement scale preset, or select Custom to define your own.

*   If no measurement scale exists, the default scale is 1 pixel = 1 pixel.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Select Data Points and do one of the following:

*   Select Custom to manually choose data points.
*   Select an existing data point preset.
*   In the Select Data Points dialog box, data points are grouped by measurement tool. Common data points include file name, measurement scale, and date/time.

When you measure with a specific tool, only the relevant data points for that tool appear in the log, along with any selected Common data points.

Select an image feature and the appropriate tool:

*   Create one or more selections on the image.
*   Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Ruler**tool**, or select the Ruler tool from the toolbar, then measure length or angle.
*   Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Count**tool**, or select the Count tool from the toolbar, then count items.

Select Window**>**Measurement Log to open the Measurement Log panel.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Record Measurements, or select Record Measurements in the Measurement Log panel to record all the measurement data.

If your selected data points don't match the current measurement tool, photoeditor will prompt you to update them.

## Measurement Log behaviour

*   The Measurement Log creates a column for each selected data point.
*   Each measurement adds a new row of data in the log.
*   Multiple selected areas create one summary row of cumulative data, followed by a row for each selection. Each selection area is listed as a separate feature in the Label column and assigned a unique number.
*   The Document column identifies the source document for each measurement.

To build comprehensive measurement data, you can repeat the steps across multiple selections or documents.


====================================================================================================

## 460. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/measurement-data-points-overview.html

Learn more about how to measure image features in photoeditor and understand what each data point records in the Measurement Log.

When you measure an image in photoeditor, the data points you select determine the values recorded in the Measurement Log. Each data point corresponds to the measurement tool you use.

## Heading

**Column heading****Column heading**
**Angle**Records the angle of orientation (±0-180) defined by the Ruler tool.
**Area**Records the area of the selection in square pixels, or in calibrated units according to the current measurement scale (for example, square millimeters).
**Circularity**Records the shape factor using the formula 4π × (Area ÷ Perimeter²). A value of 1.0 indicates a perfect circle, while values closer to 0.0 indicate elongated polygons. May not be valid for very small selections.
**Count**Records the count value based on the active tool: number of discontinuous selection areas, number of counted items (Count tool), or number of ruler lines (Ruler tool).
**Date and Time**Records the date and time stamp of when the measurement occurred.
**Document**Records the name of the document (file) measured.
**Gray Value**Records brightness values of the selection. Values range from 0–255 (8-bit), 0–32,768 (16-bit), or 0.0–10 (32-bit). photoeditor converts the image internally to grayscale and calculates mean, median, minimum, and maximum values.
**Height**Records the height of the selection (max y – min y), based on the current measurement scale.
**Histogram**Records histogram data for each channel (RGB, CMYK, etc.). Data is exported as CSV files when saving the Measurement Log. For multiple selections, one histogram file is generated for the total selection and additional files for each separate selection.
**Integrated Density**Records the sum of all pixel values in the selection. Equivalent to Area (in pixels) × Mean Gray Value.
**Label**Records a unique identifier for each measurement (for example, Measurement 1, Measurement 2). For multiple selections, assigns an additional Feature label and number.
**Length**Records the linear distance defined by the Ruler tool, based on the current measurement scale.
**Perimeter**Records the perimeter of the selection. For multiple selections, one perimeter is recorded for the total selection and additional values for each separate selection.
**Scale**Records the measurement scale of the source document (for example, 100 px = 3 miles).
**Scale Units**Records the logical units of the measurement scale (for example, pixels, millimeters).
**Scale Factor**Records the number of pixels assigned to each scale unit.
**Source**Records the measurement source: Ruler tool, Count tool, or Selection.
**Width**Records the width of the selection (max x – min x), based on the current measurement scale.


====================================================================================================

## 461. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/create-edit-and-delete-data-point-presets.html

Learn more about creating, editing, and deleting data point presets in photoeditor to speed up your measurement workflows and reuse frequently used configurations.

Select Image**>**Analysis**>**Select Data Points**>**Custom.

## 462. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/manage-measurement-logs.html

Learn more about displaying, selecting, organizing, and deleting data in the photoeditor Measurement Log panel.

## Display the Measurement Log

Select Image**>**Analysis**>Record Measurements**.

You can also select **Window>Measurement Log** to display measurement log**.**

![Image 1: The measurement log table is highlighted at the lower-bottom of the photoeditor canvas.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-measurement-log-table-is-highlighted-at-the-lo?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

The Measurement Log panel records the measurement data.

## Select rows in the log

Select a row in the Measurement log table.

To select multiple contiguous rows, select the first row and then Shift-click the last row, or click and drag through the rows.

To select non-contiguous rows, selectthe first row and then Ctrl-click (Windows) or Command-click (macOS) additional rows.

To select all rows, select Select all measurements .

To deselect all rows, select Deselect all measurements **.**

## Select columns in the log

In the Measurement log table, select a column header to select it.

To select multiple contiguous columns, select the first header and then Shift-click the last header, or select and drag.

To select non-contiguous columns, select the first header and then Ctrl+click (Windows) or Command+click (macOS) additional headers.

## Reorder, resize, or sort columns in the log

To reorder columns, drag the selected columns to a new position. A double black line indicates the placement.

To resize a column, select the column header and drag the separator.

To sort data in a column, do one of the following:

*   Select the column header to change the sort order.
*   Right-click the header and select Sort Ascending or Sort Descending.

Rows cannot be manually reordered.

## Export Measurement Log data

Select one or more rows of data in the Measurement Log.

Do one of the following:

*   Select Export from the Measurement Log options menu.
*   Select Export selected measurement
*   Right-click in a row and select Export from the menu.

Enter a filename and location.

Select Save.

The measurements are exported to a comma-delimited, UTF-8 text file.
