## 463. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/measurement-scale-and-scale-markers.html

Learn more about measuring objects, defining scales, and using scale markers in professional photoeditor to calculate and log accurate dimensions.

## Measurement in photoeditor

The Measurement feature in photoeditor allows you to calculate and record dimensions of specific areas in your images. You can measure areas defined with the Ruler**tool** or any **selection tool**, including irregular selections made with the Lasso, Quick Selection, or Magic Wand  tools.

With this feature, you can:

*   Compute height, width, area, and perimeter.
*   Track measurements across one or multiple images.
*   Record measurement data in the Measurement Log**panel**.
*   Customize Measurement Log columns, sort data, and export results to a tab-delimited Unicode text file.

A measurement scale links the number of pixels in an image to a real-world unit such as **inches, millimeters, or microns**. Once a scale is defined, photoeditor displays all calculated values in those units.

Key points about measurement scales:

*   A scale ensures accurate, real-world calculations for areas and dimensions.
*   You can save multiple **scale presets**, but only one scale can be active in a document at a time.

## Scale markers

Scale markers provide a visible reference to the measurement scale directly on an image. These markers help communicate scale clearly when images are shared or printed.

*   You can add scale markers with or without captions.
*   Captions display the unit of measurement, such as millimeters or inches.
*   Only one marker is needed to represent the active measurement scale.


====================================================================================================

## 464. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/use-rulers-in-photoeditor.html

Position elements precisely by displaying rulers, adjusting the zero origin, and changing measurement units.

Rulers help you position images and elements with precision in your workspace. When visible, rulers appear along the top and left edges of the active window with markers that track your pointer position in real time. The ruler origin (the 0,0 mark) determines where measurements begin and set the grid's origin point.

Rulers provide essential visual feedback for layout work, photo editing, and design tasks requiring exact positioning. You can customize both the visibility and measurement system to match your workflow needs.

## Show or hide rulers

Select View>Rulers to toggle ruler visibility, and then press Ctrl + R (Windows) or Command + R (macOS) for a quick show or hide rulers.

## Change the ruler origin

Moving the ruler origin allows you to measure from a specific point.

Select View >Snap To, then choose any combination of options from the submenu to snap the ruler origin to guides, slices, document bounds, or the grid.

Position the pointer over the intersection of the rulers at the beginning of the window.

Drag diagonally down onto the image. A set of crosshairs appears, marking the new origin on the rulers.

Hold Shift while dragging to snap the ruler origin to the ruler tick marks.

## Change the measurement unit

Open the Units & Rulers preferences by doing one of the following:

*   Select a ruler and then choose Edit>Preferences>Units & Rulers (Windows)
*   Select photoeditor>Settings>Units & Rulers (macOS).

Choose your preferred unit of measurement from the Rulers dropdown menu.

Select one of the following options for Point/Pica Size:

*   PostScript (72 points/inch) for output to PostScript devices
*   Traditional (72.27 points/inch) for traditional print measurements.

Select OK.

You can also quickly change ruler units by right-clicking (Windows) or Control-clicking (macOS) directly on a ruler and selecting a new unit from the context menu. Changing units in the Info panel automatically updates the ruler units. Use [Position with the Ruler tool](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/positioning-ruler-tool.html?x-product=Helpx/1.0.0&x-product-location=Search:Forums:link/3.8.2) to understand measurement and the Info panel tool options.


====================================================================================================

## 465. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/use-grids-measurement-guides/measure-scale/measure-and-position-with-the-ruler-tool.html

Learn how to use the Ruler tool to calculate distances, angles, and coordinates for precise image positioning in professional photoeditor on desktop.

The Ruler tool helps you position images or elements precisely by calculating the distance between any two points in your workspace. When you measure from one point to another, photoeditor draws a non-printing line and displays measurement information in the options bar and Info panel. If your document already has a measuring line, selecting the Ruler tool displays it automatically.

## Measure between two points

Select the Ruler tool from the toolbar.

Drag from your starting point to your ending point. Press Shift to constrain the tool to 45° increments.

View the measurement data in the options bar or Info panel.

## Create a protractor angle

Create an initial measuring line using the Ruler tool.

Press Alt-drag (Windows) or Option-drag (macOS) at an angle from one end of the measuring line or double-click the line and drag.

Press Shift while dragging to constrain the protractor to multiples of 45°.

The protractor displays two length measurements (D1 and D2) along with the angle between them.

## Edit or remove a measuring line

Select the Ruler tool.

To resize the line, drag one end of the existing measuring line to a new position.

To move the entire line, place the pointer on the line away from either of the endpoints and drag it to a new location.

To remove the line, drag it completely out of the image or select Clear in the options bar.

Use the Ruler tool to straighten images. Draw a measuring line along a feature that should be horizontal or vertical, then select Image>Image Rotation>Arbitrary. Enter Angle for rotation and select required rotate options and then OK to rotate canvas.


====================================================================================================

## 466. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/create-frame-based-animations.html

Create frame-by-frame animations by configuring layers in different positions, opacities, or styles for each frame in professional photoeditor.

Frame-based animations in photoeditor allow you to create simple animated content by building a sequence of frames, with each frame representing a different configuration of your layers. This technique is ideal for creating animated GIFs for web content, social media posts, or simple motion graphics.

Frame animations work by displaying a sequence of frames in order, creating the illusion of movement. Unlike timeline animations that use keyframes and automatic interpolation, frame animations give you precise control over each individual frame. This workflow is best suited for simple animations with distinct states rather than complex, fluid movements.

Open the Timeline and Layers panels by selecting Window>Timeline and Window>Layers.

Select the down arrow  icon in the middle of the Timeline panel, and select Create Frame Animation.

Add a new layer or convert the background layer to a regular layer.

Add content to your animation. If your animation includes several objects that move independently or if you want to change the color or content in different frames, create these objects on separate layers.

Add a new frame by selecting the Duplicate Selected Frames icon at the bottom of the Timeline panel.

Select the new frame in the Timeline panel and modify the layers for this frame in the Layers panel. You can turn visibility on or off for different layers, change layer positions, adjust opacity to create fade effects, or modify layer styles.

Continue adding frames and editing layers as needed. Add as many frames as your animation requires. The number of frames is only limited by your system's available memory.

Set the time for each frame by selecting the delay time (shown under each frame) and select a duration from the dropdown menu.

Set looping options by selecting Once, Forever, or Other from the Select looping options dropdown menu at the bottom of the Timeline panel.

Select the Play icon in the Timeline panel to preview your animation.


====================================================================================================

## 467. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/add-frames-to-animations.html

Add new frames to your timeline to create dynamic animations, allowing you to build frame-by-frame sequences in professional photoeditor.

Select Window>Timeline to open the Timeline panel if it's not already visible.

Make sure the Timeline panel is in Create Frame Animation mode. If you see Create Video Timeline, select the down arrow  icon, select Create Frame Animation from the dropdown and then select the Create Frame Animation button.

Select the Duplicate Selected Frames icon and use the Layers panel to modify the content of the new frame by changing layer visibility, position, opacity, or other properties.

To add a new layer for each new frame, select the panel menu **(icon will be added here)** icon and select Create New Layer for Each New Frame in the Timeline panel.

After adding frames, you can further enhance your animation by adjusting frame delay times, setting looping options, or using the Tween feature to create intermediate frames between two existing frames.


====================================================================================================

## 468. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/select-animation-frames.html

Select specific frames in your animation sequence for precise editing and manipulation in professional photoeditor.

When working with frame animations in photoeditor, you need to select specific frames before you can edit their content. The currently selected frame appears in the document window, allowing you to make changes to its layers. Frame selection is essential for editing animation content, adjusting timing, or applying effects to specific parts of your animation.

In the Timeline panel, select the frame thumbnail you want to select.

The selected frame appears with a shaded highlight, and its content displays in the document window. Make your desired edits to the layers for this frame using the Layers panel.

Hold Shift to select multiple consecutive frames and Hold Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) and select additional frames you want to include.

After selecting frames, you can edit their content by adjusting layer properties in the Layers panel, including visibility, position, opacity, and blending modes. You can also set timing options or delete, duplicate, and reorder selected frames using the Timeline panel controls.


====================================================================================================

## 469. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/edit-animation-frames.html

Adjust layer visibility, position, opacity, and blending modes to modify animation frames in professional photoeditor.

Animation frames in photoeditor allow you to create dynamic content by making sequential changes to your layers. By editing individual frames, you can create smooth animations with objects that move, fade, or transform.

Frame animation is ideal for creating simple animations like GIFs for web use. Each frame represents a specific configuration of your layers, and by making small changes between frames, you can create the illusion of movement or transformation. This technique works best for animations with discrete changes rather than continuous motion.

Select a frame in the Timeline panel.

Select the eye  icon next to the selected layers to adjust their visibility in the Layers panel.

Change the position of objects in a layer and using the Move tool.

Adjust layer opacity in the Layers panel to create fade-in or fade-out effects between frames.

Change blending modes of layers to create different visual effects as your animation progresses.

Select the Layer effects icon in the Layers panel to add effects.

Select the Play icon in the Timeline panel to preview your changes.

For more complex animations with multiple moving elements, consider creating each element on a separate layer. This approach gives you greater control over individual animation components.


====================================================================================================

## 470. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/unify-layer-properties-in-animation-frames.html

Use the unify layer buttons to consistently apply position, visibility, and style attributes across multiple animation frames in professional photoeditor.

The unify layer buttons help you control how changes to layer attributes in one frame affect other frames in your animation. The unify buttons in the Layers panel determine how changes you make to attributes in the active animation frame apply to other frames in the same layer.

## Apply layer properties across all frames

Select the frame where you want to change an attribute in the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Make the desired changes to the selected frame's layer such as move an object, change visibility, or modify a style.

Select the Unify Layer Position icon, Unify Layer Visibility icon, or the Unify Layer Style icon from the Layers panel. The attribute you changed will now be applied to all frames in the active layer, maintaining consistency throughout your animation.

You can also propagate changes from the first frame to all subsequent frames using the Propagate Frame 1 option.

## Propagate changes from the first frame

Select the first frame of your animation in the Timeline panel.

Make the desired changes to your layer attributes in this frame.

Select the Propagate Frame 1 option from the Layers panel. The changes you made to the first frame will be applied in relation to all subsequent frames in the active layer.

You can also propagate frames by holding Shift and selecting any consecutive group of frames in the layer and changing an attribute in any of the selected frames.

## Show or hide unify layer buttons

Select the panel menu icon from the Layers panel.

Select Animation Options.

Select Automatic, Always Show, or Always Hide.


====================================================================================================

## 471. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/copy-frames-with-layer-properties.html

Duplicate animation frames while preserving layer visibility, position, opacity, and effects in professional photoeditor.

Copying frames with their layer properties intact saves time and ensures consistency throughout your animation sequence. This procedure helps when creating frame-based animations where you need to preserve specific layer attributes (such as position, opacity, or effects) while making incremental changes between frames. It's particularly useful for animations that require consistent styling across frames with only small positional or content changes.

Select the frame in the Timeline panel that contains the layer properties you want to copy.

Select the panel menu icon and select Copy Frame.

Select on the frame after which you want to insert the new frame.

Select the panel menu icon, select Paste Frame, and select one of the following options:

*   Replace Frames: Replaces the selected frames with the copied frames. No new layers are added. The properties of each existing layer in the destination frames are replaced by those of each copied layer.
*   Paste Over Selection: Adds the contents of the pasted frames as new layers in the image. When you paste frames into the same image, using this option doubles the number of layers in the image.
*   Paste Before Selection: Adds the copied frames before the selected frame. When you paste frames between images, new layers are added to the image; however, only the pasted layers are visible in the new frames, and the existing layers are hidden.
*   Paste After Selection: Adds the copied frames after the selected frame. When you paste frames between images, new layers are added to the image; however, only the pasted layers are visible in the new frames, and the existing layers are hidden.

Select Link Added Layers, to link pasted layers in the Layers panel. This option works only when pasting frames into another document. Select it when you plan to reposition the pasted layers as a unit.

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 472. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/create-frames-using-tweening.html

Use the Tween command to automatically generate intermediate frames between existing animation frames in professional photoeditor.

When creating frame animations in photoeditor, you can save significant time by using the Tween command to automatically generate intermediate frames between two existing frames. Tweening automatically varies layer properties (position, opacity, or effects) evenly across new frames, creating fluid movement or transitions without manually creating each frame.

Select the layer you want to animate in the Layers panel.

Select the frame or frames you want to work with in the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline). You can select a single frame to tween with either the previous or next frame, select two contiguous frames to add new frames between them, or select multiple frames to alter existing frames between the first and last selected frames.

Select the Tweens animation frames icon in the Timeline panel.

Update the following options in the Tween panel:

*   Tween With: Select from Selection, Previous Frame, Next Frame, First Frame, or Last Frame.
*   Frames to Add: Add the number of frames you need.
*   Layers: Select from All Layers or Selected Layers to specify which layers to vary.
*   Parameters: Select the properties that will be applied evenly across the new frames from Position, Opacity, and Effects.

Select OK.

You can apply tweening multiple times to different sections of your animation to create complex movements.

For smoother animations, add more tweened frames. The more frames you add, the more gradual and fluid the animation will appear.


====================================================================================================

## 473. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/manage-layer-visibility-in-animation-frames.html

Control layer visibility in individual animation frames to create dynamic effects, transitions in professional photoeditor.

Hiding layers in animation frames is useful when you want to create animations with elements that appear and disappear, or when you need to swap content between frames. This technique works with any frame animation in photoeditor and requires no special plugins or extensions.

Open the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Select the frame for which you want to manage the layer visibility.

Select the panel menu icon and select New Layers Visible In All Frames to make all new layers visible in all the frames in the animation.

To hide a layer in a specific frame, select the frame, and then hide the desired layer in the Layers panel.


====================================================================================================

## 474. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/specify-a-delay-time-in-frame-animations.html

Learn how to set the display duration for individual frames or multiple frames simultaneously in professional photoeditor.

When creating frame animations in photoeditor, controlling the timing between frames is essential for achieving the right visual effect. The delay time determines how long each frame appears before moving to the next one, allowing you to create smooth transitions or emphasize specific moments in your animation.

Delay times are measured in seconds, with fractions displayed as decimal values (for example, 0.25 seconds equals one-quarter of a second). Setting appropriate delay times helps control the pace of your animation and can dramatically affect how viewers perceive motion. Once you set a delay for the current frame, photoeditor will apply that same delay value to any new frames you create afterward.

Select one or more frames in the Timeline panel.

Select the delay time value displayed below the selected frames to open the delay time menu.

Select a preset delay time from No delay, 0.1 seconds, 0.2, 0.5, 1.0, and more, or select Other to add a custom delay time. The new delay time will be applied to all selected frames. You'll see the updated value displayed below each frame.

You can set different delay times for various parts of your animation to create effects like acceleration, deceleration, or to emphasize specific moments. For example, use shorter delays for fast-moving sections and longer delays when you want viewers to focus on a particular frame.

For smoother animations, use consistent delay times throughout most of your animation. Only vary the timing when you specifically want to create an effect like acceleration or to emphasize a particular moment.


====================================================================================================

## 475. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/choose-a-frame-disposal-method.html

Select how frames are processed during animation playback by choosing the appropriate disposal method for each frame in professional photoeditor.

Selecting the right disposal method ensures your animation displays correctly without unwanted artifacts. Frame disposal methods control whether a frame remains visible or is removed before displaying the next frame. The right choice depends on your animation's needs—whether you want frames to build upon each other or replace previous content entirely.

Select one or more frames in the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Right-click (Windows) or Control-click (macOS) the selected frames.

Select one of the following disposal methods:

*   Automatic: Lets photoeditor determine the appropriate disposal method for the frame.
*   Do Not Dispose: Preserves the current frame when the next frame is displayed. This option allows transparent areas of the next frame to show content from previous frames. Use this when building cumulative animations where elements are added progressively.
*   Dispose: Removes the current frame before displaying the next frame. Only the current frame will be visible, with no content showing through from previous frames. Use this when each frame should completely replace the previous one.

Use the playback controls at the bottom of the Timeline panel to test how your disposal methods affect the animation.


====================================================================================================

## 476. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/specify-looping-in-frame-animations.html

Control how many times your frame animation plays by setting loop options in professional photoeditor.

Frame animations in photoeditor can be set to play once, a specific number of times, or continuously in a loop. Setting the right looping option ensures your animation plays as intended when exported or viewed online.

Looping settings are applied to the entire animation and determine how many times the sequence repeats when viewed. These settings don't affect how the animation appears while editing in photoeditor, but they do control playback behavior when the animation is exported as an animated GIF or other supported format.

Open the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Select the looping options menu in the bottom-left corner of the Timeline panel. The default option is Forever.

Select one of the available looping options: Once, 3 Times, Forever, or Other. If you selected Other, enter the number of times you want the animation to loop in the dialog box that appears, then select OK.

After setting your loop options, you can preview the animation using the playback options in the Timeline panel.


====================================================================================================

## 477. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/create-animation-frames/delete-animations.html

Remove unwanted frame animations from your professional photoeditor project using the Timeline panel to clean up your animation workflow.

When creating animations in photoeditor, you might need to remove specific frames or entire animations to refine your project. Deleting animations helps maintain a clean workspace and optimizes file size.

## Delete individual animation frames

Select the frames you want to delete in the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Select the Deletes selected frames icon.

Select OK.

## Delete an entire animation

Open the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline).

Select the panel menu icon and select Delete Animation.


====================================================================================================

## 478. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/use-keyframes/create-timeline-animation-workflow.html

Learn more about creating a timeline-based animation in photoeditor using layers, keyframes, and the Timeline panel.

![Image 1](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image760315?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Timeline animation panel overview

A. Playhead B. Timeline panel menu C. Add media to track D. Keyframe E. Zoom In F. Zoom slider G. Zoom Out H. Convert to frame animation I. Enable Keyframe animation J. Playback options

Create a new document with appropriate dimensions for your animation. Make sure the color mode is set to RGB and resolution to 72 pixels/inch for standard web animations.

Open the Timeline panel by selecting Window>Timeline. If the panel shows frame animation mode, select the Convert to Video Timeline<insert icon here>icon in the lower-left corner.

Select Create Video Timeline from the drop-down menu, then click the button to the left of the arrow.

Add content by creating a new layer (Layer>New>Layer) or convert the background layer to a regular layer by double-clicking it in the Layers panel.

Set the timeline frame rate by selecting the Timeline panel menu and selecting Set Timeline Frame Rate.

Choose an appropriate rate such as 24 fps, which is standard for video.

Move the current-time indicator (the playhead) to where you want to set your first keyframe.

Expand the Layer properties by clicking the triangle next to the layer name, then select the Enable Keyframe animation icon next to the property you want to animate such as Position, Opacity, or Style.

Move the current-time indicator to a different point in time, then change the Layer property (move the layer, adjust opacity, etc.). photoeditor automatically creates a new keyframe.

Save your animation by selecting File>Export>Render Video for video output or File>Export>Save for Web (Legacy)for animated GIF.

Preview your animation by selecting the Play button in the Timeline panel.


====================================================================================================

## 479. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/add-video-and-animation/use-keyframes/overview-of-animating-layer-properties.html

Learn how to animate layer properties in photoeditor using keyframes for position, opacity, and more.

In photoeditor, keyframes allow you to animate layer properties such as Position**,**Opacity**, and**Style. Each property can change independently or in combination with others, giving you control over complex animations. Place each object on a separate layer for best results when animating multiple objects.

To animate a property:

*   Set at least **two keyframes** for the property. Changes made without two keyframes will remain static for the duration of the layer.
*   Select the Time‑Vary stopwatch <insert icon here>**icon** next to the property to begin animation. When active, photoeditor automatically creates new keyframes whenever you adjust the property at a different point in time.

**Examples of Animatable Properties:**

*   **Position:** Add a keyframe to the Position property, move the current time indicator, and drag the layer to a new location in the document window.
*   **Opacity:** Add a keyframe to the Opacity property, move the current time indicator, and adjust the opacity in the Layers panel.
*   **3D properties:** Animate object or camera positions for 3D layers. (See Create 3D animations for details.)

When the stopwatch is inactive:

*   Any typed value remains for the duration of the layer.
*   Deselecting the stopwatch permanently deletes all keyframes for that property.

Using keyframes effectively allows for precise, dynamic animations, giving your projects a professional and polished look.


====================================================================================================

## 480. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/record-an-action.html

Learn more about recording actions in professional photoeditor to automate repetitive tasks and save time.

Recording actions in photoeditor lets you automate repetitive tasks by capturing a sequence of steps and playing them back later. You can even include tool operations, such as brush strokes, by enabling tool recording in the Actions panel.

Select Windows>Action.

Select New Action from the Actions panel menu.

In New Action dialog box, set the following options:

*   Name**: Type the name of the action.**
*   Set**: Select the**Set**you created, otherwise**Default Actions**is selected by default.**
*   Function Key: Assign a shortcut, for example Ctrl + Shift + F3 (Windows) or Command + Shift + F3 (macOS). On Windows, you cannot use F1 with Ctrl. If the shortcut is already in use, it will run the action instead of the original command.
*   Color: Assign a color for display in Button mode.

Select the Record .

Perform the commands and operations you want to capture. For nonrecordable tasks, insert commands from the Actions panel menu.

![Image 1: In the Actions panel, the Pause, Record, and Play button is highlighted to record action.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/image7343?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Select Stop, Record, Play button to perform actions.

Select Stop Playing/Recording to finish.

Select Create new set to create a folder to store and organize your recorded actions.


====================================================================================================

## 481. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/record-a-path.html

Learn more about how to record a path in professional photoeditor using the Insert Path command to include complex paths in an action.

You can use the Insert Path command to include a complex path (created with the Pen tool) as part of an action. When the action is played back, the work path is set to the recorded path. You can insert a path either while recording an action or after the action has been recorded.

## Record a path in an action

Start recording an action.

Select an existing path from the Paths panel.

In the Actions panel, select the menu icon >Insert Path.

If you need to insert more than one path in an action, save each path after inserting it. Record a Save Path step in the Paths panel after each Insert Path step so that the previous path isn't replaced.


====================================================================================================

## 482. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/insert-a-stop.html

Learn more about inserting stops in an action in professional photoeditor to pause playback for tasks that cannot be recorded.

Use stops in an action to pause playback for tasks that cannot be recorded, such as painting. After you finish the task, select Play in the Actions panel to resume the action.

Select an action’s name to insert a stop at the end of the action, or select a command to insert a stop after that command.

Select Insert Stop from the Actions panel menu.

Type the message you want to appear.

Select OK.

You can insert a stop either while recording an action or after the action has been recorded.

You can display a short message at the stop as a reminder. You can include a Continue button in the message box, if no task is required.


====================================================================================================

## 483. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/change-settings-when-playing-an-action.html

Learn more about inserting modal controls in professional photoeditor actions to pause playback and adjust settings for specific steps.

You can insert a modal control to pause an action and change settings for a specific step. This allows you to adjust the options of a command while the action runs.

In the Actions panel, locate the command you want to pause for.

Select the box to the left of the command name to enable modal control.

![Image 1: The modal control button is highlighted in the Action panel.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-modal-control-button-is-highlighted-in-the-Act?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1500)

Toggle on or off modal button to allow to change default settings.

When the action pauses, adjust the settings in the dialog box that appears.

Press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS) to continue playing the action.


====================================================================================================

## 484. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/exclude-commands-from-an-action.html

Learn more about how to exclude commands from an action in professional photoeditor so they don’t run when the action is played back.

You can exclude commands from an action, so they don’t play back.

In the Actions panel, expand the action to view its commands.

Clear the check mark next to the command you want to exclude.


====================================================================================================

## 485. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/insert-a-non-recordable-menu-command.html

Learn more about inserting non-recordable menu commands into an action in professional photoeditor to make them run during playback.

Use the Insert Menu Item command to add commands that can’t be recorded directly into an action. These commands run only when the action is played. Playback pauses until you select OK or Cancel if the command opens a dialog box.

In the Actions panel, select the action or command where you want to insert the menu item.

From the Actions panel menu, select Insert Menu Item.

In the Insert Menu Item dialog box, open your menu (such as File, Edit, or Image), and select the command to be inserted.

Select OK.

## 486. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/overwrite-a-single-command.html

Learn more about how to edit a command in an action in professional photoeditor.

In the Actions panel, double-click the command you want to edit.

Adjust the settings in the dialog box.

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 487. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/add-commands-to-an-action.html

Learn more about how to add new commands to an action in professional photoeditor.

In the Actions panel, select the action name to add commands at the end, or select a specific command to insert them after that step.

Select Begin Recording in the Actions panel.

Perform the commands you want to include while recording is on.

Select Stop Playing/Recording in the Actions panel.


====================================================================================================

## 488. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/rearrange-commands-within-an-action.html

Learn more about how to rearrange commands within an action in professional photoeditor.

In the Actions panel, expand the action to view its commands.

Drag the command you want to move to a new position within the same action or into another action.

Release the mouse button when the highlighted line appears in the desired position.


====================================================================================================

## 489. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/create-record-actions/record-an-action-again.html

Learn more about how to re-record an action in professional photoeditor.

In the Actions panel, select the action you want to re-record, then select Record Again from the panel menu.

If the modal  tool appears, use it to create a different result and press Enter (Windows) or Return (macOS) to record, or keep the same settings.

Adjust the settings in the dialog box and select OK to record or Cancel to retain the values.

By default, recorded and installed actions are saved in the Actions Palette.psp file inside your [photoeditor preferences](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/get-started/settings-and-preferences/adjust-preferences.html) folder. It’s good practice to export recorded action (.atn) files so they can be archived or shared.

## 490. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/process-a-batch-of-files/image-processor-overview.html

#### Learn about professional photoeditor capabilities to process multiple files simultaneously, without creating an action.

The Image Processor(File>Scripts>Image Processor) allows you to convert files in a single operation and handle everyday batch processing tasks through a straightforward interface. Unlike the Batch command, it works directly without requiring an action.

With the Image Processor, you can:

*   Convert a set of files to JPEG, PSD, or TIFF format, or save them in all three formats simultaneously.
*   Process camera raw files using the same options.
*   Resize images to fit within specific pixel dimensions.
*   Embed a color profile or convert files to sRGB and save them as JPEG images for the web.
*   Include copyright metadata in the converted images.


====================================================================================================

## 491. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/process-a-batch-of-files/convert-files-with-the-image-processor.html

Learn how to batch convert, resize, and save multiple files in professional photoeditor with format, color, and metadata options.

Select File>Scripts>Image Processor.

Select images to process from open files or include all subfolders.

(Optional) Select Open First Image To Apply Settings to adjust the first image, then apply the same settings to the rest.  
 For PSD or JPEG files, use this option to convert the first image’s color profile and apply it to all images.  
Image Processor settings are temporary and apply only to that location. Camera RAW files retain their current settings unless you make changes.

Select a location to save the processed files.  
Each processed file is saved with a unique name and not overwritten.

Select the location where you want to save the processed files.

Select the file types and options:

*   Save As JPEG:Saves images in JPEG format within a folder called JPEG in the destination folder.
*   Quality:Sets the JPEG image quality between 0 and 12.  
*   Resize to Fit:Resizes the image to fit within the dimensions you enter in Width and Height. The image retains its original proportions.  
*   Convert Profile To sRGB:Converts the color profile to sRGB. Make sure that Include ICC Profile is selected if you want to save the profile with the image.
*   Save As PSD:Saves images in photoeditor format within a folder called PSD in the destination folder.
*   Maximize Compatibility:Saves a composite version of a layered image within the target file for compatibility with applications that can't read layered images.
*   Save As TIFF:Saves images in TIFF format within a folder called TIFF in the destination folder.
*   LZW compression:Saves the TIFF file using the LZW compression scheme.

Set other preferences:

*   Run Action: Select an action set and an action that must be loaded in the Actions panel to run on each image.
*   Copyright Info: Add or overwrite metadata.
*   Include ICC Profile: Select to embed the profile in the saved files.

Select Run to process the files.   
The Image Processor creates JPEG, PSD, or TIFF file folders in the selected destination.

Select Save to save the settings. To use them again, select Load and select the saved file.


====================================================================================================

## 492. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/process-a-batch-of-files/batch-process-files.html

#### Learn how to process multiple files at once in professional photoeditor and save them to your chosen location or back to their original folders.

The Batch command automates repetitive editing by running an action on groups of files. It saves you time by handling large sets of images in a single workflow.

Select File>Automate>Batch.

Select the action you want to use from the Set and Action dropdown menus. Load the correct set in the Window>Actions panel if your action isn't visible.

Select the source files from the Source dropdown menu:

*   Select Folder to process files in a chosen folder. Select Choose to locate it.
*   Select Import to process images from a camera, scanner, or PDF.
*   Select Opened Files to process all currently open files.
*   Select Bridge to process selected files in professional Bridge, or all files in the current Bridge folder if none are selected.

Select the Override Action "Open" Commands to process your selected files instead of the file recorded in the action.

Select Include All Subfolders to process files in subdirectories of the specified folder.

Select Suppress File Open Options Dialogs to hide dialog boxes when processing camera raw files, using the default or previously set options.

Select Suppress Color Profile Warnings to turn off the display of color policy messages.

Select where to save files in the Destination dropdown:

*   None (leave open)
*   Save and Close (overwrite originals)
*   Folder (save to a chosen location)

Select OK to run the batch process.  
 Open the destination folder (or original files if you have selected Save and Close) to verify that all files were processed and saved correctly.

## Batch-process files in nested folders into different formats

Use this procedure to sharpen, resize, and save images as JPEGs in their original folders. Create an action with these steps, then batch-process it by selecting Include All Subfolders, setting the destination to Save and Close, and selecting Override Action “Save As” Commands.

Process your folders until the Destination step.

Select Save and Close as the destination.

Select Override Action “Save As” Commands to:

*   Use the document’s name instead of any filename recorded in the Save As step.
*   Save files in their original folders instead of the folder recorded in the Save As step.

Ensure your action includes a Save As step, as files won’t save automatically without it.

Select OK to run the batch process.

## 493. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/process-a-batch-of-files/create-a-droplet-from-an-action.html

Learn how to create droplets in professional photoeditor to batch-process files quickly by dragging them onto an icon.

Select File>Automate>Create Droplet.

Select Choose in the Save Droplet In section and choose a location, such as your desktop.

Select an action set from the Set dropdown, then select the specific action from the Action dropdown.

Select OK to create the droplet. Your droplet appears as an icon at the specified location.

Drag a file or folder onto the droplet icon to automatically run the assigned action in photoeditor.

## Best practices for cross-platform droplets

*   Use the EXE extension when creating droplets for compatibility on Windows and macOS.
*   Drag a Windows droplet onto the photoeditor icon on macOS to update it for use.
*   Avoid hardcoded filenames in actions, as they can pause execution and prompt for a filename.

## 494. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/automate-tasks/process-a-batch-of-files/batch-and-droplet-processing-options.html

Understand professional photoeditor batch options for setting sources, destinations, and naming rules to automate the processing of multiple files.

## Important notes about processing options

### Override Action "Open" Commands

If the action contains an Open command that opens a saved file and you don’t select this option, photoeditor reopens the recorded file after each source file and processes only that file. This happens because the Batch command always opens the file specified in the action, so the most recently opened file is used, and none of your selected files are processed.

This option requires an action to include an Open command. It ignores only the file choice, not the other Open settings.

### Override Action "Save As" Commands

If you don’t select this option and your action includes a Save As command, photoeditor saves files to the folder recorded in the action instead of the Batch destination. If the action’s Save As step specifies a filename, photoeditor overwrites that same file every time it processes an image.

To use this option, your action must include a Save As step. Otherwise, the Batch command won’t save the files. This option ignores only the filename and folder from the Save As command, not other settings.

If you want files saved with original names in the Batch folder, add a Save As step in your action, then select Override Action 'Save As' Commands and select a destination folder. If you rename files in the Batch dialog box but don’t select this option, photoeditor saves two copies, one with the new name in the Batch folder and one with the original name in the action’s folder.

Some Save options, like JPEG compression or TIFF settings, aren’t available in Batch or Droplet dialog boxes. To use them, record a Save As step with those options in your action, then select Override Action "Save As" Commands so photoeditor applies the options but saves the files in the folder you specify.

## 497. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/save-your-work.html

#### Learn how to save your work in professional photoeditor, choose from multiple file formats, and store it locally or in the cloud.

Select File>Save.

Select Save in the Save As dialog box.  
 Your document is saved in its current format and location, preserving all layers and settings.

## Save a document with a new name, location, or format

Select File>Save As.

Enter the file name, select the format, and location (local or Creative Cloud) in the Save As dialog box.

Select additional options as needed:  
Notes: Include text notes saved with the photoeditor file.

Alpha Channels: Preserve extra channels used for masks or selections.

Spot Colors: Retain custom spot colors for printing.

Layers: Keep all layers instead of flattening the file.

Color: Embed a color profile to maintain a consistent appearance across devices.

Select Save.  
A dialog box with additional options may appear depending on the selected format.

You can change the default save location to your computer or Creative Cloud. Select Edit>Preferences>File Handling (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>File Handling (macOS), then choose your preferred option under Default File Location and select OK.

![Image 1: The Preferences dialog box is open and the File Handling panel is displayed with options to set file saving behaviors, recovery intervals, default file locations, and compatibility settings.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Preferences-dialog-box-is-open-and-the-File-Ha?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Configure file saving preferences to manage how photoeditor saves and recovers files.

## Save a copy while preserving your original document

Select File>Save a Copy.

Enter the file name, select the format, and location (local or Creative Cloud) in the Save As dialog box.

Select additional options as needed:  
Notes: Include text notes saved with the photoeditor file.

Alpha Channels: Preserve extra channels used for masks or selections.

Spot Colors: Retain custom spot colors for printing.

Layers: Keep all layers instead of flattening the file.

Color: Embed a color profile to maintain a consistent appearance across devices.

Select Save.

The copy is saved while your original document remains open and unchanged. Depending on the selected format, a dialog box with additional options may appear.

To prevent photoeditor from adding the word copy to filenames, select Edit>Preferences>File Handling (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>File Handling (macOS). Then, choose Do not append "copy" to filename when saving a copy on both macOS and Windows. If there's a risk of overwriting the original file, you'll receive a warning.

The Save a Copy workflow lets you create a new version, such as a flattened JPEG or PNG, without altering the original.

## Create temporary versions without saving to disk

To temporarily store a version of your image in memory:

*   Select Window>History to create a snapshot and temporarily store a version of your image.
*   Select Image>Duplicate to create a copy of the image in memory without saving it to your hard disk.

Use a snapshot for quick, temporary backups and a duplicate when you need a separate version for detailed edits.

## 498. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/save-large-documents.html

#### Learn how to manage oversized documents in professional photoeditor using specialized formats.

Make sure you have enough disk space. Use special formats for images larger than 30,000 pixels in width or height, or documents over 2GB.

Select File>Save As.

Enter the document name and select its location (local or Creative Cloud) in the Save As dialog box.

Select a format from the Save as type (Windows) or Format (macOS) dropdown:

*   Large Document Format (*.PSB): Saves documents of any size with full photoeditor feature support. However, some plug-ins may not work if your document exceeds 30,000 pixels in width or height.
*   photoeditor Raw (*.RAW):Saves documents of any size. This format flattens your documents and doesn’t support layers.
*   TIFF(*.TIF,*.TIFF): Saves documents up to 4GB in size. Documents larger than this limit can’t be saved in TIFF format.

Select any additional saving options as needed.

Select Save.

Open the Preferences dialog box.

*   On macOS: Select photoeditor>Preferences>File Handling
*   On Windows: Select Edit>Preferences>File Handling

Select Enable legacy "Save As".

On macOS, you'll see a warning message about the risk of overwriting files. Select OK to acknowledge the warning.

Select OK to apply the changes.   
The Save As and Save a Copy commands will now use the legacy save dialog box available in photoeditor v22.3 and earlier.

## Control filename appending when saving different versions

Open the Preferences dialog box.

*   On macOS: Select photoeditor>Preferences>File Handling
*   On Windows: Select Edit>Preferences>File Handling

Locate the Do not append "copy" to filename when saving a copy option.

*   On macOS: This option is automatically enabled when you select the Enable legacy "Save As" option. It cannot be turned off unless you deselect it. 
*   On Windows: This option can be toggled independently of the Enable legacy "Save As" option.

Select OK to save your preferences.  
If the Do not append "copy" to filename when saving a copy option is selected, you'll receive a warning about the potential risk of overwriting files.

## 500. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/file-saving-properties-and-preferences.html

#### Learn about saving, naming, previewing, and recovering files, including format-specific settings and auto-recovery options in professional photoeditor.

photoeditor provides a range of options that determine how and where your files are saved, what information is preserved, and how the application handles file management.

Using the Save As or Save a Copy commands, you can configure various file saving options. The options available will vary based on the selected file format.

**Option****Usage**
As a Copy Make a copy of your file in a different format or location without overwriting the original.
Notes Save notes with the image.
Alpha Channels Save the alpha channel information with the image. Deselect the option to remove alpha channels.
Spot Colors Save the spot channel information with the image. Deselect the option to remove spot colors.
Layers Preserve all layers in the image. If unavailable, flatten or merge all visible layers, based on the selected format.
Use Proof Setup, ICC Profile (Windows), or Embed Color Profile (macOS)Create a color-managed document.
Save to cloud documents Save your file to Creative Cloud for easy access on all devices and sharing.

### Additional options

Image preview and filename extension options are only available when Ask When Saving is selected in the Image Previews (Windows) dropdown or the Append File Extension (macOS) dropdown in the File Handling panel of the Preferences dialog box.

**Option****Usage**
Thumbnail (Windows)Save notes with the image and quickly add a small preview image to view the file in folders or dialog boxes.
Image Previews options (macOS)Save notes with the image and quickly add a small preview image to view the file in folders or dialog boxes.

File saving preferences

When you're in the File Handling panel of the Preferences (Edit>Preferences (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences (macOS)) dialog box, customize how photoeditor manages file extensions, previews, saving options, and recovery to improve your workflow and prevent data loss.

**Option****Usage**
Image Previews Save image previews as follows:

*   Never Save: Save files without previews.
*   Always Save: Save files with specified previews.
*   Ask When Saving: Set previews individually for each file.

Select one or more preview types in macOS.
File Extension (Windows)Attach three-character extensions to define a file's format as follows:

*   Use Upper Case: Attach file extensions with uppercase characters.
*   Use Lower Case: Attach file extensions with lowercase characters.
Append File Extension (macOS)Select an option to append extensions to filenames:

*   Never: Save files without file extensions.
*   Always: Attach file extensions to filenames.
*   Ask When Saving: Attach file extensions on a file-by-file basis.
*   Use Lower Case: Attach file extensions using lowercase characters.
Default File Location Save files to Creative Cloud or your computer by default.
Save As to Original Folder Default to the folder the image originated from (when checked), or the last saved folder (when unchecked).
Save in Background Continue working in photoeditor while files are being saved.
Automatically Save Recovery Information Every:Store crash-recovery information automatically at a specified interval.
Enable legacy "Save As"Restore the older file saving behavior used in previous versions of photoeditor.
Do not append "copy" to filename when saving a copy Prevent photoeditor from adding the word copy to the filename when saving a duplicate.

## 502. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/common-questions-on-photoeditor-cloud-documents.html

Get answers to common questions about professional photoeditor cloud documents, including how to access, save, and work with them across multiple devices.

### What are photoeditor cloud documents?

Cloud documents are professional's cloud-native file format that can be accessed online or offline directly within photoeditor. They enable seamless work across devices while automatically saving your edits through the cloud.

### What are the benefits of using cloud documents?

photoeditor cloud documents offer reliability with automatic saving, mobility to access files across devices, quality with full-fidelity layer storage, and easy access to all your work directly within photoeditor on any device.

### Where can I find my cloud documents?

Access your cloud documents from the photoeditor home screen under the Your files tab on desktop or iPad, or go to [https://www.professional.com/files/cloud-documents/](https://www.professional.com/files/cloud-documents/).

### How do cloud documents differ from Creative Cloud Files?

Cloud documents are optimized for working on the go and enable seamless workflows between applications like photoeditor, XD, Fresco, and Aero. Creative Cloud Files store any file type but don't have the same native cloud [capabilities](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/using/cloud-documents-faq.html#cloud-documents-benefits) as cloud documents.

### Can I access cloud documents from Creative Cloud Libraries?

No, cloud documents can't be accessed from Creative Cloud Libraries. You can access them from within photoeditor or at assets.professional.com, but not from Libraries.

### Can I save my documents as local PSD files?

Yes, on desktop you can choose to save as either a cloud document or local file. On iPad, you can export your cloud document to other formats including PSD for local storage.

### How do I make my PSD accessible across devices?

To access your PSD across devices, save it as a cloud document by selecting File>Save As>Save to cloud documents.

### Can I edit a cloud document on multiple devices simultaneously?

Editing on multiple devices simultaneously can create version conflicts. It's recommended to save your changes to the cloud before switching to another device to avoid conflicts.

### Can I work with cloud documents when offline?

Yes, you can work offline with any cloud document that's in your device cache. Open documents before going offline to ensure local availability. Changes will sync when you reconnect to the internet.

### Are Smart Objects supported in cloud documents?

Embedded Smart Objects are fully supported in cloud documents. However, linked Smart Objects are only supported on the device where the cloud document was originally created.

### Do cloud documents in photoeditor have anything to do with Lightroom photos?

Although both Lightroom photos and cloud documents are stored in your Creative Cloud account, your Lightroom photos and cloud documents are unrelated and stored separately.

To see your photoeditor edits reflected within your Lightroom catalog, you can export your cloud document into a file format supported by Lightroom, such as a PSD or TIFF, and import your photo back into Lightroom.


====================================================================================================

## 506. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/manage-slices-in-photoeditor.html

Learn how to use the Slice Select tool to select, move, resize, align, and organize slices for web image optimization in professional photoeditor on desktop.

Slices divide images into smaller sections for web optimization. After creating slices, you can select, move, resize, combine, and organize them to refine your layout before export. User slices offer full editing control, while auto slices adjust automatically when you modify user slices or layer-based slices.

These procedures apply to user slices and layer-based slices in photoeditor. Auto slices cannot be edited directly, but they regenerate automatically to fill the remaining image areas when you modify other slice types. To edit an auto slice, first convert it to a user slice. Layer-based slices must be converted to user slices before you can move, resize, or combine them independently of their source layers.

## Select slices

Select the Slice Select tool from the toolbar.

Do one of the following:

*   Select a slice by clicking it,
*   Press Shift-click additional slices to add them to your selection
*   Click in an auto slice area or outside the image and drag across multiple slices to select them.

Press Ctrl (Windows) or Command (macOS) to temporarily switch between the Slice tool and Slice Select tool when either is active.

When working with overlapping slices, select the visible portion of an underlying slice to bring it into focus.

## Move slices

Select one or more user slices with the Slice Select tool.

Position your pointer inside the slice selection border and drag to the new location.

Press Shift while dragging to constrain movement to vertical, horizontal, or 45-degree diagonal directions.

### Resize slices

Select one or more user slices with the Slice Select tool.

Drag a side or corner handle of the selection border.

Press Shift while dragging to constrain proportions. When you resize adjacent slices together, their shared edges move as a unit.

### Position slices numerically

Select one or more user slices.

Select the Options button in the options bar, or double-click a slice to open the Slice Options dialog box.

In the Dimensions area, enter values for X (left edge position), Y (top edge position), W (width), or H (height) in pixels.

The X and Y coordinates measure from the ruler origin in the document window, which defaults to the upper-left corner of the image.

### Snap slices

Select View>Snap To and choose the elements you want slices to snap to, such as guides, other slices, or document bounds.

Select View>Snap to enable the snap feature.

Move your selected slices near the snap target—they automatically align when within 4 pixels of the target element.

## Divide slices

Select one or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Select Divide in the options bar to open the Divide Slice dialog box.

Enable Preview to see changes as you adjust settings.

Select Divide Horizontally Into to split slices lengthwise, Divide Vertically Into to split them widthwise, or both.

Choose either Slices Down or Slices Across, then enter a number to evenly divide each slice, or choose Pixels Per Slice and enter a pixel dimension to create slices of a specific size.

Select OK to apply the division.

You cannot divide layer-based slices. Divided slices are treated as user slices regardless of their original slice type. If a slice cannot be divided evenly by your specified pixel dimension, photoeditor creates an additional slice from the remainder.

## Duplicate slices

Select one or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Press Alt-drag (Windows) or Option-drag (macOS) from inside the selected slice to create a duplicate at the new position.

Duplicate slices maintain the same dimensions and optimization settings as the original. If you duplicate a linked user slice, the duplicate joins the same linked collection. All duplicates become user slices regardless of the original type.

## Copy and paste slices

Select one or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Select Edit>Copy.

If pasting into another image, open and display the image first.

Select Edit>Paste.

You cannot copy a slice if an active marquee selection or selected path exists in the document. Deselect these elements before copying slices. Copying a slice captures all layers within the slice bounds, not just the active layer. When pasted into a photoeditor document, the slice content creates a new layer.

## Combine slices

Select two or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Right-click (Windows) or press Ctrl-click (macOS) the selection and select Combine Slices.

photoeditor creates a rectangular slice from the outer edges of the combined slices. If the original slices are not adjacent or have different proportions, the combined slice may overlap other slices. The combined slice adopts the optimization settings from the first slice in your selection and always becomes a user slice. You cannot combine layer-based slices.

## Change stacking order

Select one or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Select a stacking order option in the options bar: Bring To Front, Bring Forward, Send Backward, or Send To Back.

The stacking order determines which slice appears on top when slices overlap. Newly created slices appear at the top of the stacking order by default. You cannot rearrange the stacking order of auto-slices.

## Align slices

Select the user slices you want to align.

Select the Slice Select tool and choose an alignment option in the options bar: Top, Vertical Centers, Bottom, Left, Horizontal Centers, or Right.

Aligning user slices can eliminate unnecessary auto slices and generate more efficient HTML code when you export your optimized image. To align layer-based slices, align the layers' contents instead.

## Distribute slices

Select the user slices you want to distribute.

Choose a distribution option in the options bar: Top, Vertical Centers, Bottom, Left, Horizontal Centers, or Right.

Distributing user slices evenly can eliminate unnecessary auto slices and generate more efficient HTML code when you export your optimized image. To distribute layer-based slices, distribute the layers' contents instead.

## Delete slices

Select one or more slices with the Slice Select tool.

Select Backspace or Delete.

When you delete user slices or layer-based slices, photoeditor regenerates auto slices to fill the document area. Deleting a layer-based slice does not delete its associated layer, but deleting the layer removes the layer-based slice. You cannot delete auto slices. After deleting all user- and layer-based slices, one auto-slice remains covering the entire image.

To delete all user slices and layer-based slices at once, select View>Clear Slices.

## Lock slices

Select View>Lock Slices to prevent accidental changes to all slices.

To unlock slices, select View>Lock Slices again to disable the lock.

Locking slices prevents resizing, moving, or modifying any slice properties. This protection helps preserve your slice layout while you work on other aspects of your image.

After organizing your slices, you can optimize and export them using File>Export>Export As or the legacy File>Export>Save for Web (Legacy) command. Each slice exports as a separate image file with its own optimization settings.


====================================================================================================

## 508. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/enhance-animation-frames.html

#### Learn how to reduce animation file size in professional photoeditor and improve playback quality by applying frame-based settings and export options in the Timeline panel.

Once your animation is ready, improve it for efficient download to a web browser:

*   Limit the frames to only include areas that change between frames to reduce the size of the animated GIF.
*   Apply the same animation settings as a standard GIF. Note that consistent dithering is used across the frames to prevent flicker, which may increase processing time compared to a standard GIF.
*   Use the Adaptive, Perceptual, or Selective palette to ensure the colors stay consistent across frames.  

Open the Timeline panel (Window>Timeline) in Frame Animation mode.

If the timeline shows keyframes instead of individual frames, select the menu icon and then select Convert Frames>Convert to Frame Animation.

Select the menu icon and then select Optimize Animation.

![Image 1: The Timeline panel menu is open with the Optimize Animation option highlighted.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/The-Timeline-panel-menu-is-open-with-the-Optimize-?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Set frame optimization options to reduce file size and improve playback consistency in animated GIFs. 

Review the options in the Optimize Animation dialog box and select OK.

*   Bounding Box: Crops each frame to only the changed areas. It reduces file size but may not be compatible with some GIF editors. 
*   Redundant Pixel Removal: Makes unchanged pixels transparent to reduce file size. To apply this, select the Transparency option in the Optimized panel (File>Export>Save for Web).

Right-click the frame in the Timeline panel and select Automatic as the disposal method when using the Redundant Pixel Removal option.


====================================================================================================

## 509. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/flatten-frames-into-layers.html

#### Learn how to convert video frames into individual layers in professional photoeditor to edit frames separately and apply custom effects.

*   Import your video layer and switch to frame animation mode or video timeline mode.
*   Save your file before proceeding, as this action changes the layer structure.
*   Be aware that audio data from video is discarded when flattening frames.

Convert videos to Smart Objects before flattening for non-destructive editing.

Open the Timeline panel and select your video layer.

Select the panel menu icon and choose Flatten Frames Into Layers.   
photoeditor creates a separate layer for each frame and arranges them in the Layers panel, with the first frame at the base of the stack.

## 510. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-video-files-or-image-sequences.html

#### Learn how to export your timeline as a video or image sequence in professional photoeditor. Select formats, define the frame range, and adjust the quality settings.

Before exporting, save your project, ensure you have sufficient disk space, and complete all necessary edits.

Open export dialog box:

*   Select File>Export>Render Video.

Enter a name and select the location to save:

*   Enter a name for your video or image sequence.
*   Choose where to save the file.
*   (Optional) Select Create New Subfolder and enter a folder name.

Select export method:

*   Choose either professional Media Encoder or photoeditor Image Sequence.

(Optional) Select format:

*   Choose from the Format dropdown. Options vary by export method.

(Optional) Configure export settings:

*   If you choose professional Media Encoder, set the following:  
Format: Choose video format  
Preset: Select predefined settings  
Size: Set dimensions  
Frame Rate: Set frames per second  
Field Order: Choose the interlacing option  
Aspect: Set aspect ratio  
Color: Manage: Enable/disable color management  
*   If you choose photoeditor Image Sequence, set the following:  
Format: Choose image format  
Starting: Set initial frame number  
Digits: Set number of digits in filename  
Size: Set dimensions  
Frame Rate: Set frames per second

Set frame Range:

Choose one of the following options:

*   All Frames: Exports the entire timeline.
*   Start Frame and End Frame: Exports a specific range
*   Work Area: Exports frames defined by the Timeline work area bar

(Optional) Configure Render Options:

*   Set Alpha Channel to None, Straight–Unmatted, or Premultiplied, depending on how you want to handle transparency.

Export video files.

*   Select Render to export your video or image sequence.

Always preview your export settings before rendering to ensure the desired output quality and file size.

## 511. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/photoeditor-file-formats-overview.html

#### Understand how file formats differ in professional photoeditor and which format to use for full features, large files, and for 8-, 16-, or 32-bit images.

Graphics file formats store images as pixels (raster) or vectors and differ in compression and in which photoeditor features they preserve. To keep layers, effects, and masks intact, [save a copy](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/save-your-work.html) as a photoeditor (PSD) file.

PSD supports files up to 2GB. For [larger files](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/save-files/save-large-documents.html), use Large Document Format (PSB). Depending on your workflow, you can also use photoeditor Raw (flattened only), TIFF (up to 4GB), or DICOM.

Most images use 8 bits per channel. For a more dynamic range, work in 16-bit or 32-bit and use the matching save option.

**Formats that support 16-bit (use**Save a Copy**):** photoeditor (PSD), Large Document Format (PSB), TIFF, PNG, photoeditor PDF, photoeditor Raw, DICOM, Cineon, IFF, JPEG 2000.

**Formats that support 32-bit (use**Save As**):** photoeditor (PSD), Large Document Format (PSB), OpenEXR (EXR), Radiance (HDR), TIFF, JPEG XL (JXL), Portable Float Map (PFM).


====================================================================================================

## 512. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/file-compression-in-photoeditor.html

Compare compression types in professional photoeditor and select the best format for image quality, file size, and intended use.

**Compression type****Description****Use case**
RLE (Run Length Encoding)Uses lossless compression to encode repeated data sequences efficiently.Compresses simple graphics in standard Windows file formats.
LZW (Lempel-Ziv-Welch)Uses lossless compression to retain all image data while reducing file size.Compresses images with large areas of solid color in TIFF, PDF, GIF, and PostScript.
JPEG (Joint Photographic Experts Group)It uses lossy compression to reduce file size by removing image detail. You can adjust the quality using the menu, slider, or by entering a value from 0 to 12.Compresses continuous-tone images like JPEG, TIFF, PDF, and PostScript photographs. Prints only on Level 2 or later PostScript printers and may not support plate separation.
CCITT (Consultative Committee for International Telegraphy and Telephony).Uses lossless compression designed for black-and-white images.Compresses scanned documents or line art in PDF and PostScript.
ZIP Uses lossless compression similar to LZW to reduce size without losing quality.Compresses images with flat color areas in PDF and TIFF.

**Format****Reason to use**
Image content Compress photos with JPEG. Use LZW or ZIP for graphics with solid colors.
Quality requirements Choose RLE, LZW, ZIP, or CCITT to preserve exact image data.
File size constraints Use JPEG for the smallest file sizes (with some quality loss).
Output medium Choose a format based on display or print requirements.

## WebP format support in photoeditor

WebP works well for web pages and supports both lossless and lossy compression. Use the quality slider to adjust compression and choose whether to keep metadata. WebP files are smaller than most traditional formats but still look sharp. Explore the compression settings to fine-tune image quality and file size.
