## 514. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/image-file-formats-supported-in-photoeditor.html

Learn about image file formats in professional photoeditor that you can open, save, and export, along with what each format supports and where it might fall short.

In photoeditor, you can save and share your work in many file formats. Knowing what each format does helps you choose the right one, whether for keeping layers for editing, enhancing for web, or preparing files for print.

## Native photoeditor formats

PSD is the default format and the only one (besides PSB) that keeps all features intact

*   Works seamlessly with other professional apps like Illustrator, InDesign, Premiere, After Effects, and GoLive.
*   Preserves layers, effects, and other editing capabilities across compatible apps.
*   Includes an option to save a composite version of the file, making it easier to open in other apps and older versions.

This composite version helps maintain the image's look and makes it easier and faster to open outside the app.

## Large Document Format (PSB)

PSB is the preferred format for working with extra-large files that exceed PSD limits.

*   Supports documents up to 300,000 pixels in width or height.
*   Retains all features like layers, effects, and filters.
*   Allows saving of 32-bit HDR images.
*   Ideal for large-scale, high-resolution projects.

Most other apps and older versions cannot open files over 2GB, so PSB works best with the latest version.

## photoeditor 2.0

This legacy format is only helpful if you're working with version 2.0 or apps that require it. It’s available only on macOS.

*   Opens files in photoeditor 2.0.
*   Exports images to apps that support only this older format.
*   Flattens the image and removes all layers.

Not recommended unless you are working with legacy systems.

## photoeditor Raw

photoeditor Raw is a flexible format for moving image data between apps and platforms.

*   Supports CMYK, RGB, and grayscale images with alpha channels.
*   Handles multichannel and Lab images without alpha channels.
*   Works with any pixel dimensions or file size.
*   Doesn’t support layers.

A photoeditor Raw file isn’t the same as a camera raw image—it’s not tied to a specific camera and doesn’t include unprocessed sensor data.

## Common image formats

## Joint Photographic Experts Group (JPEG)

JPEG format is commonly used for photographs and continuous-tone images in web documents.

*   Supports CMYK, RGB, and Grayscale color modes.
*   Does not support transparency.
*   Uses lossy compression that reduces file size by discarding data.
*   Higher compression lowers image quality.

## Portable Network Graphics (PNG)

PNG format is used for lossless compression and web display.

*   Supports 24-bit images
*   Produces clean background transparency without jagged edges
*   Preserves transparency in grayscale and RGB images

## Graphics Interchange Format (GIF)

GIF format is used to display indexed-color graphics and images in HTML documents.

*   Uses LZW compression to reduce file size and speed up transfer
*   Preserves transparency in indexed-color images
*   Does not support alpha channels

Tagged Image File Format (TIFF)

TIFF is used to exchange files between applications and platforms.

*   A flexible bitmap format supported by nearly all paint, image-editing, and layout apps.
*   Choose the BigTIFF option to save files larger than 4GB.

## BMP

BMP is a standard Windows image format supported on Windows-compatible systems.

*   Supports RGB, Indexed Color, Grayscale, and Bitmap color modes.
*   Let's you choose between Windows or OS/2 format and select bit depth when saving.

## WebP

WebP is a modern image format designed for the web that offers lossless and lossy compression.

*   In version 23.2 and later, you can open, create, edit, and save WebP files directly.
*   In version 23.1 and earlier, you must install the WebPShop plug-in.

## Professional and specialized formats

## Portable Document Format (PDF)

PDF is a flexible, cross-platform, cross-application format. Based on the PostScript imaging model, PDF files accurately preserve fonts, page layouts, and vector and bitmap graphics.

photoeditor recognizes two types of PDF files:

*   photoeditor PDF files: Created when Preserve photoeditor Editing Capabilities is selected in the Save professional PDF dialog box.
*   Standard PDF files: Created when Preserve photoeditor Editing Capabilities is deselected, or created by other applications.

## Encapsulated PostScript (EPS)

EPS is a language file format containing both vector and bitmap graphics. It’s supported by almost all graphics, illustration, and page-layout programs.

*   Used to transfer PostScript artwork between applications.
*   When opened, vector graphics are rasterized into pixels.

## Desktop Color Separations (DCS)

DCS is a version of EPS used to save color separations for CMYK images.

*   DCS 2.0 supports exporting images with spot channels.
*   Requires a PostScript printer for output.

## Digital Imaging and Communications in Medicine (DICOM)

DICOM is widely used to store and transfer medical images, such as ultrasounds and scans.

*   Stores both image data and metadata, including patient information.

## Cineon

Cineon, developed by Kodak, is a 10-bit-per-channel digital format for high-quality image processing.

*   Ideal for electronic composition, editing, and enhancement.
*   Allows output back to film without image quality loss.

## HEIF/HEIC

Modern mobile devices provide the capability to capture HEIF/.heic photos.

*   **macOS:** Supported on macOS High Sierra (v10.13) or later
*   **Windows:** Requires installing both HEIF and HEVC CODECs from Microsoft

Canon HIF/HEIC files are not supported. Use raw files from Canon cameras for best results.

## Digital Negative (DNG)

DNG is an archival format for camera raw files designed to provide compatibility and decrease the proliferation of camera raw file formats. The Camera Raw plug-in can save camera raw image data in the DNG format.

## High Dynamic Range (HDR)

photoeditor supports several formats for working with 32-bit HDR images:

*   OpenEXR (EXR): Used in visual effects workflows; supports transparency, works only with 32-bit/channel images. Developed by Industrial Light and Magic.
*   Radiance (HDR): This 32-bit-per-channel format was initially created for the Radiation system. It stores actual light data (luminosity), not just display colors, making it ideal for lighting simulations and 3D rendering.

## Other supported formats

## Interchange File Format (IFF)

IFF is a general-purpose data storage format that associates and stores multiple data types. IFF is portable and includes Maya IFF and IFF (formerly Amiga IFF).

## PICT File and PICT Resource

PICT format is used in macOS graphics and page-layout applications as an intermediary file format for transferring images between applications. PICT format supports RGB images with a single alpha channel and Indexed Color, Grayscale, and Bitmap mode images without alpha channels.

Though photoeditor can open raster PICT files, it cannot open QuickDraw PICTs or save them in PICT format.

## Pixar

Designed for high-end graphics applications like 3D rendering and animation. Pixar format supports RGB and grayscale images with a single alpha channel.

## Portable Bit Map (PBM)

PBM file format, also known as Portable Bitmap Library and Portable Binary Map, supports monochrome bitmaps. It can be used for lossless data transfer and is part of a larger family that includes Portable Graymap (PGM), Portable Pixmap (PPM), and Portable Anymap (PNM).

## Scitex Continuous Tone (CT)

CT format is used for high-end image processing on Scitex computers. Contact Creo for utilities to transfer files saved in Scitex CT format to a Scitex system.

## WBMP

WBMP format is the standard format for improving images for mobile devices. WBMP supports 1-bit color, meaning WBMP images contain only black and white pixels.

## File format compatibility

When selecting a file format, consider these factors:

*   **Layers and features**: Preserve layers, masks, or photoeditor-specific features using PSD or PSB formats.
*   **File size**: For web use, choose formats with efficient compression, such as JPEG, PNG, or WebP.
*   **Color requirements**: For print work requiring CMYK, choose formats that support this color mode, such as PSD, TIFF, or PDF.
*   **Compatibility**: If sharing with users of other applications, consider standard formats like TIFF, JPEG, or PDF.
*   **Quality needs**: For maximum quality, use lossless formats like PSD, TIFF, or PNG. For acceptable quality with smaller file sizes, consider lossy formats like JPEG.


====================================================================================================

## 515. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-your-work-using-the-quick-export-as-option.html

Learn how to save your work in professional photoeditor in your preferred image format quickly and output it without adjusting settings each time.

Use Quick Export as command to save your photoeditor work in your preferred format in one step. It applies your saved settings, so you export fast and consistently, and it's ideal when you need assets with the same specs repeatedly.

## Export instantly

Select the content:

*   To export the entire document, select File>Export>Quick Export as (image format). 
*   To export specific layers, select them in the Layers panel, right-click, and then select Quick Export as (image format).

Select the destination folder if prompted for a location (when preferences are set to Ask where to export each time).

Enter a filename and then select Save.

## Set preferences (one-time setup)

Select Edit>Preferences>Export (Windows) or photoeditor>Preferences>Export (macOS).

Select your Quick Export Format: PNG, JPG, or GIF.

Set format options: For PNG, Transparency or Smaller File (8-bit). For JPG, Quality.

Select the Quick Export Location. Ask where to export each time, or Export files to an assets folder next to the current document.

Select Quick Export Metadata to include Copyright and Contact Info.

Decide on Color Space, to convert assets to sRGB or not.

Set Export As Location to current document’s location or the last used location.

(Optional) Enable Use legacy "Export As" for legacy Export As interface.

Select OK to save your preferences.

![Image 1: Preferences dialog box is open with Export panel selected, showing options for Quick Export format, save location, metadata, color space, and the Use legacy Export As toggle.](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProd/Preferences-dialog-box-is-open-with-Export-panel-s?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1600)

Set output defaults to speed up asset delivery.


====================================================================================================

## 516. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/fine-tune-your-export-settings-using-the-export-as-option.html

#### Learn how to save layers, groups, or artboards as separate images in professional photoeditor and fine-tune their settings.

Select File>Export>Export As to access [Export As](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-settings-and-export-location-preferences.html) dialog box.

Select a format (PNG, JPG, GIF, or SVG) from the Format dropdown.

Adjust format-specific settings. For example, allow transparency for PNG or set quality levels for JPG.

Set a relative size (like 1.25x), add a suffix (such as @1.25x), and use the add  icon to include more sizes.

Select Export to save each chosen layer, group, or artboard as a separate image.

## 517. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-settings-and-export-location-preferences.html

Understand Export As options and preferences in professional photoeditor to adjust export settings and choose where assets are saved.

### File Settings

**UI option****Description**
Format PNG, JPG, GIF, or SVG options are available for exported files.
PNG

*   Transparency (32-bit)
*   Smaller File (8-bit)Transparency generates 32-bit assets. Smaller File generates 8-bit assets. With both deselected, 24-bit assets are generated.
JPG  
Quality slider (1%–100%)The quality slider (1%–100%) adjusts the balance between file size and image quality. Higher values preserve detail but increase file size.
GIF Images are transparent by default.
SVG Images are vector-based and scale without losing quality.

### Image Size

**UI option****Description**
Width   
Height Dimensions of the image asset. Width and height are locked together by default, changing one adjusts the other proportionately.
Scale Choose how big the exported image should be. This option is helpful for exporting assets with larger or smaller resolutions. Changing the scale impacts image size.
Resample Bilinear: Averages surrounding pixel colors to add pixels, producing medium-quality results.

Bicubic: Complex calculations on surrounding pixels for slower, more precise results with smoother tonal gradations.

Bicubic Smoother: Enlargement of images with Bicubic interpolation, designed to create smoother results.

Bicubic Sharper: Reduction of image size with Bicubic interpolation while enhancing sharpness. Bicubic can be used if over-sharpening occurs.

Bicubic Automatic: Automatic selection of the most suitable Bicubic method for the image.

Nearest Neighbor: Replication of pixels for fast but less precise results, best for illustrations with hard edges, but may create jagged effects when scaled.

Preserve Details: Preservation of sharpness and detail when resizing an image.

## Canvas Size

The dimensions for the exported asset are set. The Export As dialog centers the image within these bounds. Use this for icons that must fit within fixed boxes (such as 50×50 px) or banners that need exact dimensions.

*   If the image is larger than the canvas, it clips to the set width and height.
*   If the canvas is larger, extra space fills with:
    *   White (with a background layer)
    *   Transparency (if no background and format support it)
    *   The final swatch in the color table (for Indexed Color mode)

*   Reset restores the values set in Image Size.

**UI option****Description**
Width Horizontal canvas size for the exported asset. Wider images are clipped, smaller images leave extra space filled with white, transparency, or the final color swatch.
Height Vertical canvas size for the exported asset. Taller images are clipped, smaller images leave extra space filled with white, transparency, or the final color swatch.

### Additional options

**UI option****Description**
Metadata Inclusion of limited metadata (copyright and contact information) in exported assets. Physical resolution (DPI/PPI) is always included.
Color Space Conversion of the exported asset to the sRGB color space, with the option to embed the color profile.

## Scale All

**UI option****Description**
Size Relative scale (for example:1x, 0.5x, 2x) for exporting assets at different resolutions.
Suffix Text suffix (for example: @1.25x, @0.5x) added to the filename to identify scaled versions.
Add (+)New row for creating additional scaled export options.
Delete (trash) icon Removal of a size/suffix pair from the list.
Select All Selection or deselection of all assets for export at once.
Asset list Display of file name, format, dimensions, and file size for each exported item.

Tip

To change the Export As location preference, select Edit>Preferences>Export. In the Preferences dialog box, in the Export As Location section, select one of the following:

*   Export assets to the location of the current document
*   Export assets to the last location specified

## 518. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-files-in-different-sizes.html

## 519. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-layers-as-files.html

Learn how to save each layer as a separate file in professional photoeditor, select your preferred format, and name files exactly as you want.

Converting layers to individual files allows you to create assets for web design, create variations for a client, or organize pieces for animation, all without manually exporting each layer one by one.

Select File>Export>Layers to Files.

Select the destination folder.

Enter a name in the File Name Prefix text box to specify a common prefix for all the exported files.

Select Visible Layers Only to export only the layers for which visibility is enabled in the Layers panel.

Select a format from the File Type dropdown.

Set any format-specific options.

Select Include ICC Profile to embed the working space color profile. This is important for color-managed workflows.

Select Run.

The dialog box contains settings to choose a destination folder, enter a file name prefix, select only visible layers, pick a file format, adjust format-specific options, and include an ICC color profile. ## 520. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-artboards-as-pdf.html

Learn how to save artboards as PDF files in professional photoeditor, select single or multiple PDFs, and adjust quality and ICC profiles.

Select File>Export>Artboards To PDF.

In the Artboards To PDF dialog box:

*   Select destination location. 
*   Enter a prefix in the File Name Prefix field.
*   Select either Artboard Contents Only or Include Overlapping Areas to control whether content outside the artboard is included in the PDF.
*   Select Export Selected Artboards to export only specific artboards. 
*   Select Include Background In Export to add the artboard background to the generated PDF files.
*   Select Multi-Page Document to create one PDF with all artboards or Document Per Artboard to create separate PDFs using the prefix.
*   Select an encoding for the exported PDF: ZIP or JPEG. For JPEG, set the Quality value to a value between 0 to 12.
*   Select Include ICC Profile to embed the color profile in exported PDFs.
*   Select Include Artboard Name to add the artboard name to exported files. Customize the font, size, and canvas extension color.
*   Select Reverse Page Order to save the exported PDFs with artboards arranged from last to first.

Select Run to generate the PDF documents.  
After export, your PDFs appear in the selected folder. A single file includes all artboards, while individual files use your set prefix.


====================================================================================================

## 521. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-artboards-as-files.html

Learn how to export your canvas content in professional photoeditor as separate files for different screen sizes by saving selected or all artboards in your preferred format.

Select File>Export>Artboards To Files.

Select a destination folder in the Artboards To Files dialog box.

Enter a prefix for all exported files in the File Name Prefix field.

Select either Artboard Contents Only or Include Overlapping Areas.

Select Include Background In Export as required.

Select Export Selected Artboards to export only specific artboards.

Select a format from File Type and set the related options.

Select Export Options>Include Artboard Name to add the artboard name to exported files. Customize the font, size, color, and canvas extension color if needed.

Select Run to begin the export process.

## 522. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/export-files-to-different-formats/export-to-cloud.html

Save and export photoeditor assets to professional cloud so you can easily access, share, and reuse them across professional photoeditor on desktop, web, and mobile.

![Image 1: A photoeditor export to cloud icon overlayed on a scenic field with a tree in autumn colors and multiple colorful hot air balloons floating in the sky](https://helpx-prod.scene7.com/is/image/HelpxProdLoc/nfs-MAX-ps-d-export-to-cloud-docs-sept2025?$pjpeg$&jpegSize=300&wid=1920)

Export photoeditor assets to the cloud for easy access and collaboration.

Select File>Open to browse and open a file.

After making edits, select File>Save As to save your changes.

In the Save As dialog, select photoeditor as the format and then Save to cloud documents.

In the Save to professional cloud dialog, create a new project or use an existing one.

Select Save.

Your file will appear in the app homescreen as a recently saved or opened file, and as part of your professional cloud assets, accessible in photoeditor on the web and mobile.

## Export

Select File>Open to browse and open a file.

Select File>Export and then Export As.

In the Export As dialog, select a format (PNG, JPG, GIF) and adjust export settings, if required.

Select Export and then Save to cloud documents.

In the Export to professional cloud dialog, create a new project or use an existing one.

Select Export.

Your file will appear in the Projects tab and is accessible in photoeditor on the web and mobile.

## 523. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/metadata-content-credentials/use-content-credentials.html

Learn how to add attribution details and edit history to your images in professional photoeditor using Content Credentials.

Content Credentials allow you to attach attribution information and editing history to your images when exporting from photoeditor. This feature helps establish transparency with your audience and ensures proper recognition for your work as it travels across the internet.

## Turn on Content Credentials

Select Window>Content Credentials (Beta).

Select Enable Content Credentials.

Additionally, you can go to photoeditor>Settings>History & Content Credentials and select Enable for saved documents with Content Credentials to enable Content Credentials by default for all new documents.

Select Enable for new and saved documents with Content Credentials to ensure edits are captured from start to finish.

Content Credentials provide transparency about how your images were created by capturing attribution details, editing history, and connected accounts. Before finalizing your export, you can preview exactly what information will be included with your image.

Select Window>Content Credentials (Beta)>Enable Content Credentials.

Review the information categories. Use the checkboxes next to each information category to select the information you want to include.

Select Connect accounts to add or manage social media or crypto wallet connections.

Select Preview to see how your credentials will appear to viewers.

## 525. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/save-and-export/metadata-content-credentials/export-your-work-with-content-credentials.html

Learn how to add attribution details and edit history to your images by attaching Content Credentials when exporting from professional photoeditor.

Content Credentials allow you to attach attribution information and edit history to your exported images, providing transparency and helping you receive proper credit for your work.

Select File>Export>Export As.

Select JPG or PNG format, as these are the only formats that currently support Content Credentials.

Select one of the following options in the Content Credentials (Beta) section of the export dialog:

*   Publish to Content Credentials cloud: Keeps your file size smaller while making your credentials more resilient. This option stores your credentials in professional's public cloud storage, allowing recovery if they're stripped from your content.
*   Attach to file: Embeds the Content Credentials directly in your exported file. This increases file size but keeps your credentials more private. Note that attached credentials can be stripped when published online.
*   None: Doesn’t include Content Credentials with this export.

Configure your other export settings and select Export.

## 532. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/app-integrations/open-photoeditor-files-in-illustrator.html

Learn to open, place, link, or embed photoeditor files in Illustrator with control.

To preserve layers, text, and paths when opening photoeditor designs in Illustrator:

In Illustrator, select File**>**Open.

Locate the photoeditor file in the Open File dialog box and select Open.

In the photoeditor Import Options dialog box, choose the required settings:

*   Convert Layers to Objects**:** Preserves masks, blend modes, transparency, slices, and image maps, but not adjustment layers or effects.
*   Flatten Layers to a Single Image**:** Merges layers into one editable layer, preserving appearance but removing layer-level editing.

Select OK.

## Place photoeditor files in Illustrator

Placing files, such as layer comps, text, and paths, helps preserve photoeditor data. Illustrator imports adjustment layers with visibility turned off, which are restored if exported back to photoeditor.

### Place a linked photoeditor image into an Illustrator file

Linking keeps the photoeditor file connected. Changes in the source file are updated in Illustrator.

In Illustrator, select File**>**Place.

Locate the photoeditor file, select Linked checkbox, and then select Place.

A red X over the image indicates it’s linked and not directly editable.

### Embed a photoeditor image into an existing Illustrator file

Embedding copies the artwork into Illustrator, increasing file size and preventing updates when the original file changes.

In Illustrator, select File**>**Place.

Locate the photoeditor file, ensure Linked checkbox is not selected, and select Show Import Options.

Select Place, then choose the desired import options in the photoeditor Import options dialog box and select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 533. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/troubleshoot-tools-resources/set-up-and-manage-scratch-disks.html

Configure scratch disks, free up space, and optimize storage settings in professional photoeditor to resolve startup failures and performance issues.

When photoeditor crashes during startup or while working on projects, the issue is often related to insufficient scratch disk space. Scratch disks are hard drives or SSDs that photoeditor uses for temporary storage when your computer's RAM is insufficient for the current operations.

Select Edit>Preferences>Scratch Disks (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Scratch Disks (macOS)

Select additional drives to use as scratch disks in the Scratch Disk Preferences dialog.

Use the arrow buttons to arrange drives in order of priority (fastest drives should be first).

Select OK.


====================================================================================================

## 534. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/troubleshoot-tools-resources/check-gpu-compatibility.html

Learn how to check your GPU compatibility manually and using the built-in tool in professional photoeditor.

## Check GPU compatibility manually

Select Edit>Preferences>Performance (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS).

Uncheck Use Graphics Processor.

Select OK and restart photoeditor.

Test if your issue is resolved without GPU acceleration.

If the problem disappears, your GPU or its driver is likely the cause.

Select Help>GPU Compatibility.

Check if the Microsoft OpenCL/OpenGL Compatibility Pack is installed, if you view the message Your graphics processor is incompatible on a Windows Intel-based system.

If the compatibility pack is installed, uninstall it and restart your computer.

Launch photoeditor and check if the issue is resolved.

## 535. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/performance-stability-issues/known-and-fixed-issues.html

Learn about the known issues, their workarounds, and the resolution status in professional photoeditor on desktop.

We update this information regularly as fixes are released and new issues are identified. Check back frequently for the latest status updates.

Freeze or lag when moving artboards in documents with many (6+) artboards

Open

App version professional photoeditor 27.7
Impacted device types macOS, Windows
Last updated September 23, 2026

Summary

**Summary**

When working with Artboard documents that have six or more Artboards, you can experience a temporary freeze when moving the Artboards.

**Workaround**

Specify canvas boundaries and disable Auto-size Canvas.

1.   Use two shape layers, i.e., rectangles to specify the outer canvas boundaries in the work area outside where your artboards are placed.
2.   Disable Auto-size Canvas in the Artboard tool options gear menu.

Before exporting, remove the canvas boundary shapes, then save your document.

Performance may be slower than cloud on older hardware

Resolved

App version professional photoeditor 26.7
Impacted device types Windows, Mac
Last updated

Summary

**Summary**

On-device processing is 2.2.Sx slower than cloud on Apple MI Max (e.g., 1:22 vs 0:37), especially on first use. Subsequent operations are faster. Older hardware or insufficient VRAM may result in degraded performance.

**Workaround**

Ensure GPU drivers are up to date. For systems that do not meet hardware requirements, photoeditor will disable the on-device option and display a tooltip explaining the limitation. Users can continue to use cloud-based Remove tool. Upgrading to newer hardware (Apple M2+ or NVIDIA RTX series) provides the best results.

Turn off pref by default: "Open JPEG without Background layer"

Resolved

App version professional Desktop 26.7
Impacted device types Windows, Mac
Last updated

Summary

**Summary**

Turn off pref by default: "Open JPEG without Background layer"

Premium GenAI features capped by credit allocation

Resolved

App version professional Desktop 26.7
Impacted device types Windows, Mac
Last updated

Summary

**Summary**

Generative Al features are gated by the user's monthly generative credits allocation (100 credits for eligible subscribers). Once exhausted, premium features become unavailable until the next billing cycle.

**Workaround**

Monitor credit usage in the Creative Cloud app. Additional credits may be available with higher-tier plans. The **Learn More** link in the quota exhausted notification has been updated with correct information.

Some users on specific Wi-Fi networks may not be able to open photoeditor in-app deeplinks.

Under Review

App version Affects all app versions
Impacted device types Desktop experiences (in-app links that depend on browsers)
Last updated

Summary

On some Wi-Fi networks, photoeditor in-app tutorial deeplinks open an “Install photoeditor” page instead of launching photoeditor. This issue has been reported primarily in Germany and does not occur when the same device uses a mobile hotspot or a different network.

**Next steps**Try connecting to a different network, such as a mobile hotspot. If the issue persists, check back for updates.

Using larger fonts for the UI clips Remove Tool options.

Partially resolved

App version professional photoeditor 27.0
Impacted device types Mac, Windows
Last updated September 23, 2025

Summary

Options for the **Remove Tool** (flag and commit icons) are clipped and not visible.

**Next steps**Enable the narrow options bar(**Preferences**>**Workspace**>**Enable Narrow Options Bar**), and the width of the options bar will be further reduced.

Remove Tool options bar appears truncated.

Partially resolved

App version professional photoeditor 27.0
Impacted device types Mac, Windows
Last updated September 23, 2025

Summary

The **Remove Tool** options bar is appearing truncated (option for **Report this result**, **Reset all strokes**, **Apply current strokes** is deactivated).

**Next steps**Enable the narrow options bar(**Preferences**>**Workspace**>**Enable Narrow Options Bar**), and the width of the options bar will be further reduced.

Generating additional variations fails if a pixel layer is added beneath a harmonized layer.

Under Review

App version professional photoeditor 26.11
Impacted device types Windows, Mac
Last updated October 28, 2025

Summary

The output is severely degraded, producing unusable variations.The Generate function becomes unavailable when one or more layers are positioned between the Harmonize layer and the original subject layer.

Next steps

To proceed, either move the interfering layers or apply Harmonize to a new layer.

## 536. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/performance-stability-issues/resolve-graphics-processor-gpu-and-graphics-driver-issues.html

Resolve GPU-related problems in professional photoeditor with driver updates, compatibility checks, and performance optimizations.

When photoeditor has issues with your graphics processor (GPU) or graphics driver, you might experience various symptoms that affect your workflow and productivity.

You may notice one or more of these symptoms when experiencing GPU-related issues in photoeditor:

*   photoeditor displays a GPU compatibility screen shortly after launch
*   Performance slowdowns or lag when using tools or filters
*   Visual artifacts, flickering, or flashing canvas
*   photoeditor crashes or freezes when launching or using certain features
*   Missing menu bar or interface elements
*   Slow screen redraw when zooming in or out
*   Partial screen redraws with remnants from other windows
*   Graphic elements like crop handles or transform controls appear discolored
*   Images filled with bright green color

## Outdated, corrupted, or incompatible graphics driver

Download and install the latest graphics driver directly from your GPU manufacturer's website.

Restart your computer after installation.

Launch photoeditor and test if the issue is resolved.

If problems persist, try installing a slightly older but stable driver version.

## Rendering issues or performance problems due to automatic graphics switch between integrated and discrete GPUs on macOS

Go to System Settings>Battery (macOS Ventura or later).

Uncheck Automatic graphics switching.

Restart your computer.

Launch photoeditor and test if the issue is resolved.

## GPU settings in photoeditor are causing conflicts with certain features

Select Edit>Preferences>Performance (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS).

Make sure Use Graphics Processor is checked.

Select Advanced Settings.

Uncheck Use OpenCL and GPU Compositing, and select OK.

Select OK and restart photoeditor.

Some photoeditor features require GPU acceleration and won't work if you disable the graphics processor. These include Oil Paint filter, Perspective Warp, Smooth Brush Resizing, and certain 3D functionality.

## Corrupt photoeditor preference files

Close photoeditor.

Hold Alt+Ctrl+Shift (Windows) or Option+Command+Shift (macOS) while launching photoeditor.

Select Yes when prompted to delete the professional photoeditor Settings file.

Test if your issue is resolved with fresh preference files.


====================================================================================================

## 537. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/performance-stability-issues/troubleshoot-scratch-disk-full-errors-in-photoeditor.html

Resolve scratch disk errors by freeing up space, configuring additional drives, and optimizing professional photoeditor settings.

[Video 3](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_3NE44Rkau0)

_Explore the basics of how to fix a scratch disk full error in photoeditor._

When working in photoeditor, you might encounter the "Scratch disk full" or "Scratch Disk Low" error message that could prevent you from continuing your work or even launching the application. This error occurs when photoeditor runs out of temporary storage space on your hard drive.

When the error occurs, you'll see a dialog box with the message Could not initialize photoeditor because the scratch disks are full or Could not complete your request because the scratch disks are full or a popup at startup that says Scratch Disk Low. photoeditor may freeze, crash, or refuse to launch entirely. You might also notice significantly slower performance before the error appears.

## Your scratch disk drive doesn't have enough free space

Free up at least 100 GB of space on your primary scratch disk drive.

**Windows:** Delete the files beginning with photoeditor Temp in C:\ drive.

**macOS:** Clean up your Macintosh HD drive or move files to an external drive.

Restart photoeditor to see if the error is resolved.

## Set up additional scratch disks

You can [set up additional scratch disks](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/troubleshoot-tools-resources/set-up-and-manage-scratch-disks.html) for the temporary files. Supported drive formats for scratch disks are APFS and macOS Extended (Journaled) for macOS, and NTFS, exFAT, and FAT32 for Windows.

## Optimize photoeditor settings

The History States and auto-recovery features can consume significant scratch disk space during complex operations. These changes will reduce the amount of temporary data photoeditor needs to store, helping prevent scratch disk full errors.

Select Edit>Preferences>Performance (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>Performance (macOS).

Reduce the number of History States (default is 50) in the History & Cache section.

Select Edit>Preferences>File Handling (Windows) or photoeditor>Settings>File Handling (macOS).

Select Automatically Save Recovery Information if you're working with very large files.

Select OK and restart photoeditor.

## Reset preferences

The photoeditor preference file may be corrupted, affecting how it handles scratch disks. Resetting preferences returns all settings to their defaults, but should fix any corruption issues affecting scratch disk functionality.

Close photoeditor completely.

Hold Shift + Alt + Ctrl (Windows) or Shift + Option + Command (macOS) while launching photoeditor.

Select Yes when prompted to delete the professional photoeditor Settings file.

Reconfigure your scratch disk preferences after photoeditor launches.

Test if the scratch disk error has been resolved.


====================================================================================================

## 538. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/resolve-program-error-while-saving-files.html

Fix file saving program errors in professional photoeditor by adjusting system permissions or modifying document layers.

When you attempt to save a file in photoeditor, a dialog appears with the message, Could not save as [filename].psd because of a program error, and prevents the file from being saved. This typically happens when saving large or complex files with multiple layers, when working with certain file formats, or after making significant edits to your document.

## photoeditor may have bugs or compatibility issues

Open the Creative Cloud desktop application.

Navigate to the Apps tab.

Find photoeditor in your installed apps list.

Select Update.

Restart photoeditor and try saving your file again.

## On macOS, photoeditor may lack the necessary disk access permissions to save files to certain locations

Select the Apple menu in the top-left corner.

Select System Settings.

Select Privacy & Security>Full Disk Access.

Select the lock icon and enter your system password to make changes.

Select the box next to professional photoeditor to grant full disk access.

Restart photoeditor and try saving your file again.

## Complex layer structures or layer effects

Hide all the layers, and save the file with a new name.

Gradually make layers visible again in small groups.

Save after each group to identify which layer might be causing the issue

For the problematic layer, try simplifying it by rasterizing or removing effects.

Restart photoeditor and try saving your file again.

## Unified Extensibility Platform (UXP) panel issues

Quit photoeditor completely.

Go to /Applications/professional photoeditor [Version]/professional photoeditor [Version].app/Contents/Required/UXP/ (macOS) and Program Files\professional\professional photoeditor [Version]\Required\UXP (Windows).

Rename the folder com.professional.unifiedpanel to ~com.professional.unifiedpanel.

Restart photoeditor and try saving your file again.

## Save engine incompatible with your specific file or system configuration

Go to photoeditor>Settings>File Handling on macOS.

Select Enable Legacy "Save As" to use the older save method.

Try saving in a different format like TIFF or PSB temporarily. You can later try reopening and saving in your preferred format.


====================================================================================================

## 539. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/cloud-documents-file-access-error.html

Learn how to fix cloud documents that won’t open due to file access errors in professional photoeditor on desktop.

If a cloud document in photoeditor fails to open and displays the file access error: Could not open "filename". The account you signed in with doesn't have access. Check with account owner or sign into another account, it’s usually caused by the Creative Cloud sync service or your sign-in session. The fix depends on the photoeditor version.

To check the photoeditor version, select Help>About photoeditor (Windows) or photoeditor>About photoeditor (macOS), then follow the steps for that version.

## Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer isn't running (photoeditor version 27.9.1 and later)[](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/cloud-documents-file-access-error.html)

Check whether the Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer process is running, then restart it if needed.

### macOS

In Finder, select Applications>Utilities>Activity Monitor.

Type “Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer” in the search bar.

#### Turn on App Background Activity (macOS)[](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/cloud-documents-file-access-error.html)[](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/app-background-activity)

In the Apple menu, select System Settings.

Select General>Login Items & Extensions.

In the background items section, Allow in the Background, select professional Creative Cloud. Toggle to turn it on.

CCLibrary appears as a separate item.

From the Apple menu, select Creative Cloud>Quit Creative Cloud.

Reopen the Creative Cloud desktop app so it restarts the synchronizer automatically.

Open Activity Monitor and confirm the synchronizer is running.

### Windows

Press Ctrl + Shift + Esc to open Task Manager.

Select the Processes tab (Windows 11) or the Details tab (earlier versions).

Check whether Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer is running.

#### Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer is not responding (macOS)[](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/cloud-documents-file-access-error.html)

In Activity Monitor, select Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer, then Force Quit from the Apple menu.

From the Activity Monitor, select photoeditor, then Force Quit.

Wait 10 seconds, then relaunch photoeditor to restart the synchronizer.

Open Activity Monitor and confirm the synchronizer is running. If it doesn’t appear, restart your computer and check again.

When the synchronizer is running, try to open the cloud document.

#### [](https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/file-format-issues/cloud-documents-file-access-error.html)Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer is not responding (Windows)

In Task Manager, right-click Creative Cloud Libraries Synchronizer.exe, then select End task.

Right-click photoeditor.exe, then select End task.

Wait 10 seconds, then relaunch photoeditor to restart the synchronizer.

Open Task Manager and confirm the synchronizer is running. If it doesn’t appear, restart your computer and check again.

When the synchronizer is running, try to open the cloud document.

## Creative Cloud sync needs an update or isn’t running correctly (photoeditor version 27.8 and earlier)

Quit photoeditor, then check and install pending Creative Cloud updates. If the issue continues and you have administrator access to your machine, try the advanced steps:

Sign out of Creative Cloud in the app preferences.

Quit the Creative Cloud desktop app.

In Activity Monitor (macOS) or Task Manager (Windows), confirm that professional Content Synchronizer and professional Desktop Service are closed.

Go to the professional Sync folder.

*   **On Windows:** C:\Program Files (x86)\professional\professional Sync. 
*   **On macOS:** /Applications/Utilities/professional Sync.

Rename the professional Content Synchronizer folder to professional Content Synchronizer-OLD.

Restart your machine, then update the Creative Cloud desktop app.

### photoeditor isn’t available in mainland China

professional Creative Cloud services, including cloud documents, are available only in countries where Creative Cloud is offered. Because Creative Cloud services aren't available in mainland China, cloud documents cannot be used there.See the [list of countries where Creative Cloud is available](https://helpx.professional.com/creative-cloud/apps/get-started/faq.html). If you're in China, you can[cancel your Creative Cloud membership or request a refund](https://helpx.professional.com/in/manage-account/using/cancel-creative-cloud-subscription-china.html).


====================================================================================================

## 540. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/tool-feature-issues/resolve-crash-issues-while-using-bullets-and-numbers.html

Resolve the professional photoeditor crash issue when working with bullets and numbers.

Update to photoeditor version 26.6 or later.

Open the document that has the issue.

Select No on the Some text layers might need to be updated... dialog.

Save a screenshot of your canvas so you have a backup of the text needing to be recreated.

Delete all text layers that have a gray triangle with an exclamation mark in the Layers panel. Select OK on any program errors encountered along the way.

Recreate the Type layers without utilizing the Bullets and Numbering functionality. You can type the list text as long as the auto detection preference is disabled.

Save as a new PSD document.

For photoeditor versions 26.4 and 26.5, uncheck Automatic detection of Bulleted and Numbered lists while typing in Preferences>Type.


====================================================================================================

## 541. https://helpx.professional.com/photoeditor/desktop/troubleshoot/tool-feature-issues/keyboard-shortcuts-not-working.html

Restore keyboard shortcut functionality by identifying and resolving common conflicts in professional photoeditor.

When keyboard shortcuts stop working in photoeditor, your workflow can significantly slow down. This issue typically stems from corrupted preferences, conflicting applications, or third-party utilities interfering with the photoeditor keyboard input handling.

## Corrupt photoeditor preferences

Close photoeditor completely.

Hold down Alt+Ctrl+Shift (Windows) or Option+Command+Shift (macOS) while launching photoeditor.

When prompted to Delete the professional photoeditor Settings File, select Yes.

Test keyboard shortcuts to verify they're working again.

## Running multiple applications simultaneously

Save your work in photoeditor.

Close all other applications one by one.

Test keyboard shortcuts after closing each application to identify the conflict.

Once you identify the conflicting application, check if it has an update available.

If updating doesn't help, adjust that application's keyboard shortcut settings or consider using it only when photoeditor is closed.

Common applications known to conflict with photoeditor keyboard shortcuts include:

*   **1Password:**Update 1Password. Older versions have affected keyboard shortcuts in photoeditor.
*   **Chrome:**Update Chrome. Older versions have affected keyboard shortcuts in photoeditor.
*   **Notability:**In photoeditor, you receive the message "Could not place because the Postscript data is empty or invalid." To resolve this issue, uninstall the Notability app.If you don't have the app installed on your system, but had it installed once, ensure that all parts of the app have been successfully removed.

## Third-party utilities, extensions, and drivers

Restart your computer in Safe Mode.

*   Windows: Restart while holding Shift, then select Safe Mode.
*   macOS: Restart while holding Shift until the Apple logo appears.

Launch photoeditor and test if keyboard shortcuts work in Safe Mode.

Common problematic utilities include:

*   **Default Folder X utility:**Disable or uninstall this utility. You can disable Default Folder X by selecting the Disable button in the Default Folder X system preferences. Or contact[St. Clair Software](http://stclairsoft.com/DefaultFolderX/)for any available updates.
*   **MagicMenu utility:**You can disable MagicMenu by selecting the Disable button in the MagicMenu system preferences. Or contact[MagicPrefs](http://magicprefs.com/)for any available updates.
*   **Comcast/Xfinity utilities:**Contact Comcast/Xfinity for any available updates or instructions on how to disable or uninstall the utilities.
*   **BetterTouchTool:**Disable this utility or contact[BetterTouchTool](https://www.boastr.net/)for troubleshooting or any available updates.
*   **Webroot Antivirus:**Contact[Webroot](http://www.webroot.com/)for troubleshooting or any available updates.

## Outdated or conflicting tablet drivers

Visit the tablet manufacturer's website and download the latest driver.

Uninstall the current tablet driver completely.

Restart your computer.

Install the new driver.
